# Mojito Feature Matrix

This is the authoritative summary of language support. Update it in the same
change that alters a feature's status. [`grammar.md`](grammar.md) remains authoritative for
surface syntax; the tests remain authoritative for detailed behavior.
The machine-checkable comparison with pinned Mojo—including explicit divergences
and exclusions—lives in [`conformance/parity.tsv`](../conformance/parity.tsv).

Status meanings:

- **Run** — accepted by the production compiler and executed by the register VM.
- **Check** — represented and checked, but has no independent runtime behavior.
- **Parse** — preserved in the AST but deliberately rejected before execution.
- **No** — not reliably accepted even as syntax.

## Where Mojito Stands

Mojito has one production path: source is linked and elaborated, checked into
typed HIR, lowered to verified MIR, ownership- and liveness-analyzed, drop
elaborated, and executed by the register VM. The supported CPU surface includes
exact numeric literals, type/value/origin generics and heterogeneous packs,
scope-stable and lexical nested-pack specialization, refined and conditional
traits, recursively lifted typed closure environments, linear whole-pack
forwarding, generic-anonymous callable contracts, symbolic callable defaults,
residual callable specialization, origin-bearing references and current-model
unsafe pointers (the `unsafe_*` vocabulary, empty-`[]` dereference,
layout-based linear `std.memory` allocation with a tracked
`Allocation.unsafe_ptr()`, multi-element interior-domain origins carrying
the borrowed `Span`/`StringSpan` views — with current Mojo's strict slice
bounds, grapheme-cluster and Span element iteration, and the `@implicit`
List-to-Span conversion whose temporary refines its origin to the source —
and the experimental conservative `Origin._subtree` form, including
`Pointer(to=…)` through `ref` bindings),
collection-owned interior-origin generations, explicit lifecycle semantics, and
a self-hosted proof-subset standard library. Method-dispatched nominal
subscripts retain ordinary checked method selection in one complete verified
call contract, including effects, caller places, capture access, generic values,
and reference results; call-less index and slice operations name their exact
compiler-owned intrinsic family instead of asking the VM to infer one from a
runtime value. The narrow nominally typed `Slice.indices()` result still crosses
private Tuple storage through that explicit intrinsic bridge. Public
`List`, `Set`, `Dict`, heterogeneous `Tuple`, and current Mojo's private
Int/Scalar range family are nominal library structs; only compile-time lists
and the private heterogeneous runtime-pack carrier retain compiler-owned
aggregate representations.

## Support Matrix

| Area | Feature | Status | Boundary / notes |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Frontend | Indentation, continuations, comments, numeric/string literals, Mojo escapes | Run | Includes current radix/decimal/exponent separators, `Byte == UInt8`, raw and triple forms, and same-line or indented adjacent strings. Diagnostic parsing can report multiple statement-level errors. |
| Types | Exact numeric literals and scalar materialization | Run | Source `IntLiteral` values use arbitrary-precision integers; finite `FloatLiteral` values use exact rationals with negative-zero preservation. Literal-only arithmetic remains exact through compile-time evaluation. Checked HIR records the contextual boundary, MIR emits `MaterializeLiteral`, integer targets wrap to their width, and floating targets round once directly to binary32/binary64. A literal argument to a generic constructor materializes to the solved parameter type on every constructor path (explicit or inferred application, fieldwise, overloaded), and a literal against an explicitly bound parameter widens to the binding (`Pair[Float64](0.5, 1)`). |
| Types | Target-layout size queries | Run | `size_of[T]()` returns an `Int` byte count from the shared native `LayoutCx`, including aggregate padding. Parametric MIR keeps the query over the checked type (`MirInstr::SizeOf`, over `T` in a template-served `def`); the elaborator answers it under the compilation's native target — the host for a VM run, `--target` for a native compile — once the instance's types are concrete, so concrete MIR holds the constant and both backends agree by construction. Layout is asked only of a concrete type: a symbolic one is `LayoutError::Symbolic`. A module constant built by a layout query (`comptime S = size_of[Pair]()`) and a direct `size_of[T]()` in a SIMD width retain the query through checking; type substitution replaces its operand, and the elaborator resolves its size. Generic vector parameters, returns, and constructions at that width run (`assets/ok/comptime_layout_constant.mojo`). |
| Frontend | T-strings | Run | Prefix order/case and raw/triple combinations are accepted; nested lexical structure is respected. A `t"…"` is a lazy value: interpolations require `Writable`, and the checker writes each occurrence in one pass, as the pin does, as a call of the bundled entry point `__make_tstring` (`std/format/tstring.mojo`, named by module path, not looked up in user scope) whose interleaved pack captures literal segments and interpolation snapshots at creation into the self-hosted `TString[*Ts]` struct, deferring formatting to Writable `write_to` (so `print`/`String(...)` consume it ordinarily; nesting works). The literal's type is that call's, `TString[String, …]`. The checked arena and HIR hand MIR the call in the literal's place (`SemanticAdjustment::SpelledConstruction`, shared with initializer lists), so a t-string in a template-served generic body or a struct method runs on both backends (`assets/ok/tstring_template_served.mojo`). Capture is by typed value snapshot — a place whose type is not `ImplicitlyCopyable` (a `List[Int]` included) snapshots as a creation-time formatted string — where real Mojo captures immutable borrows (see `docs/mojo-nightly.md`). `TString[...]` type annotations are not spellable. A check without the bundled module (a raw stage-composed parse) rejects a t-string explicitly. A t-string inside a function evaluated at compile time stops with "TString.write_to: unspecialized type-keyed method", as a `Tuple` written there does (R486). |
| Frontend | Walrus `:=` | Run | Evaluates once, assigns to a name already in scope, and produces the value. A walrus never introduces a binding: an unknown target is `AssignToUndeclared`, as the pinned Mojo reports "use of unknown declaration". |
| Modules | File-scope declarations and runtime entry | Run | Runtime statements at file scope are rejected; execution begins in zero-argument `main()`. |
| Bindings | Typed/inferred `var` (required), late initialization, assignment | Run | `var` is the sole declaration form: a bare `x = e` (or `a, b = e`) targeting a name not in scope is rejected (`AssignToUndeclared`). `var` is **block-scoped** — a declaration inside a nested block is not visible after it. Reads require initialization on every reaching path (path joins retain a maybe-uninitialized state unless every reachable path assigns it); same-scope redeclaration and type changes are rejected. A local declared without an initializer (`var a: S`) stores nothing until its first assignment, so it runs on both backends, including a struct assigned in both arms of an `if`, one declared before a raise in a `try` body, and one declared before a `try` and first assigned inside it, which survives the body's exit (`assets/ok/late_initialized_local.mojo`). |
| Bindings | Field/index places, augmented assignment, tuple unpacking | Run | Place expressions evaluate indexes once. Augmented assignment on a user-defined value dispatches to its dedicated in-place dunder (`x += y` → `x.__iadd__(y)`, plus `__isub__`/`__imul__`/`__itruediv__`/`__ifloordiv__`/`__imod__`/`__ipow__` and bitwise `__iand__`/`__ior__`/`__ixor__`) as a checked `mut self` method call for variables, projected fields, and nominal-subscript elements, mutating the receiver in place; a value of a bare type parameter whose bound requires the in-place dunder (`T: Accum` declaring `__iadd__`) dispatches through the bound the same way; Mojo does not fall back to the ordinary binary dunder. A missing in-place dunder, an immutable receiver, and a mismatched right-hand side are rejected, and a raising in-place dunder participates in ordinary `try` handling. A collection element converts to a borrowing view operand in place (`out += parts[i]` over a `String` collector, `t += xs[0]` over a `List[String]`, `width(xs[1])` at a `StringSpan` parameter): the view binds the element's place and keeps the container alive until the call returns, and an operand overlapping the subscript target (`xs[1] += xs[0]`) is rejected as aliasing, as the pin does (`assets/ok/string_element_view_conversion.mojo`, `assets/type_error/subscript_inplace_operand_aliasing.mojo`). A reference result converts the same way (`width(p.name_ref())`, `out += pick(t)`), its view binding the referent the accessor returns, and a binding annotated with the view type (`var v: StringSpan[origin_of(p.name)] = p.name_ref()`, `= xs[1]`) keeps that source's owner alive as long as the binding lives (`assets/ok/reference_result_view_conversion.mojo`). Native Int/UInt targets keep the builtin read-modify-write, including `&=`/`|=`/`^=`. Unpacking a tuple place (`var s, n = t`, `a, b = t`) copies each element into its target, so a `String` element is owned by the target and by the tuple independently; an element that is not `ImplicitlyCopyable` is rejected at a named target, and `_` reads nothing (`assets/ok/tuple_unpack_place_copies.mojo`, `assets/type_error/tuple_unpack_place_element_not_implicitly_copyable.mojo`). |
| Bindings | Local `ref name = place` | Run | Aliases variable/field/index storage; indexed places bind once; persistent CFG loans enforce exclusivity through last use. |
| Control flow | Loop `else`, `for ref`, context managers | Run | `break` bypasses loop `else`; reference iteration (`for ref`) runs the ordinary origin-parameterized `Iterable` protocol — the bundled List/Set iterators borrow their source and yield element references resolved to the source's mutability at the loop site, writing through element handles (including over temporaries); a `ref` target over a generic `Iterable` bound is rejected because the abstract contract yields values; `with` is the checked desugar of the Contexts row. |
| Calls | Defaults, keywords, `/`, `*`, homogeneous `*args` | Run | One structural contract serves free, method, static-method, and hand-written constructor calls in the checker and VM. A homogeneous `*args` over a loan-carrying element type (`count(Span(xs), Span(xs))` against `def count[T: Copyable](*args: T)`) binds the element's origins from the collected arguments as a regular parameter binds its own, so it runs over a read parameter or a local and a `-> T` result carries the bound origin; arguments over different origins are rejected, as upstream (`assets/ok/homogeneous_variadic_loan_carrying_element.mojo`). A collector indexed by a runtime value (`sum += xs[i]`, `xs[i].byte_length()`) reads that element on both backends, natively through the pack's element pointers behind a bounds trap (`assets/ok/collector_runtime_index.mojo`). A function with a homogeneous collector has one instance for every call arity, taking the pack at run time as the pin's `VariadicList` (`fused(1, 2)` and `fused(1, 2, 3)` run one `fused`); natively the pack is upstream's `{ elements, len }` descriptor over the caller's element slots (`docs/native-abi.md`). A `for` loop or comprehension over a named homogeneous collector, read or `var`, borrows each element in place, as upstream's `VariadicListMem` iterator yields `ref` elements: `for x in xs` copies and moves nothing, `for ref x in xs` mutates a `var` collector's elements, and `for var x in xs` copies an `ImplicitlyCopyable` element (`assets/ok/variadic_collector_forwarded_and_borrowed.mojo`). Indexed mutation of a `var` collector's element (`xs[0].v += 7`) is still rejected (`docs/roadmap.md` R742). A default that is neither a literal nor an `@implicit` conversion of one (`s: String = String("a")`, `x: Int = TWO + 1`, `xs: List[Int] = List[Int]()`) lowers as a zero-parameter default function, and every call leaving the slot out runs it at the call, as upstream's call binding writes the callee's default into the call: the lowering of each checked call records its callee, and once every declaration is lowered `mir::fill_omitted_arguments` writes each omitted argument into the call, whatever its shape, under the call's own parameter arguments, for a method the struct's from its receiver's type, and for a nested `def` the enclosing function's own, before ownership analysis. A literal default is filled the same way, materialized at the parameter type as a written literal is, and an `@implicit` conversion of one runs its constructor at the call, so a positional-only slot after an omitted default is passed by position (`plit[n, m](x: Int = 1, y: Int = m * 2, /)` called as `plit[1, 2]()`). A borrowed slot's value is the caller's temporary, destroyed when the call returns (`use()` prints `drop dflt`, a nested `plain()` `drop nested`, `a[1]` over `__getitem__(self, i: Int, r: Owner = Owner(String("sub")))` `drop sub`, as on the pin), and a `var` slot's is handed to the callee. A direct call, a method call, a static-method call, a construction, a subscript, a nested `def`'s call by name or through a sibling's capture, a bundled function's call from the program or from another bundled unit (`f.seek(6)`), a call through a trait bound to a trait default's body (`u.f()` in `use[U: T]`), and a function value's call (`var g = use; g = use; g()`) all fill alike (`assets/ok/evaluated_default_argument.mojo`, `assets/ok/trait_default_parameter_defaults.mojo`). The callee never evaluates a default, and its instances are keyed without the binders only a default reads (`Box[n].get[m](self, x: Int = Self.n + m)` has one instance however `m` varies: `assets/ok/lifted_body_owner_instances.mojo`, `assets/ok/nested_unread_enclosing_instances.mojo`); a slot some call shape still leaves out is an explicit error on both backends. The default of an argument the callee never uses is not evaluated: a direct call of the generator without the argument passes no operand for it, and the instance of the caller erases the default's call and the read temporary bound to it, its destruction included, so `fside()` over an unused `x: Int = side()` and `kw(3)` over an unused `s: String = make()` print nothing for the default, as on the pin (`assets/ok/unused_argument_instances.mojo`); an `@implicit` conversion of a literal default still runs its constructor, and the pin's destruction of the compile-time value it evaluated for an unused droppable default does not happen (`docs/roadmap.md` R528); a call of a function value rebound to a different function is rejected, where the pin binds the default of the function the variable was declared from (R1084); a bodiless requirement's default is still the conformer's to bind when a call through the bound leaves it out (R769). A default naming runtime storage (an enclosing local) rejects with upstream's `cannot use a dynamic value in default argument`, and a raising one rejects; one that prints runs at each call leaving the slot out, as the pin's does. A default may read a compile-time parameter in scope: the struct's through `Self` (`x: Int = Self.n * 2`), the method's or function's own (`x: Int = n`), an enclosing function's from a nested `def`, or a type parameter (`x: T = T()`); its default function declares those binders and the elaborator runs one instance per assignment of them, so each owner instance gets its own value (`assets/ok/default_reads_binder.mojo`). A collection display default is typed against its parameter, so `xs: List[Int] = [1, 2]` is a fresh `List` per call, not an `Array` (`assets/ok/collection_display_default.mojo`). |
| Calls | Homogeneous `var **kwargs` and `**kwargs^` forwarding | Run | Current declaration syntax requires consuming `var **kwargs: T` and rejects bare `**kwargs: T`; function types retain the keyword-variadic role, and callable symbols distinguish it from an ordinary parameter of the same type. Free, generic, instance, static, bounded-trait, and indirect calls materialize an owned, insertion-ordered `StringDict[T]` (keys spelled `StringLiteral` — keyword names are compile-time strings; the VM ABI is unchanged by the nominal-String takeover). Forwarding consumes it through the shared binder, specialization, duplicate, origin, ownership, and effect checks. The native backend builds the collector as the VM does — the instance's empty constructor, then one `__setitem__` per collected keyword, each value as the MIR passes it, a borrowed one copied first — so keyed reads and membership tests (`kwargs["b"]`, `"color" in options`) run natively too, through direct and indirect calls (`assets/ok/kwargs_keyed_read.mojo`). A forwarded dictionary feeds the callee's `**kwargs` alone and never a named parameter, as the pin binds a forward (`f(**kwargs^)` over `def f(a: Int = 10, var **rest: Int)` prints `1002` for `a=1, b=2`); a forward into a callee declaring no `**kwargs` is rejected, as at the pin. |
| Calls | Heterogeneous `*args: *ArgTypes` | Run | Per-element bound checking, an explicit checked runtime-pack ABI with private heterogeneous `Value::Tuple` storage, and compile-time `args.__len__()` iteration/indexing run for any checked argument (`b.__len__()` also answers at runtime, in the VM and natively, from the specialized pack's static arity, as `len(b)` does): every module-level pack-keyed `def` — whatever its body holds (a nested `def`, a lambda, or a `comptime for` the template does not keep as a loop, `assets/ok/pack_def_unkept_loop_template_served.mojo`; none is cloned), its collector read or owned, an `Int`, `UInt`, `Bool`, `Float64`, `StringLiteral`, or `DType` binder beside the pack included, whose explicit application `scaled[3](7, "x")` binds the pack from the collected arguments, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/pack_beside_value_binder.mojo`, `assets/ok/pack_def_scalar_value_binders_served.mojo`), or its pack inferred through a `Tuple[*Ts]` parameter from the argument's element list rather than from a collector (`def count[*Ts: Movable](other: Tuple[*Ts])` called with a tuple, a tuple literal, or explicit brackets, on a `def` and on a method with a pack of its own, the caller's own pack forwarding through the parameter whole — `assets/ok/pack_inferred_through_tuple.mojo`) — is served by its template, checked once with the collector a pack of the symbolic `Ts`, each `args[i]` the dependent `Ts[i]`, whose `.copy()` calls the element's copy (`assets/ok/pack_element_copy_served.mojo`) — as is a `Ts[i]` annotation and a `comptime T = Ts[i]` alias under the loop, which the elaborator carries as the element it denotes, a `StringLiteral` element's included (`assets/ok/pack_element_alias_served.mojo`, `assets/ok/pack_element_alias_string_literal.mojo`) — and the pack's length (`args.__len__()`, `Ts.length`, `len(Ts)`) a parameter expression bounding its `comptime for` (`assets/ok/pack_template_served.mojo`); such a `def` may spread its pack whole into `print`, into another served `def`, or into a method whose collector is a type pack — the method's own (`sink.take(*a)`, `Sink.take(s, *a)`, `Sink().drain(*a^)`) or a variadic struct's (`V[*Ts]().put(*a)` against `*b: *Self.Ts`) — after a fixed positional prefix too and transferring an owned pack (`tally(10, *rest)`, `drain(*items^)`), or into a constructor whose `__init__` collects a type pack — `Tuple[*Ts](*args^)`, `Tuple(*args^)`, a user variadic struct's `Bag[*Ts](*args^)` and `Bag(*args^)` against `*a: *Self.Ts`, or an own-pack constructor `Box(*args^)` beside a nullary one: the construction selects the declared `__init__` and binds the forwarded pack whole to its collector, checking its ownership and bounds, so a read pack spread into `Tuple`'s `var` collector is rejected as at the pin (`assets/ok/pack_constructor_spread_served.mojo`, `assets/type_error/pack_read_spread_into_tuple.mojo`; a cloned body still rejects a spread into a generic constructor, roadmap R440): MIR carries the call with the collector as its spread argument (`MirInstr::Call::spread`, schema 1.17; `MirInstr::MethodCall::spread`, schema 1.24), the ownership analysis sees the collector lent or moved whole, and the elaborator passes the bound pack's elements to the callee's template, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/pack_template_served.mojo`'s `relay` and `forward_owned`, `assets/ok/pack_forwarding_print.mojo`, `assets/ok/pack_argument_destroyed_after_call.mojo`'s `outer`, `assets/ok/pack_overload_sibling_forward.mojo`); a clone that spreads its bound pack into a served `def` passes the elements itself; `len(args)` is a runtime value, as upstream, and cannot bound a `comptime for` (`assets/type_error/comptime_for_runtime_pack_length.mojo`, `assets/type_error/comptime_for_runtime_pack_length_value_binder.mojo`). Before generating a specialization, the call-site conformance diagnostic identifies the one-based failing element, its concrete type, the pack, and the unsatisfied trait. A pre-check resolver assigns stable value/type binding identities, so pack spreading and type expansion respect nested definitions, lexical blocks, loops, comprehensions, sibling methods, and empty packs. Nested templates have scope-qualified identities, specialize independently at their lexical declaration, lift recursively at arbitrary depth, and preserve captures/defaults/keywords/named results. A complete forwarded pack follows the fully supplied fixed positional prefix and precedes a keyword/default tail; its private pack collector moves as one value without copying linear elements. Type and value packs may be interleaved with semantic-only `Origin`/`OriginSet` parameters; a pack consumes only its source-layout segment, so a named Origin suffix remains at the rewritten call. A second spread or explicit positional overflow after the spread is rejected, matching the pinned nightly. A variadic struct's method may collect the struct's own pack (`def put(self, *b: *Self.Ts)`): a closed instance (`V[Int, String]().put(3, "z")`) checks each argument against its element with exact arity, `b.__len__()` bounds a `comptime for` the template serves, and a forwarded pack must carry the struct pack's bounds exactly, as the pin's packs are invariant in their bounds (`assets/ok/pack_struct_method_collector.mojo`); a static call spreading that collector inside the struct's own method (`Self.count(*b)`) is rejected (roadmap R415). A pack-keyed body may also forward its pack whole into another pack-keyed method (`self.take(*a)`, after a fixed positional prefix too) or into `print` (`print(*a, sep=", ")`): each instance passes the pack element by element, and a method template whose body reads its own collector's length (`a.__len__()`) checks as a per-call clone, as one reading `Ts.length` does. One overload of a type-pack `def` may forward its pack whole to a same-named sibling (`return first + tally(*rest)`): the forward binds the one declaration whose collector the spread follows, as the pin binds it when it checks the template, and a forward that several declarations of the family would bind stays unsupported. Specialization exposes the concrete type of each statically indexed pack element; ordinary tuple-valued homogeneous variadics remain nominal `List` collectors. A read collector borrows what it gathers, as a `read` parameter does: a local outlives the call and a temporary is destroyed once the call returns, where an owning `var` collector takes its elements (`assets/ok/pack_argument_destroyed_after_call.mojo`); an element passed on to `print` or to another read parameter is read where it lies, as an element of a `Tuple`, a `List` or an `Array` is, so no copy constructor runs for the pass (`assets/ok/pack_element_read_borrowed.mojo`); a value read of a homogeneous collector's element (`var x = a[1]`) loads the element place and copies that element alone, as a field read does (`assets/ok/variadic_element_value_read.mojo`), and `len(a)` — like `len` of a named tuple — reads the storage in place and copies no element (`assets/ok/variadic_collector_len.mojo`); an element read through a `Span` still copies (roadmap R117). An owning `var` collector lives until the last use of any element read out of it, and its elements are then destroyed last to first — the reverse of a `Tuple`'s storage — for a module `def` (served by its template), a method, a homogeneous collector, and a pack nothing reads, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/owned_pack_destroyed_in_reverse.mojo`); an element transferred out of a pack by subscript (`a[i]^`) is rejected, as the pin rejects it (`assets/type_error/owned_pack_element_transfer.mojo`); the pin destroys a heterogeneous owned pack before consuming a literal-index element read that is its last use, printing an element whose destructor already ran, and Mojito keeps consuming it first (`docs/non-goals.md`, `conformance/probes/owned_pack_literal_index_destroyed_before_use.mojo`). |
| Calls | Generic callable constraints and callable-value parameters | Run | `F: def(T) -> T` is retained as a dependent checked constraint, makes `F` callable inside the generic body, and validates a monomorphic contract against one monomorphic function type or nominal callable struct. A nominal callable struct is called directly or through such a constraint, and its `__call__` is instantiated only where a call reaches it; it is no value of a `def(...)` type, so passing it where a `def(...)` parameter or binding is expected is rejected, as at the pin ("cannot be converted from 'Scale' to 'def(Int) -> Int'"). A contract with its own `def[...]` list instead accepts an alpha-equivalent generic function, retaining binder arity, bounds, conventions, effects, reference origins, and invocation defaults. Compatibility is directional: a nonraising/read-only implementation may satisfy a raising/mutable contract, while ownership-changing conventions and reference-origin contracts remain exact. An explicit environment qualifier on the right-hand side (`callback: def(...) thin` or `capturing[...]`) declares a compile-time callable-value parameter; `OriginSet`, `capturing[_]`, `capturing[origins]`, and the infer-only `//` marker are retained, and the supplied function or closure is reified into a hidden typed runtime local for indirect calls; a generic closure that captures (`callback: def[n: Int](Int) capturing[origins] -> Int`, called as `callback[2](40)`) is instead folded into the instance like a function, which takes only the closure's by-reference environment as trailing reference parameters and calls the closure's `n = 2` specialization directly with it, so writes to the captured places are seen and the environment forwards through a further binding. Callable-value defaults are symbolic selected-function, earlier-parameter, or compile-time-conditional plans evaluated in declaration order. Comptime specialization bakes the scalar/type/pack values it inspects while retaining callable and Origin arguments on the residual signature and call site; `CtValue` deliberately has no function or closure variant, and arbitrary callable CTFE is not implied. A generic `def` passed where a non-generic `def(...)` value is expected is rejected at the argument, as at the pin, which infers no specialization from the parameter: only an explicit specialization (`f[...]`) is a non-generic function value, and a generic `def` named as a tuple display element is rejected as the pin rejects it ("cannot use parametric function as a runtime closure"). |
| Calls | Callable capture-origin contracts | Run | Checked function types distinguish unqualified, `thin`, and `capturing` environments. Materialized closures carry a canonical concrete origin set with read/write access per captured owner; `OriginSet` parameters and inference placeholders remain symbolic until a concrete callable is supplied. These dependencies participate in substitution, nominal conformance, origin escape checking, checked HIR, typed MIR declarations, and direct, indirect, method, and nominal subscript capture effects, so persistent-loan analysis sees mutations performed by a directly called closure or a non-escaping callable argument. A capturing closure no longer binds to an unqualified `def(...)` value contract — the checker rejects it with a hint naming the `capturing[...]` spelling, matching current Mojo; comptime callable bounds (`F: def(...)`) still ground capturing values, as upstream accepts. A thin (non-capturing) callable satisfies every `capturing[...]` value contract — its capture set is empty, a subset of any allowed origin set — matching upstream's acceptance of plain functions for capturing funargs. |
| Calls | Method/generic argument-binding parity | Run | Includes ordinary-parameter write-back. Explicit method-level value-parameter bindings on non-pack structs (`widget.pick[3]()`, `holder.pick[3]()` on an origin-parameterized struct, defaulted `scaled(4)` beside `scaled[10](4)`) select the per-call clone, whose baked parameters occupy no MIR argument slots; a `ref self` method on a call temporary (`s.split(",")[1]`) keeps the temporary alive through the result. |
| Calls | User-defined static methods | Run | Uses the same positional/default/keyword ABI without a receiver slot. A static called through `Self` in a method or another static (`Self.cells()`, `Self.offset(100)`) is called on the instance `Self` names, the enclosing struct applied to its own parameters, as at the pin (`assets/ok/static_call_through_self.mojo`). A receiver is a type when the check resolved it to one, so a local that once shared the type's spelling in a block that has ended leaves `Point.origin()` calling the static, while a live local of that spelling stays a value receiver (`assets/ok/static_call_through_shadowed_type_name.mojo`); a `comptime for` header that spells one is still rejected (roadmap R340). An instance method called through its type takes the receiver as the first argument, as upstream (`List[Int].__len__(xs)`, `Point.shift(p, 10)`, `Optional[Int].value(opt)`): with no static of that name, the call checks and lowers as `xs.__len__()`, and a receiver whose type arguments disagree with the application is rejected (`List[String].__len__(xs)`). On a parameterized struct, a static dispatches through an explicit `Dict[Int, String].fromkeys(...)` receiver or a bare `Dict.fromkeys(keys, 0)` receiver whose struct parameters are inferred from the argument types (defaults such as `H: Hasher = default_hasher` fill in); `Self` in the signature resolves to the instantiated struct, and overloads — including same-arity pairs — run their checker-selected symbols on both backends. A static call read as a call argument or operator operand, a `ref`-returning one included, runs on both backends: its type receiver names no place (`assets/ok/static_reference_result_argument.mojo`). An explicit receiver application partitions its origin slots as construction does (`View[origin_of(w)].make(w)`, `Span[Int, origin_of(xs)]`); a static binding `ref [Self.o]` arguments is a constructor in all but name — the explicit origin is checked against the binding argument and the result keeps it lent — and every static retains its `mut`/`ref` argument places and alias-checks like an instance call. A static's own compile-time parameters apply explicitly on every receiver spelling — a plain struct's bare name (`Lanes.ident[Int](3)`), the single-argument application that parses as a value subscript (`Box[String].ident[Int](3)`), and a type application (`Box[Int].ident[Int](3)`) — with a type or `DType` key, a `comptime if` folding on it, and the same per-call clone the inferred spelling mints (`assets/ok/static_method_explicit_application.mojo`). |
| Calls | Callable expressions / first-class function values | Run | Non-capturing, generic, origin-specialized, and contextually selected overloaded functions can be stored, passed, and indirectly invoked with checked raising and capture effects plus reference returns. A call or read through a local callable names the declaration its name resolves to, so a block's `var f` shadows an outer `f` only inside that block, and once the block ends the name is the outer local or module function again. Explicit Origin arguments participate in direct overloaded and generic calls, combine with explicitly supplied ordinary generic arguments in function values, and select an overloaded function value when an expected `def(...)` type identifies one candidate; an uncontextualized overloaded value remains ambiguous. User structs are callable only when they nominally conform to a matching `def(...)` contract and implement `__call__`; callable generic parameters, environment constraints, `mut`/`ref` conventions, origin-bearing results, the receiver place, and the exact signature-qualified `__call__` target survive conformance and indirect lowering, including same-arity overload sets. A same-arity method overload whose parameter is the enclosing struct type keys as `$ov$Self` on both the declaration and call sides however it is spelled (bare `Self` or the explicit self-application `List[Self.T]`), so a `def m(self, other: Self)` beside `def m(self, n: Int)` dispatches to the right definition instead of failing at the VM. Captured nested Origin specialization remains a documented Mojito extension over the pinned nightly. |
| Calls | Named `out` results | Run | A function's or a method's single `out` parameter is a caller-transparent result slot and must be initialized before fallthrough or bare return (`def flip(deinit self, out result: Pair)`, on an instance, a consuming, a static, and a generic struct's method — `assets/ok/method_named_result.mojo`); a second named result, and one beside `->`, are rejected as at the pin (`assets/type_error/method_two_named_results.mojo`). A named result of a type parameter is still reported abandoned (roadmap R351). |
| Conventions | `imm`/legacy `read`, `var`, `mut`, `ref` parameters and `ref self` | Run | `imm` and its temporary compatibility synonym `read` share immutable-reference semantics. A bare aggregate variable passed to a read-convention or `mut`/`ref` parameter binds as a shallow place read — no argument-side `__copyinit__` runs, matching Mojo's borrow (consuming `var`/`deinit` passes still run the copy lifecycle; field-chain and reference-result actuals already read shallowly). The builtin `print` binds its positional arguments the same way: a named struct- or type-parameter-typed value is read in place, and an owned temporary (`print(x.copy())`, `print(IC(5))`) is destroyed once `print` returns (`assets/ok/print_argument_read_in_place.mojo`). A subscript is its getter's call: over a by-value `__getitem__` (`-> T`), `b[i]` is an owned temporary rather than a place, so `f(b[1])`, `b[2] < b[3]`, `b[4].__lt__(b[5])`, `b[6].v`, and a discarded `b[13]` destroy it once the call returns, and `g(b[9])` into a `var` parameter, `var t = b[10]`, `_ = b[11]`, `b[12]^`, and a chain middle take it without demanding a copy, as upstream (`assets/ok/by_value_subscript_temporary_drop.mojo`). Copyable immutable arguments overlapping a `mut`/`ref`/`^` access in the same call are materialized through the implicit copy that satisfies exclusivity; non-Copyable aliases remain errors. A construction is alias-checked as its initializer's call, keyword, generic, and `*args` constructors included: `P(s, s)` over `__init__(out self, mut a: S, b: S)` and `Tuple(Span(xs), Span(xs))` are rejected as upstream's "initializer call" (`assets/type_error/constructor_mut_argument_aliases_read.mojo`, `assets/type_error/initializer_arguments_share_mutable_origin.mojo`). |
| Conventions | `out self`, named `out` results, `deinit self`, owned-`var` transfer | Run | Lifecycle receivers and a single named result, on a function or a method, are supported. Consuming positions — `var`/`deinit` parameters and receivers, operator `var` operands (`p + q` on `List`), variable initialization, assignment, field stores, returns, displays, and constructor arguments — accept a *place* only when its type is `ImplicitlyCopyable`; otherwise spell `^` (a mutable or owned binding) or `.copy()`. A static method's `var` parameter makes the same demand as an instance method's (`assets/type_error/implicit_copy_static_var_argument.mojo`); an owned `var *args` collector makes it of each place it gathers, on the free, method, static and constructor paths, and a tuple display or `Tuple(…)` reaches `Tuple`'s own collector (`assets/type_error/implicit_copy_variadic_var_collector.mojo`, `assets/type_error/implicit_copy_tuple_display_element.mojo`). There is no last-use move: `take(a)` rejects even when `a` is dead afterwards, as upstream. A non-`ImplicitlyCopyable` read overlapping a `mut`/`ref` access in the same call is an aliasing error rather than a hidden copy, and a *read* of a nominal value (including `String`) never disarms exclusivity by copying — `bump(mut s, s)` on a `String` is rejected. A consuming `var`/`deinit` slot does copy the place out before the callee runs, so it never overlaps: `bump(mut s, var s)`, and the element store `xs[i] = xs[j]` it underlies, are accepted for every element type. A borrowing view conversion (`var s: Span[Int, _] = xs`) consumes the conversion temporary, not the source place; a temporary source (`f(String("abc"))` at a `StringSpan` parameter, or the receiver of a view-returning method — `String("abc").codepoints()`, `.__reversed__()`, `.as_bytes()`, `.split(",")`) materializes in a hidden owned slot the view borrows, living as long as the view, so such a view cannot be returned. Every other owning temporary is destroyed as soon as its consumer finishes, running its `__deinit__` where the pinned Mojo does: the receiver of a method (`B(3).show()`, `print(B(2).get())`, a chained `B(5).twin().show()`) right after that call returns, before the enclosing expression continues; an argument at a read parameter (`take(B(2))`, positional or keyword) once the callee returns; and a temporary read for a field (`print(B(1).x)`, `var n = B(9).name`) after the instruction consuming the field, an owning field being copied out first. A `var`/`deinit` receiver or parameter takes the temporary and destroys it in the callee. A `^` transfer of a whole local that a regular `var`/`deinit` parameter takes — an argument, a keyword argument, a receiver (`a^.consume()`), or a struct operator's operand (`(c^) + (d^)`, `-(n^)`) — hands over the source's storage, so no move initializer runs, as at the pin; a move into new storage (a binding, a return, a field store, a container element) still runs it (`assets/ok/transfer_into_owned_argument.mojo`). The same holds for each `^` transfer an owning `var *xs` collector gathers, homogeneous or heterogeneous, and for a dying `M(copy=a)` element: the pack holds the caller's storage and destroys its elements last to first when it dies (`assets/ok/variadic_collector_forwarded_and_borrowed.mojo`). |
| Lifecycle | `Deinitable`/`__deinit__`, unified copy/move initialization, and `@implicit` constructors | Run | Ordinary values are implicitly deinitable; an explicitly false conditional conformance makes a type linear. A field typed by a bare struct type parameter (`var value: Self.T`) needs a bound proving `Deinitable` (the trait, a trait refining it, or `TrivialRegisterPassable`) unless the struct declares a `Deinitable` conformance, conditionally or not, which discharges it (`field 'value' has non-'Deinitable' type 'T'`, as upstream). The pin's deprecated `__del__` spelling is accepted and normalized to `__deinit__` at parse time (removed when upstream removes it); `ImplicitlyDeletable`, which the pin has removed, is an unknown trait. A declared conditional `Movable` conformance is effective: `Movable where False` rejects `^` transfers, `var` parameters/receivers, and move/copy captures, while `deinit` consumption (destructors, named destructors) stays legal and Copyable pass-by-value is unaffected. A bare `move:` initializer parameter is rejected with a migration diagnostic naming the `deinit move: Self` spelling. `Copyable` carries current Mojo's non-overridable default `copy()`: elaboration synthesizes `def copy(self) -> Self` on every struct declaring `Copyable`/`ImplicitlyCopyable` that does not spell its own, delegating to a declared `__init__(out self, *, copy: Self)` (run exactly once per copy — the construction reads its source as a retained borrowed place) or falling back to the fieldwise copy lifecycle, with a conditional conformance's predicate carried over as the method's `where` clause; a `Copyable`-bounded parameter resolves the same `copy() -> Self` contract whenever its copyability is proven (a refining bound, a member bound, or a `conforms_to(T, Copyable)` availability clause), and `copy()` on a built-in copyable value (scalars, literals, tuples, variants) is the value read itself. `ImplicitlyCopyable` may be declared alongside an explicit `__init__(out self, *, copy: Self)` — the explicit initializer is then the conformance, as for upstream `String`; without one every field must be `ImplicitlyCopyable`. `String`, `Codepoint`, and `Optional[T]` where `T` is `ImplicitlyCopyable` conform. An explicit `Name(copy=src)` or `Name[Args](copy=src)` whose source is a temporary, or a whole owned local or `var` parameter at its last use, moves the source instead of copying it, as upstream's lifetime checker does; a source read afterwards, a field source, a type parameter's `T(copy=x)`, and `.copy()` still copy (`assets/ok/copy_construction_last_use_moves.mojo`). One an owned argument or owned (`var`/`deinit`) receiver takes at its source's last use (`take(M(copy=y))`, `M(copy=d).consume()`) forwards the source's storage instead, with no move initializer, as at the pin; a raising operand evaluated after it before the call keeps the move (`assets/ok/transfer_into_owned_argument.mojo`, `assets/ok/copy_construction_owned_receiver_forwards.mojo`). One a read parameter, read receiver, or read operator operand takes at its source's last use (`show(M(copy=a))`, `M(copy=a).show()`) lends the source's storage, with no copy or move initializer, and the source dies when the call returns; a raising argument evaluated after the construction makes it a move, and a `ref self` receiver, whatever the method does with it, keeps the move, as at the pin (`assets/ok/copy_construction_read_argument_lends.mojo`, `assets/ok/copy_construction_read_receiver_lends.mojo`). A copy or move naming its constructor is the call upstream's copy/move insertion leaves (`mir::lower_copies_and_moves`): `place.load` plus a `Call` of the `__copyinit__` for a copy, a forwarding `var.use` plus a `Call` of the `__moveinit__` for a move, the call alone for a register copy, which both backends run as any constructor call; a copy or move naming none, of a compile-time value parameter's slot, or of a collector a call spreads stays a use. |
| Lifecycle | `@explicit_destroy` | Run | The required string argument customizes linearity diagnostics but does not itself make a type linear. As at the pin, the decorator needs a conditional `Deinitable` conformance (`Deinitable where False`, or one under a `where` clause); on a struct that is `Deinitable` unconditionally it is rejected with the pin's text (`assets/type_error/explicit_destroy_unconditional_deinitable.mojo`). Named `deinit self` methods discharge path-sensitive obligations. A `var` parameter or local typed by a type parameter (or a dependent pack projection) whose bounds do not prove `Deinitable` is linear too, as upstream: abandoning it reports `abandoned without being explicitly destroyed: unhandled explicitly destroyed type 'AnyType'` (the pin names the parameter's own bound there). So is a call result of that type in a position that takes no ownership of it — an argument the callee only borrows, an argument of `print`, a discarded expression statement — reported as `'(expression temporary)'` abandoned. The same holds for any call result or display whose struct is not `Deinitable` under the body's bounds — a borrowed method receiver (`mk().get()`, `[Res(1)][0]`) and the object of a field read (`mk().id`) take no ownership either — reported with the struct's `@explicit_destroy` message, or upstream's `type 'Res' does not conform to 'Deinitable' and must be explicitly destroyed` without one (`assets/type_error/abandoned_struct_temporary.mojo`, `array_display_temporary_abandoned.mojo`); the bundled `Array`, `List`, `Tuple`, `Set`, `Dict`, and `OwnedPointer` carry upstream's `deinit_with()`/`into_inner()` messages. A parametric body is checked for these with its parameters symbolic whatever its call sites: a compile-time-keyed template through source validation, every other bound-generic template because elaboration now keeps it alongside its specializations. Abandonment, overwrite, partial/double/conditional destruction are rejected; a raising named destructor consumes the value on both edges (an `except` arm cannot re-consume it — `use of uninitialized value`, as upstream) and automatic `DropVar` destruction is suppressed. Discarding a transferred linear or explicit-destroy value (`_ = x^`) abandons it: upstream's `'x' abandoned without being explicitly destroyed: <message>` is reported for abandonment, overwrite, and discard alike, and destroying it twice is `use of uninitialized value 'x'`. Every potentially raising operation — a raising call, operator, conversion, `__iter__`, or a `raise` statement — is a raise edge, and every such value still owned there is abandoned, reported as upstream's `abandoned without being explicitly destroyed: <message>` with `note: value was not consumed when an error is thrown`: a named local or `var` parameter (a linear type-parameter one too), an owned temporary evaluated before a later raising operand, and a `^` transfer, which its call consumes only after every operand ran (`assets/type_error/explicit_destroy_temporary_abandoned_on_raise.mojo`, `explicit_destroy_transfer_abandoned_on_raise.mojo`, `explicit_destroy_local_abandoned_on_raise.mojo`, `linear_parameter_abandoned_on_raise.mojo`). A raise inside a `try` ends the values declared in its body; an outer one flows into the `except` arm, which starts from the join of the states its raise edges reach it with — a value consumed after the raising call is still owned there and must be consumed (`explicit_destroy_consumed_after_raise_in_try.mojo`), one consumed on some edges only is `use of uninitialized value` — while an arm no raise edge reaches is unreachable and unchecked, and a raise reaching `finally` runs it first (`assets/ok/explicit_destroy_raise_edges.mojo`). |
| Functions | Recursion and conservative return checking | Run | Sibling forward references, mutual recursion, and a nested function naming itself from its own body (a call, a value read, or either from a function nested deeper inside it) are rejected; upstream asks for such recursion at file scope. The future-syntax words `class`, `del`, `match`, and `yield` remain lexer-level identifiers, but declaration checking rejects them as free or nested function names; they remain legal as ordinary variables/parameters, and method names such as `match` remain valid. |
| Functions | Non-escaping closures | Run | Current capture lists use `{imm name}`, `{mut name}`, `{ref name}`, `{var name}`, move/default forms, or `{}`. A `@__parameter` (or deprecated `@parameter`) parametric closure takes no capture list (`expected ':' in function definition`, as upstream) and captures implicitly per binding — a mutable enclosing local as `mut`, an immutable one as `imm`, so writing a captured immutable parameter reports `expression must be mutable for in-place operator destination`; a plain nested def without a list cannot write an enclosing local (`Could not infer capture convention of the captured value`). A nested def or lambda is a generator over its enclosing declarations' compile-time parameters: it reads a value parameter as its own parameter, never as a capture, and names a type or value binder in its signature, its captures' types, and its own `comptime if`/`comptime for` (`assets/ok/nested_def_reads_enclosing_parameter.mojo`, `assets/ok/nested_def_enclosing_binders.mojo`). MIR records the declaration each lifted body is nested in (`MirFunctionDeclaration::enclosing`), the elaborator instantiates the body under each instance of its enclosing declaration. A call by name is a direct call of the lifted body with its captures as leading reference arguments, as upstream calls the lifted closure generator, so it reduces, forwards, and fills defaults as any direct call does (`h[n](x: Arr[n])` returning `ign2(x)` keeps neither `x` nor one instance per `n`); a closure is made only at a first-class use, or at the declaration for a copied or moved capture or a sibling-captured closure (`assets/ok/unused_argument_nested_variadic_instances.mojo`). A nested def or lambda declared in a `comptime for` body reads the loop's index in its body and its signature without capturing it, each unrolled iteration declaring its own as at the pin, in a generic `def`, a plain one, and a method, whether a plain, generic (`inner[5]()`), `@parameter`, or capturing def; so does one declared in a kept `comptime if` arm (`assets/ok/nested_def_reads_comptime_for_index.mojo`). MIR declares the indices as the body's `region_binders` and the elaborator instantiates it per region copy. A def whose only reads of its enclosing scope are parameters captures nothing and is `thin`, as upstream, and passes where a `def() thin` is expected (`assets/ok/thin_nested_def_reads_value_parameter.mojo`). A generic nested def binds its own parameters at each call, inferred or spelled (`inner[Int](3)`), beside its enclosing ones; one with any by-reference capture — a captured local, a `mut` capture — specializes per call on both backends, the native instance taking its environment as leading reference arguments (`assets/ok/generic_nested_def_captures.mojo`); one with a by-value (`{var x}`) capture runs on the VM only (roadmap R100). Reference captures retain frame/slot handles without imposing a declaration-to-call loan, so `imm` observes intervening outer updates; `var` copies at declaration, and move capture transfers at declaration. Checked captures retain exact owner identity, storage type, and convention even when unused. Lexical declarations lift recursively at arbitrary depth while preserving materialized sibling snapshots, ancestor forwarding, exact block/loop/unpack/handler shadowing, recursion, cloned compile-time/default-method occurrences, nested generics, effects, argument markers, named results, reference returns, closure values, and method `self`; escape is rejected. `def(...)`-typed struct fields and collection elements are rejected with a contextual error, matching current Mojo (which treats a bare `def(...)` in those type positions as a trait): callable values are limited to parameters and `def(...) thin` local bindings — bare and `capturing` local annotations reject like fields and elements, and a generic function does not contextually specialize into a stored callable value. An uncontextualized display of non-capturing function values (`var fns = [double, triple]`) stores them as `Array[def(...) thin -> R, N]`, as upstream, and each element calls through its subscript (`fns[0](5)`; the element reference keeps the array alive through the call); an element binds to a local and reassigns like any value (`var f = fns[1]`, since a thin value captures nothing), while a display of one capturing lambda (`[lambda (x: Int) {k} -> Int: x * k]`) stores as an `Array` of that closure's own type, and its reference captures stay borrowed while the array lives. Two capturing lambdas never share a display (each lambda has its own type upstream). A named closure element and a transferred non-`ImplicitlyCopyable` capture are both rejected, and so is reassigning or returning an aggregate that stores a closure. Calling through a `ref` to a callable (`for f in fns: f(1)`, `ref g = fns[0]`) is a recorded gap. The removed `unified {...}` spelling is rejected with a contextual parse error. |
| Functions | Lambda expressions | Run | `lambda [[params]] [(args)] [effects] [{captures}] [-> T]: expr` — the parser synthesizes a hidden nested `def` whose body is `return <expr>`, so checking, capture analysis, closure lifting, and indirect calls reuse the nested-def pipeline, and the closure materializes at the expression's evaluation point (`{var}` snapshots once per loop iteration; an immediately invoked owned-capture lambda gets a synthetic stable slot). An omitted return type is fixed to `None`, never inferred, with a contextual diagnostic naming the rule. An omitted capture list imm-captures free variables — thin when none are captured — while explicit `{}` captures nothing ("could not infer capture convention") and an explicit capture-all convention (`{imm}`) or a lambda-owned parameter list forces a closure even with zero captures. Arguments must be parenthesized and typed (contextual parse diagnostic); conventions, variadics, and `raises` parse as on `def`s. Generic lambdas invoke inline (`(lambda [N: Int](x: Int) {} -> Int: x + N)[5](3)`), bind callable-bound generic type parameters as runtime arguments, and compile-time unrolling clones a lambda per iteration with the unroll variable substituted (hidden names uniquify at final-tree rekeying); partial parameter application into a stored value remains unsupported (value-base brackets dispatch runtime element calls, and a stored callable value takes no compile-time parameters). Storing a lambda in a local requires the same explicit `def(...)`/`capturing[...]` annotation as any function value (upstream infers un-annotated `var f = lambda …`), an immediately invoked lambda whose body is a single expression evaluates in a compile-time initializer (`comptime whole = (lambda (x: Int) {} -> Int: x * 2)(21)`), and lambdas in parameter defaults, a free `def`'s or a struct method's, are rejected contextually. |
| Functions | Function-type `where` clauses | Run | A `thin` function type with its own `def[...]` parameters carries trailing `where` clauses (`F: def[w: Int](Int) thin -> None where (w > 0, "msg")`): clauses lower onto the anonymous contract's parameter declarations with alpha-renamed binder identity, explicit specializations through the parameter evaluate them, and the binding rule is directional — a constrained function needs the contract to declare the matching clause, while unconstrained-into-constrained is allowed. Binder-less clauses and `comptime` function-type aliases are recorded mojo-only gaps. |
| Functions | Inline decorators | Run | `@always_inline`, `@always_inline("nodebug")`, `@always_inline("builtin")`, `@no_inline`, and `@inline(level)` with `level` a spelled `InlineLevel` member (`.always`, `.nodebug`, `.never`, `.automatic`, or `InlineLevel.always` and the like) on a `def`, a nested `def`, a struct method, or a trait method set the function's inline level, which its MIR declaration and the package record carry (`inline: always`, `nodebug`, `builtin`, `never`; `automatic` is left unwritten). A call to a `nodebug` or `builtin` function is inlined before elaboration (`inline-parametric`, upstream's `InlineParametric`) when its arguments bind, positionally or by keyword, to `read`, owned, `mut`, or `ref` parameters and it returns a value; A call to a plain `@always_inline` function is inlined when its finished body is below the pin's size bound, measured as the pin measures it, by a count of operations (`inline_cost` under `INLINE_SIZE_BOUND`, 32): 31 `s += x` statements inline and 32 keep the call, ten `if`s inline and eleven do not, four `while` loops inline and five do not, one `print` inlines and two do not, and a `for` loop never does, as at the pin (assets/ok/always_inline_small_calls.mojo); its code takes the call's location, as a `nodebug` callee's does, where the pin keeps the callee's own under an `inlinedAt` scope (R1074). Other calls, and every call of a `never` function, stay calls. A generic callee, or a non-parametric struct's method with its own compile-time parameters, is inlined with its binders bound to the call's arguments, a literal, an inferred or defaulted one, or one over the caller's own parameters, so it is no longer instantiated per binding. A variadic binder binds to the call's element list, so `len(values)` and `values[i]` over `*values: Int` and `len(Ts)` over `*Ts` read it, a spread of a type pack in the body's types (`Tuple[*Ts]`) becomes its elements, and a pack the caller forwards, as `relayed[*Us](t: Tuple[*Us])` forwards its own to `arity(t, 1)`, binds to the caller's (assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_generic_calls.mojo). A parametric struct's method, and a call naming a struct that declares one `__init__`, are inlined with the struct's parameters bound to the receiver's or the constructed type's arguments, so `Self.k` reads as its constant and the constructed value is the `out self` slot the inlined body initializes; a struct keyed on one type pack, as `Tuple[*Ts]` is, binds it to the receiver's element list, one over the caller's own parameters included, and a subscript calls its accessor as a method, its compile-time index bound to the index's constant, so `t.__len__()` and `t[0]` inline (assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_tuple_members.mojo). A `Tuple` member whose body applies a compile-time function to the owner's pack (`Tuple.reverse`, `Tuple.concat`, their `comptime for` bounded by `Self.Ts.length`) inlines too, on a forwarded, loaded, or copied receiver, the application applied to the bound element list, so `pair.concat(suffix)` in `main` holds no `__getitem_param__` call. A `mut` or `ref` parameter, a `mut self` included, is the caller's place the call passes, as the VM binds the parameter to a reference to it, so `bump(y)`, `bump(p.a)`, and `p.advance()` inline, through a caller's own `mut` parameter too. A callee returning a reference is inlined too, the reference its body makes being to the caller's place the call passes, a `read` argument's included, so `pick(y) += 1` over `pick(ref x) -> ref [x] Int`, `b.val() += 1` over a `ref self` getter, and a callee of several returns inline (assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_reference_results.mojo); one whose returned reference is to an argument the call has no place for keeps the call. Arguments bind through the shared call matcher, as the VM binds them, and an omitted argument takes its default, materialized at the call as the pin materializes it in the caller: a literal (`scale(3)` for `factor: Int = 2`), or a converting constructor run on one (`label()` for `s: String = "plain"`), which the call drops after the inlined body when the parameter only borrows it; so `scale(offset=1, x=3)` inlines too. A raising callee is inlined too, its raises propagating as the call's: one inlined into a `try`'s body ends the callee's locals on that `try`'s raise edge, as the pin destroys them, where a kept call still leaks them, as every raise leaving a function does (R20). A nested `nodebug` `def` is inlined into the body that declares it, through the closure that body makes: a capture by reference (`{base}`, `{mut count}`) is the captured place itself, and a read of an enclosing declaration's parameter (`k` of `def scaled_by[k: Int]`, `Self.n` in a method) reads that binder, so a generic nested `def` inlines too. A drop of a value whose struct's `__deinit__` is `nodebug`, `String`'s included, holds the destructor's body where the value dies, its `deinit self` the dropped variable and its struct's parameters bound to the variable type's, so `Self.k` in `D[k]`'s destructor reads the caller's `n` for a `D[n]` local; only a variable holding a whole value on every path to its drop is so expanded, and one a path may have moved from keeps the destructor call. A field's drop expands the same way where its owner holds the field whole, its `deinit self` the field's place, as the pin calls `__del__` on the field's address: a synthesized destructor's fields, so a `Holder[k]` holding a `Probe[k]` drops through `Probe`'s inlined body, a `deinit self`'s remaining fields, and a field a store overwrites, through a `mut self` too; so does a drop in an `except` handler of a variable whole at the body's raise points. An explicit `self.d^.finish()` of a `nodebug` `finish` binds its `deinit self` to the field's place, and a raising `finish` to a copy of the field (R1106). A destructor available only under a `where` clause keeps its drop until the elaborator names the instance's (R302). Output is otherwise the same either way (assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_calls.mojo, assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_generic_calls.mojo, assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_struct_calls.mojo, assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_tuple_members.mojo, assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_nested_calls.mojo, assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_destructor.mojo, assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_reference_results.mojo). A nested `def` with a copied or moved capture (`{var x}`, `{s^}`) used only by name holds each such capture in a slot of its own in the declaring frame, copied or moved at the declaration, and its calls pass that slot, so they inline too; one declared in a `comptime for` inlines in the loop, naming the loop's index; a callee passed a closure of reference captures as its callable binder (`apply[plus](3)`) inlines with the closure's call; and a closure of reference captures nothing reads after inlining is erased, its slot and drop with it (assets/ok/nested_def_inline_shapes.mojo). A trait default's synthesized forwarding wrapper is `nodebug`, as at the pin. Every kind of compile-time parameter binds at an inlined call, as the pin's inliner substitutes every parameter alike: a callable binder bound to the function the call passes (`apply[double](3)`), a struct member whose owner carries an origin argument (`Span.unsafe_ptr`, `Span.__getitem__`, an iterator's `__copyinit__`), a `where` clause or constrained binder the checker judged at the call (`check[2]()`), a conditional member, and a `comptime for` over a value pack (`weighted[1, 2, 3]()`); a `read` argument passed from a place (`peek(b)`), read through a reference the body made (`range`'s `__iter__` copying `self`), or trivially copied binds too, and a copied, iterated, or lent place-bound parameter reads the caller's place (`total(xs)` over `for x in xs`), so a `for` over `range` holds no `_ZeroStartingRange.__copyinit__` call and `s.unsafe_ptr()` no `Span` call (assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_binders.mojo). A callee constructing its type binder at a concrete binding inlines too, the inlined body holding the bound type's own construction as an instance's does: `make[Int]()` over `return T()` a zero, `make[SIMD[DType.float32, 4]]()` a zero splat, `make[Counter]()` the struct's initializer call, `first[Int, Bool]()` over `return Ts[0]()` the selected element's, and `dup(7)` and `dup(s)` over `return T(copy=x)` the value copy and the struct's copy initializer call (assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_type_construction.mojo). A call that collects or spreads arguments inlines as the pin inlines it: a homogeneous collector (`total(1, 2, 3)`, `names(a, String("cde"))`, `sink.take(1, 2)`, the empty `total()`), an owned one (`owned(a, String("f"))`), a heterogeneous one (`emit(s, 1, "x", 2.5)`), a spread of the caller's pack (`show(*args)` from `outer[*Ts]`), and a forwarded `**kwargs^` (`inner(**kwargs^)`): the call's block builds the collector as a `tuple.make` typed the collector's slot, its elements the caller's for a `read` collector and the body's to drop for an owned one, a spread binds the caller's collector whole, and a forward binds the dictionary, on the VM and natively (assets/ok/always_inline_nodebug_collectors.mojo). A place-bound parameter named as a slot other than by a copy, an iteration, or a loan, a `read` argument read through a reference the block did not make, and a reference-returning callee whose reference is to a temporary of its own (R1099), a call collecting keywords afresh into `var **kwargs` (R1070), a default computed by a function (R1077), a literal default at a SIMD vector or a constructor's binder (R1077), and a copy- or move-capturing nested `def` that a sibling captures, called through its closure (R1104), are not yet inlined, nor is a `Ts[i]()` under a `comptime for` index (R445), a `conforms_to` test of a concrete binding (R1096), and a `call.indirect` through an inlined callable binder's constant stays indirect until `canonicalize` folds it (R1098); the size bound prices `print` and a `for` loop by the pin's observed cost rather than by their operations (R1075), and a slot with several stores, whose test against the value it holds the pin folds, as live code (R1076); a recursive `@always_inline` function, which the pin rejects, is accepted and its calls kept (R925). An `@always_inline` operand other than `"nodebug"` or `"builtin"`, more than one operand, an operand to `@no_inline`, an `@inline` with no operand, more than one, or a keyword one, an `InlineLevel` member that does not exist (`.bogus`, `.builtin`), or two decorators naming different levels are rejected with the pin's message (assets/type_error/always_inline_operand_rejected.mojo, assets/type_error/inline_level_conflict_rejected.mojo, assets/type_error/inline_level_missing_operand_rejected.mojo, assets/type_error/inline_level_unknown_member_rejected.mojo). An `@inline` operand that is any other compile-time expression is rejected as unsupported: a closed `InlineLevel` value such as a `comptime` constant (R940), and a level given by a parameter (R941). The same decorators on a trait method, requirement or default, are validated as a function's are, and a default's member function (`Trait.m`) records the level it names; the documentation decorators `@doc_hidden`, `@stable`, and `@deprecated` are accepted there too, `@staticmethod`, `@export`, `@__parameter`/`@parameter`, and `@__unsafe_nested_origins_read_only` are unsupported there (R1165), and a `@staticmethod` requirement, which has no `self`, is a parse error (R942). The bundled library carries the pin's inline markings, in the pin's spelling, on the functions it shares with upstream's std (`@inline(.always)`, `@inline(.nodebug)`, `@inline(.never)`, and `@always_inline("builtin")` on `Span` and `Tuple` methods); a Mojito-only overload stays unmarked. Each records its level, and the pass inlines the bundled `nodebug` callees a call reaches directly: `_is_valid_utf8` into `String(from_utf8=)`, so 493 fixtures no longer instantiate it, `String`'s destructor at every drop it can expand, `String.unsafe_ptr` and `capacity_bytes`, `rotate_bits_left`, and `List`'s `__len__` and `capacity`; with the pass off, the corpus's concrete functions would be 46,968 rather than 46,388, and its concrete instructions 651,762 rather than 659,919 (`benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`). `Tuple`'s `__len__` and `__getitem_param__` inline too, so 4 fixtures rather than 148 keep a `__getitem_param__` instance. The rest keep their calls: `String`'s literal constructor, which the bundled library leaves unmarked (R1101), and a member reached only through a trait-bound call, as `len(xs)` reaches `List.__len__`, which only the elaborator resolves (`len` itself is a checker builtin, R988), and which native code does not mark for inlining either (R987); a destructor whose every drop was expanded is no longer instantiated, so `assets/ok/mut_owned_params.mojo`'s concrete program has no `String.__deinit__`. |
| Declarations | Decorator validation | Check | Every decorator is validated against the declaration it decorates, as the pin resolves a decorator by name for each declaration kind (`checker/decorators.rs`). Every site accepts `@doc_hidden`, `@stable(since=…)`/`@stable(recursive=…)`, and `@deprecated` with one reason message or one `use=` symbol; a struct accepts `@fieldwise_init` and `@explicit_destroy("…")`; a `def` or method the inline levels, `@__parameter`/`@parameter`, and `@__unsafe_nested_origins_read_only`; a method `@staticmethod`, and an `__init__` `@implicit`. Malformed arguments get the pin's texts. A removed `@register_passable` or `@register_passable("trivial")` gets the pin's removal text, and any other name, `@value` included, is rejected as at the pin: "unsupported compiler decorator" when it names a declaration, "use of unknown declaration 'NAME'" otherwise (`conformance/fixtures/unmodeled_struct_decorator.mojo`). A second `@fieldwise_init` is rejected with the pin's text. The pin's decorator warnings (`@deprecated` at each use, `@parameter`'s deprecation) are not emitted (R1164), and `@align(n)` (R1161), `@unavailable` (R1162), `@export` (R936), and `@fieldwise_init("implicit")` (R1163), which the pin accepts, are unsupported. A decorator naming a checker intrinsic (`@print`) or a module constant is reported as an unknown declaration where the pin calls it an unsupported compiler decorator. |
| Expressions | Bitwise and shift operator precedence | Run | The infix levels follow Python and Mojo, loosest first: comparisons, `\|`, `^`, `&`, `<<`/`>>`, `+`/`-`, then `*` and its siblings, each left-associative. `a << 1 \| b >> 1` is `(a << 1) \| (b >> 1)`, `a & b + c` is `a & (b + c)`, and `a \| b == c` is `(a \| b) == c` (`assets/ok/bitwise_shift_precedence.mojo`). |
| Expressions | Contextual member references | Run | A leading-dot chain (`var c: Color = .red()`, `takes_color(.of(7))`, `[.red(), .of(3)]` under a `List[Color]` annotation, `return .red()`) resolves its base against the expected type: the parser builds the chain over the compiler-internal `$contextual` sentinel, the checker re-checks the spelled form with spans preserved, and HIR substitutes the base name. Without a contextual type the form rejects. A bare `.member` comptime value member (`takes_color(.red)` against `comptime red = Color(1)`) resolves the same way (`conformance/fixtures/contextual_member_value.mojo`). Recorded gaps: parametric statics, non-struct and generic expected types, type positions. |
| Functions | Escaping closures | Excluded | Current Mojo does not support closures that outlive their enclosing scope; this is not a parity target. |
| Types | Scalars, strings, nominal collections, SIMD and slices | Run | Public `List`, `Set`, `Dict`, heterogeneous `Tuple`, and fixed-size `Array[T, length]` are self-hosted nominal structs (`List[T: AnyType]` matches the head's relaxed bound: the moving APIs — `append`/`insert`/`pop`/`grow`/`reverse`/`remove`/`__setitem__`/literal construction/owned iteration — state their own `Movable` requirement, so an empty `List` of a pinned element type is legal; growth 2026-08: capacity/length-fill constructors, `reserve`/`resize`/`shrink`/`swap_elements`/`capacity`, `unsafe_get`/`unsafe_set`, upstream's `List(unsafe_uninit_length=n)` and `resize(unsafe_uninit_length=n)` (the storage is owned at full length while the caller initializes every slot through raw pointer stores; the VM keeps a never-written heap slot distinct from a taken one — reading it traps, taking it yields a tombstone that traps at its first variable use, destroying it is a no-op, and a taken slot still traps — while native storage is raw bytes as upstream; the builtins `len`/`print` read a named struct place in place rather than copying it), equality, `__bool__`, `+`/`+=` concatenation and `*`/`*=` repetition, Optional-returning `try_index`, raising `index`, and upstream's consuming `extend(var other)`). `Dict[K: Hashable & Equatable & Movable, V: Movable, H: Hasher = default_hasher]` (upstream's Movable-only `KeyElement` bounds; `Copyable`/`Equatable`/`Hashable`/`Iterable`/`Writable` are conditional on copyable keys and values, `copy`/`get`/`fromkeys`/`update`/`|`/`==`/`__hash__`/the borrowing views carry per-API `where conforms_to(K, Copyable)` guards where upstream launders the key bound with `downcast`, and a violated struct-parameter bound at construction names the bound) is hash-bucketed (each entry caches its `UInt64` hash under `H`) over dense insertion-ordered entries (doubling at load factor one) with pop/LIFO popitem/setdefault/update/clear/`|` merge/order-insensitive equality/`__bool__`/capacity construction, and `keys`/`values`/`items` return self-iterable, non-indexable borrowing views; `Set[T: Hashable & Equatable & Movable, H: Hasher = default_hasher]` (Movable-only elements; the copying set algebra, comparisons, hashing, printing, and borrowed iteration are `where conforms_to(T, Copyable)`-guarded; the list-backed set hashes its elements only in its own `__hash__`, through the default hasher as upstream; `H` is signature parity) adds remove/discard/LIFO pop/clear/update, the union/intersection/difference/symmetric_difference families with `&`/`|`/`-`/`^` operators and subset/superset comparisons, read-only element yields, and explicit variadic construction (`Set(a, b, c)` selects the same marker-defaulted literal constructor as `{a, b, c}` and infers the element type — a zero-argument `Set[T]()` prefers the empty constructor over the variadic one, the shared overload rank upstream uses; a generic variadic constructor's overflow arguments are type-checked against and infer the element parameter), and `range(...)` returns current Mojo's private range family — `_ZeroStartingRange`/`_SequentialRange`/`_StridedRange[dtype: DType = DType.int]`, each its own borrowed typed-raising iterator over `Scalar[dtype]` elements, with upstream's construction rules (a negative zero-start end clamps empty, the two-argument form never counts down, a zero step canonicalizes to the empty range). Int arguments select the stdlib overloads; scalar arguments infer the dtype through checker-selected specialization (upstream's infer-only `range[dtype: DType, //]` overloads have no explicit spelling), rejecting mixed dtypes and Bool. A float dtype takes only the strided form, `_FloatStridedRange`, which iterates by index as upstream does: element `k` is the fused multiply-add `k * step + start` (a `__fma__` intrinsic at the lane's precision on both backends) over `ceil((end - start) / step)` elements, none for a zero step (1/2-argument float ranges need a step, with upstream's text), including `Float16` ranges. Length and Int subscripts run; `reversed()`/`bounds()`/`__has_next__`, containment, formatting, non-Int `Indexer` arguments, and `comptime for` over scalar ranges are recorded subset gaps. `Array` (prelude-exported, `UnsafePointer`-backed) declares conditional `Copyable`/`Movable`/`Deinitable`/`Equatable`/lexicographic `Comparable`/`Iterable`/`IterableOwned`/`Writable` conformances over its `T: AnyType` element and an `Int` value parameter; it supports keyword `fill`/`copy:`/`deinit move:` construction and the checker-driven variadic literal constructor, by-reference `__getitem__` (no `__setitem__`, matching upstream Array), `__len__`/`__eq__`/`__ne__`/`__contains__`, and borrowed plus owned iteration, and is `Defaultable` when `T` is (upstream's conditional conformance: `Array[Int, 3]()` default-constructs every element through `Self.T()`, the generic type-parameter construction a `Defaultable`-bounded binder reifies at the call — directly or through a `make[T: Defaultable]()` bound), but not `ImplicitlyCopyable` (consuming `concat`/`repeat` need dependent result lengths — recorded mojo-only gaps) (`Hashable`, `unsafe_ptr`, `__getitem_param__`, `uninitialized:` construction, and `deinit_with` are not yet provided). Their supported indexing, sizing, and containment use ordinary checked methods; borrowed List/Set/Dict/range-family iteration and owned List/Set iteration use selected nominal iterator methods (`Optional` is prelude-exported like upstream; `Set` stays prelude-visible — upstream requires an explicit import for the name, a recorded divergence — while set displays work without the name on both sides). Public Tuple has no runtime iteration contract and never falls through to the private runtime-pack iterator path. Concrete List/Set/Dict-place borrowed iteration retains an origin-preserving owner loan, re-established on a reference-yielding loop binding so an invalidation names the user's variable; List and Set support `for ref` element write-through via the generic protocol. A `for ref` loop lends the source's elements mutably, so it is exclusive against a live whole-place view of the source (`var view = Span(xs)` then `for ref x in xs: x += 1` rejects while `view` lives, as `xs.append(4)` does; a value loop coexists with the view, and nested `for ref` loops over one list stay generation-tracked). Dict lookup refreshes its exact `value` owned-interior generation without invalidating the sibling `element` generation retained by key iteration. The bundled owned protocol uses current Mojo's monomorphic `IteratorOwnedType`; the borrowed trait uses current Mojo's origin-parameterized `IteratorType[iterable_mut: Bool, //, iterable_origin: Origin[mut=iterable_mut]]` with `__iter__(ref self) -> Self.IteratorType[origin_of(self)]`, while the bundled member templates stay origin-erased: every bundled borrowed iterator (`_ListIter` for List/Set, `_SpanIter` for Span, `_DictKeyIter` for Dict/StringDict) carries its origin as an erased struct parameter, borrows its source through a `ref` field, iterates itself (a parameterized `IteratorType` alias plus `__iter__(ref self)`, so a stored `arr.__iter__()`/`span.__iter__()` drives a loop, resumes after an explicit `__next__`, and lends its source view itself beside the storage the view borrows — a method returning a borrowing view keeps a named view receiver alive, and one called on a container element (`ys[0].rstrip()`, `ps[0].name.rstrip()`, `d["k"].rstrip()`) lends that element's owned interior — sibling element reads and views coexist with it, and an `append`, a store, or an in-place update (`ys[0] += "tail"`) over the element under the live view is rejected as an invalidated interior reference naming the interior the method's return origin names (`ys["element"]["bytes"]` for `rstrip`), while a view whose origin is the element itself (`ys[0].codepoints()`) lends the whole subtree below it (`assets/ownership_error/list_element_view_stale_after_append.mojo`; a store over the element under such an iterator is rejected where upstream reads the destroyed bytes, `docs/non-goals.md`; a second `Dict` lookup is rejected more strictly than upstream, roadmap R115; an in-place `ys[0] += …` under a live view is not yet rejected, roadmap R23); a struct's own value parameter spells `Self.n` inside its aliases and bracket arguments, upstream's required form: the bare spelling reports `unqualified access to struct parameter 'n'; use 'Self.n' instead`, as does a bare `T` field type, and `Self.<field>` in a bracket slot reports `cannot access instance field '<field>' without an instance of '<Struct[params]>'`), and declares its yielded reference as `ref[iterable_origin._get_owned_interior["element"]]` — the checker derives each source loan's granularity from that declared projection instead of a collection-name whitelist, so any conformer iterating at interior granularity gets the same protection.
