Any `mut self` mapping method during iteration lazily invalidates the iteration generation (mapping mutation during iteration is a rejected error, matching Mojo's documented contract), while `d[key]` value reads refresh only the sibling `value` generation; `keys`/`values`/`items` views borrow the live entries (source mutation while a view lives is rejected; value/entry yields are read-only, a conservative subset of upstream's mut-following value references), `_DictKeyIter`/`_DictValueIter` are upstream-shape adapters wrapping a `_DictEntryIter` field (a monomorphic comptime alias / a direct origin application in field position) whose stepping uses upstream's expression-origin spelling (`-> ref [self.iter.__next__().key] Self.K`), and `take_items` drains lazily through a mutably borrowing iterator. An ordinary value read through a reference-returning subscript requires a `Copyable` referent and runs that referent's copy lifecycle; an explicit `ref` binding retains the alias and remains legal for linear referents. Consuming a projected Copyable field similarly runs its nested copy lifecycle. Slice syntax preserves omitted, negative, and strided bounds and binds a subscript parameter as upstream's `__slice_literal__` constructors do: a literal with a step value is a `StridedSlice`, one without (`a[1:3]`, `a[::]`, `a[1:3:]`) a `ContiguousSlice`, and either binds a `Slice` parameter as a `Slice` (`conformance/fixtures/slice_descriptor_kinds.mojo`). `Slice` exposes `start`, `end`, and `step`, `ContiguousSlice` `start` and `end`, and `StridedSlice` no field (upstream's is the private `_inner`), each with `indices()` (`ContiguousSlice.indices(length)` is upstream's two-element `(start, end)`, the strided family's is three); and dispatches checked mixed/variadic user `__getitem__` and `__setitem__`. A `ContiguousSlice` or `StridedSlice` value does not convert to `Slice`, as at the pin (`conformance/fixtures/slice_kind_does_not_widen.mojo`). The descriptors are compiler-intrinsic types with current Mojo's protocol surface: `Slice` is `Equatable` (`==`/`!=` compare the three bounds; the two sub-kinds are not comparable, as upstream), every kind is `Writable` as `Slice(start, end, step)` with `None` for an omitted bound (upstream's delegating `write_to`), explicit `Slice(a, b)`, `Slice(a, b, c)`, and `slice(...)` construction takes `Int` or `None` arguments, and the two kinds are visible only where `from std.builtin.builtin_slice import ContiguousSlice, StridedSlice` binds them, as the pin's prelude exports `Slice` and `slice` alone (`conformance/fixtures/slice_kind_names_need_import.mojo`). The kinds stay compiler intrinsics rather than upstream's library structs, so `ContiguousSlice(1, 3, None)`, `StridedSlice(1, 5, 2)`, and `var t: StridedSlice = Slice(1, 4)` are rejected (R1157), and so is a `None` slice bound or step, `x[1:5:None]` (R1159). List's contiguous slice overload returns a `Span` borrowing the list's owned elements (origin `origin_of(xs)._get_owned_interior["element"]`), as the pin's does: a store through it shows in the list, it outlives no structural mutation of the list, and a call reading it cannot construct its result over the list (`xs = rebuild(xs[0:1])` rejects with the pin's aliasing text). It is strict (current Mojo bounds): a negative, out-of-range, or reversed bound aborts through the uncatchable `os.abort` trap instead of normalizing (with the audited head's interpolated start/end/reversed messages), while strided List slicing keeps `StridedSlice.indices()` normalization and the copied result through the `StridedSlice` overload, which a `Slice`-typed descriptor value also selects (the checked analogue of upstream's implicit `Slice -> StridedSlice` conversion); user-defined slice descriptors keep their own semantics. `Slice(...)` bounds are `Int`, `None`, or `Optional[Int]` values (the descriptor's own field type; both backends read the nominal slot at construction), and the explicit `s.write_to(writer)`/`s.write_repr_to(writer)` spellings write `Slice(1, 4, None)` and upstream's `Slice(start=1, end=4, step=None)` like printing, `String(x)`, `Writer.write`, and `repr(s)` do. `Span(list)` (prelude-exported, `std.span`) is a borrowed contiguous view — a multi-element origin-bearing pointer plus length whose construction lends the List's place (shared loan) so structural mutation conflicts while any view lives; element access is a reference result (write-through works on a mutable source) that outlives the span it was read through, contiguous sub-slices are strict sub-views of the same storage, and strided Span slicing selects no overload. Span is `Boolable`, `Sized`, and `Writable where conforms_to(T, Writable)` (printing `[a, b]` like `List`), with `==`/`!=` against a span of any origin and `in` over `Equatable` elements; its `repr` is the reflective default, not upstream's `Span[mut=…, T]([…])`, because `Self.mut` is unreadable (R761). Span's constructor is `@implicit` (current Mojo's temporary-origin inference): a `List` passes directly wherever a `Span` is expected — free-call and method arguments, annotated bindings, and returns — the conversion result borrowing the source list exactly like the explicit construction (a hidden retained slot keeps the source alive across the consuming expression, structural mutation while the converted view lives conflicts, and returning a view of a frame-local list rejects with the same `escapes storage` family). A bare list literal does not convert (it types as fixed-size `Array`, a recorded subset gap — bind it to a `List` first). Span is `Iterable where conforms_to(T, Copyable)` through the same origin-parameterized protocol as List: `for x in span` reads element values, `for ref x` writes through to the underlying List, a sub-slice view iterates its own window, and structural mutation of the source during iteration conflicts with the live view (`_SpanIter` borrows the span through a `ref` field and yields `ref[iterable_origin._get_owned_interior["element"]]` references; reading the source between a mutable span loop and a later use of the span keeps the recorded order-sensitive over-rejection). `List.unsafe_ptr()` mints the underlying interior-generation pointer directly (offsets legal, staled by mutation); `Pointer(to=x)` stays single-place, and `unsafe_origin_cast` rebinds pointer provenance: an immutable pointer cast to a mutable origin is accepted and reads through, but keeps its immutable capability, so a write through the result rejects as at the pin. A named bracket argument over a lowercase (value) base is a keyword subscript (`x[byte=i]`) dispatching keyword-only `__getitem__` overloads through ordinary structural call binding (a nominal keyword subscript is read-only — assignment is rejected; the pointer keyword subscript `p[unsafe_offset=i]` is a place instead; named brackets over a type name remain compile-time parameter application), and keyword-only parameter names are part of overload identity — two same-arity overloads may differ only by their keyword selectors. A named bracket argument whose value is a slice (`x[byte=a:b]`, omitted bounds preserved, explicit second colon selecting `StridedSlice`) is a keyword slice binding a keyword-only slice-descriptor parameter through the same structural call binding, read-only like every nominal keyword subscript. Every method-dispatched nominal subscript uses ordinary checked method-selection semantics and retains a complete `MirSubscriptCall`: exact target, executable result, and raising type; receiver/argument conventions and caller places; capture effects; source-ordered compile-time value arguments; reference-result origin; and setter write-back. Call-less `Index` and `Slice` name an exact intrinsic dispatch family, so the VM never selects semantics from a runtime value; the narrow nominally typed `Slice.indices()` result explicitly uses the private Tuple-storage bridge. A reference-returning subscript is evaluated once, retains its owner loans in a hidden reference slot, and can be used directly as a chained receiver/place, through a reference-valued aggregate, or as a dynamic `mut`/`ref` actual. A `Pointer` element of a `Tuple` or `List` keeps its handle: indexing designates the stored pointer, so `t[1][] += 2` over `(Pointer(to=left), Pointer(to=right))` writes `right` and reads back as at the pin, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/tuple_pointer_element_write.mojo`, `assets/ok/pointer_tuple_field_write.mojo`). A `ref` type argument (`Tuple[ref[o] Int]`, `List[ref[o] Int]`) is rejected at parse with the pin's "expected ']' in call argument list" (`assets/parse_error/ref_type_argument_rejected.mojo`); the reference-field extension is the direct field declaration alone. A store into storage that outlives the frame — a field of `self` or a parameter-rooted place — rejects a frame-locally rooted loan (including rebinding a `ref` field to a local place) with the same `escapes storage` family as the return boundary; parameter-rooted loans store outward freely. Assignment through a `ref`-typed field writes through the stored handle into the referent (the store twin of the read path's second dereference), values that outlive a frame re-root their interior reference handles, and a bare struct name as an explicit compile-time argument (`List[RefBox]()`) is erased from runtime argument emission like an origin argument. Setter selection distinguishes positional and keyword-only value shapes, validates the actual right-hand side, and preserves receiver/index/right-hand-side evaluation order; raising getters/setters and mutable alias conflicts follow ordinary call and loan rules. Plain subscript assignment on a receiver with no `__setitem__` writes through a mutable-reference-returning `__getitem__` (upstream Array's contract), finishing with a direct reference write; an immutable receiver or immutable-reference getter rejects, and slice/multi-index assignment keeps the setter requirement. The self-hosted nominal `String` (a UTF-8 byte buffer over `UnsafePointer[Byte]`, prelude-exported, with the 2026-08 stabilizations: the bare `String()` empty constructor — `String` conforms to `Defaultable`, so a `T: Defaultable` bound accepts it and `Tuple[String, Int]()` default-constructs (`assets/ok/string_default_construction.mojo`) — pre-sized `String(capacity_bytes=…)`, `String(unsafe_uninit_length=…)` with `resize(unsafe_uninit_length=…)` and `unsafe_ptr_mut()` over the same never-written byte slots, and `reserve_bytes`) is the meaning of every source `String` annotation — the type-level `StringLiteral`/`String` split is complete, and the two are distinct overload keys: `def f(x: StringLiteral)` beside `def f(x: String)` is legal and a literal argument selects the `StringLiteral` overload (exact matches rank above conversions). String literals and literal-only operations stay on the compile-time `StringLiteral` type (spellable in annotations), an un-annotated `var s = "lit"` binding materializes the nominal String as in current Mojo (aggregate elements, `comptime` bindings, and bare literal expressions stay literal; seams without the linked stdlib struct keep the literal default), and a literal converts wherever the nominal String is expected through the `@implicit` literal constructor — bindings, arguments, returns, collection/tuple elements, fieldwise and pack-constructor fields, and mixed operator operands (`"a" + s`, `s == "b"` dispatch the struct's dunders). `String(x)` stringify, `input()`, `repr(x)`, and `.format(...)` produce the nominal String; `Error(msg)`/`raise` and Writer `write_string` accept either spelling. Overload symbols keep the stable `String` spelling for both types, so overloads differing only in that pair are rejected as redeclarations. The struct materializes explicitly from a literal (`String("...")`, the literal-to-struct bridge), and supports migrated literal operations — `+` concatenation, `+=`, `in` membership — plus `byte_length()` (`len(s)` on a `String`, `StringSpan`, or literal rejects with upstream's `@unavailable` text: name the unit — `byte_length()`, `len(s.bytes())`, `len(s.codepoints())`, `len(s.graphemes())`), copy/move/drop, byte-wise `==`/ordering (`Comparable`), hasher-protocol `__hash__` over the UTF-8 bytes (`StringSpan` likewise; values match current Mojo), Writable `print`/`repr` through the struct-to-literal bridge, explicit keyword-indexed access — `s[byte=i]` (a raw `Byte`), `s[codepoint=i]` (pure leading-byte UTF-8 decode yielding a prelude-exported `Codepoint`, with `count_codepoints()`), and `s[grapheme=i]` (the extended grapheme cluster as a `String` substring, with `count_graphemes()`) — and strict `byte=`/`codepoint=` keyword slices returning borrowed `StringSpan` views of the same buffer: negative, out-of-range, or reversed bounds abort, byte endpoints must fall on UTF-8 codepoint boundaries, and positional String slicing (contiguous or strided, like bare positional `s[i]`) rejects with a keyword-slice hint; StringLiteral positional slicing was removed with it (the former builtin literal-slice intrinsic is retired). The prelude-exported `StringSpan` (upstream's `StringSlice` stays accepted as a never-emitted compatibility alias) carries the keyword indexing trio, byte/codepoint/grapheme keyword slices as strict sub-views (String itself has no grapheme slice), `to_string()`, and Writable formatting; a live view holds a shared loan on its source, so mutation conflicts while any view lives, while reads and further views through `ref self` accessors coexist with it (a `ref self` method returning a value classifies as a read of its receiver); an origin argument in a local's annotation (`var v: Span[Int, origin_of(xs)] = xs`, `StringSpan[ImmStaticOrigin]`) is a demand the initializer and every later assignment (`v = ys`) must satisfy (a local place cannot satisfy `ImmStaticOrigin`; a value borrowing only another place is rejected). A literal converts to `StringSpan` — and `StringSpan("…")` constructs directly — through the compiler-replaced `@implicit` `StringLiteral` constructor (upstream's `StaticString` initializer with the origin erased; the bytes live for the whole program, so the view carries no loan), and the result APIs (search, affix tests, replace, split, case, predicates, justification, strip) live on the view with `StringSpan` needle/separator/fill parameters while `String` forwards through `StringSpan(self)`, upstream's shape: a view, a String, or a literal argument converts, and a String *temporary* argument or binding source (`f(String("abc"))`, `var v: StringSpan = make()`) materializes in a hidden owned slot the view borrows for as long as the view lives (returning such a view rejects as an escaping frame-local reference). Ordinary String, StringSpan, and StringLiteral iteration yields borrowed grapheme-cluster StringSpan views (a literal iterable converts to the nominal String temporary). Result APIs are byte-offset library code with upstream's signatures (2026-09 batch 1): `find`/`rfind(substr, start=0)` (`-1` when absent; a negative `start` counts from the end and clamps; the empty needle matches at 0 / the byte length), `count`, `startswith`/`endswith(affix, start=0, end=-1)`, `replace` (an empty `old` interleaves before every codepoint), `join[T: Copyable & Writable](elems: Span[T, _])` (a `List[T]` argument solves `T` and converts through Span's implicit constructor), upstream's `split(sep)`/`split(sep, maxsplit)` overloads returning eager owned `List[String]` pieces (the empty separator yields an empty piece, every codepoint, and an empty piece, as upstream), whitespace `split()`/`split(maxsplit=)` over Python-space codepoints, `splitlines(keepends)` over the universal newline set (`\r\n` is one boundary), `Boolable` (`Bool(s)`), `__mul__`, and String as a `Writer` (`s.write(a, b, …)` appends). The strip family (`strip`/`lstrip`/`rstrip`, POSIX-space default or a `chars` codepoint set) and `removeprefix`/`removesuffix` return borrowed `StringSpan` views at upstream's `origin_of(self)._get_owned_interior["bytes"]` origin (as do the `byte=`/`codepoint=` keyword slices): a call assigned straight back over such a view of its own destination (`s = takes(s.rstrip())`, the receiver and a named view included) is rejected with upstream's "aliasing values passed immutably to … argument and constructed as a result in … call", while a plain `StringSpan(s)` view is not; a nominal `__setitem__` store is judged the same way by the values it passes (`xs[0] = keep(xs[1].rstrip())` rejects, `xs[0] = xs[1].copy()` does not). `String.__iadd__` takes a `StringSpan`, as upstream's `StringSlice` overload, so `s += t.rstrip()` appends a view in place, and a `mut self` call passing a view of its own receiver (`s += s.rstrip()`) is rejected with upstream's "aliasing values passed mutably to 'self' argument and passed immutably to … argument". `StringSpan` is prelude-visible, `Boolable`, `Equatable` against another view or an owned `String` (upstream's two `__eq__` overloads; the operator selects a same-arity dunder overload by the right operand's type, converting a literal, and records the exact symbol for the backends; `!=` is declared for views only, as upstream), supports `in`, and carries the strip/affix family as sub-views. Batch 2 (2026-09): `upper`/`lower` over upstream's full Unicode 16 simple and SpecialCasing tables (`ß` → `SS`, `ﬃ` → `FFI`, titlecase digraphs map both ways), generated from the pinned upstream lookups into `stdlib/std/_string_tables.mojo` by `scripts/gen-string-tables` as fixed-width hex records that the view binary-searches at runtime, `isupper`/`islower` (at least one cased character and none of the other case), `isspace` (non-empty, all Python-space codepoints), `is_ascii_digit`, `is_ascii_printable`, byte-width `ascii_rjust`/`ascii_ljust`/`ascii_center(width, fillchar=" ")` (a multi-byte fill character aborts; a `String` or `StringSpan` parameter's literal default lowers natively), the non-raising `count_codepoints`/`count_graphemes` (the pre-rename `codepoint_count`/`grapheme_count` are gone; every codepoint and grapheme scan runs on the view without copying, and a buffer that is not valid UTF-8 counts lead bytes instead of raising), and `Codepoint.is_ascii_digit`/`is_ascii_upper`/`is_ascii_lower`/`is_ascii_printable`/`is_posix_space`/`is_python_space`. Batch 3 (2026-09): `as_bytes(ref self) -> Span[Byte, origin_of(self)]` (borrowed; `Span` gained upstream's pointer-backed `Span(unsafe_ptr=, length=)` constructor over an untracked pointer, which `StringSpan.__hash__` now feeds to `_update_with_bytes` without copying), `unsafe_ptr()`, `capacity_bytes()`, `resize(length, fill_byte=0)` (grows with an ASCII fill byte; a shrink must land on a codepoint boundary and a non-ASCII fill byte aborts, upstream's assertion texts), `append(codepoint)`, the raising `__int__`/`__float__` (so `Int(s)`/`Float64(s)` are raising calls), the prelude free functions `atol(str, base=10)` (Python's literal rules: POSIX-space padding, sign, `_` separators, base prefixes with base 0 detection, the `Int` range, upstream's error texts) and `atof(str)` (upstream's `_parsing_numbers`: POSIX-space strip, a `+` prefix and `f`/`F` suffix, `inf`/`infinity`/`nan`, then Clinger's fast path or Eisel-Lemire over the generated power-of-five table — correctly rounded, with upstream's 24-digit and `UInt64` significand limits and error texts; `nan` prints `nan`), and the `codepoints()`/`codepoint_slices()`/`graphemes()` iterators (Sized, usable directly in `for`) on `String` and `StringSpan`, plus `codepoint_slices_reversed()`/`graphemes_reversed()`/`__reversed__` (`__reversed__` walks the grapheme clusters back to front; as upstream, neither type conforms to `ReversibleRange`, so the free `reversed(s)` rejects them from a cached CR/LF/Control boundary; a CR LF, ZWJ sequence, or regional-indicator pair stays whole), `bytes()`, `peek_next()` on the codepoint iterators, and `split_at_grapheme(n)` (a `Tuple` of the views before and after the boundary that destructures like any tuple, immutable and keeping the receiver's provenance as upstream's `ImmOrigin(Self.origin)` views are). Recorded gaps: `String == StringSpan` (spell `view == string`; the String struct keeps one `__eq__` so its trait-dispatch symbol stays unique), `isspace`'s `single_character` parameter (a value-parameterized method on the origin-parameterized view is not specialized, and an explicit method value-parameter binding fails MIR verification on any struct), a second byte-slice view of a String while another of its views is live (`s[byte=1:3]` beside a live `view` of `s` conflicts: the parametric `ref self` subscript receiver counts as a mutable access), `__radd__` (the checker has no reflected-operator fallback), the `next(it)` builtin, `Pointer`'s `[unsafe_offset=]` keyword subscript (upstream deprecates the positional form), and `Span(unsafe_ptr=)` over an origin-bearing pointer (Mojito's Pointer parameters bind only exact origins; the constructor takes the untracked pointer). Repr vocabulary (2026-09): `_unqualified_type_name[T]()` (`std.reflection.type_info`, a checker-folded compile-time string spelling the checked type as current Mojo does: `SIMD[DType.int, 1]`, `Optional[String]`, `Tuple[...]`, a struct's bare name), `TypeNames[*Ts]()` (`std.format._utils`) writing a pack's names, and upstream's repr texts: `Int(7)`/`UInt(7)`/`Float64(2.5)`, a width-1 `SIMD` under its scalar alias (`Int8(3)`, `Float32(0.5)`) and a wider one as `SIMD[DType.int32, 2](1, 2)` (`assets/ok/repr_sized_scalars.mojo`), single-quoted Strings with backslash escapes for `\\`, `'`, newline, tab, and carriage return, the Tuple frame, and the Slice keyword bounds. `Optional`, `List`, `Dict`, and `Set` write their repr in upstream's text (`List[SIMD[DType.int, 1]]([Int(1), Int(2)])`, `Dict[String, SIMD[DType.int, 1]]({'a': Int(1)})`, `Optional[SIMD[DType.int, 1]](None)`; Set spells Mojito's key-less `AHasher` where upstream prints its keyed one; `Array` writes `Array[T, n]([...])` with its element parameter still spelled `T` — no instance clones for value-parameterized structs). `_unqualified_type_name` spells a minted value specialization's baked arguments at every nesting level (`Dict[String, SIMD[DType.int, 1], AHasher[[0, 0, 0, 0] : SIMD[DType.uint64, 4]]]`), a scalar value argument as `value : Type` (`Box[SIMD[DType.int, 1], 3 : SIMD[DType.int, 1]]`) and a `Bool` one bare (`Flag[True]`), as upstream; nested non-prelude structs stay unqualified where upstream keeps their module path (a recorded divergence), and `std.format._utils.FormatStruct` builds `Name[params](fields)` for user structs through upstream's fluent chain on a temporary (`FormatStruct(writer, "P").params(2, "tag").fields(self.x)`; `params` returns `ref[self] Self`, and a discarded reference result needs no copy), with `Named` writing `name=value` through a pointer to the value (its `o: Origin[mut=False]` parameter makes lending the value a shared read at the call and a shared loan after it, so several `Named` over one value coexist, bound or temporary, as upstream) (conformance/fixtures/generic_repr_texts.mojo, assets/ok/view_temporaries.mojo). A `write_to`-only struct keeps the field-wise default repr, as upstream. Recorded gaps: `Repr` and the free `write_to`/`write_repr_to` of `_utils`, a bounded `T: Writable` cannot call `x.write_to(w)`/`x.write_repr_to(w)` (spell `w.write(x)` / `w.write(repr(x))`), native `repr` of a `SIMD` vector wider than one lane (R275). `Codepoint` carries the decoded scalar plus the character's text captured through the struct-to-literal bridge: `Intable` (`Int(cp)`), scalar-ordered `Comparable`/`Equatable`, Writable as the character itself, `is_ascii()`, and `utf8_byte_length()`; the public scalar constructor is `Codepoint.from_u32(scalar)` (Int-based; `None` for negatives, surrogates, and values beyond U+10FFFF), whose character text is UTF-8-encoded in ordinary library code through runtime `Byte(Int)` conversions; upstream's `Codepoint(UInt8)` (so a literal byte, `Codepoint(103)`) and `Codepoint(unsafe_unchecked_codepoint=UInt32)` construct directly. Grapheme segmentation implements a documented UAX #29 subset: hand-maintained Control/Extend/SpacingMark essentials ranges plus regional indicators and fully arithmetic Hangul, with GB11 simplified to "never break after ZWJ" (no Extended_Pictographic data — common emoji ZWJ sequences join, and non-pictographic ZWJ sequences over-join) and GB9b (Prepend) omitted. Tuples include bare displays/destructuring, inferred or typed construction (a display or bare `Tuple(...)` call infers a string element as `String`, as upstream infers `Ts`, so `var t: Tuple[Int, String] = (1, "x")` and `= Tuple(1, "x")` bind, and an annotated binding, parameter, or field keeps `reverse`/`concat` — `assets/ok/tuple_annotated_binding.mojo`; a generic struct's `Tuple[Self.T, Int]` field reads and indexes as the closed `Tuple` at each instance — `assets/ok/generic_struct_tuple_field.mojo`; `reverse`, and `concat` over a `String` element of its receiver, run natively too — `assets/ok/tuple_template_served.mojo`; natively, a `String` element of `concat`'s argument is double-freed, roadmap R277), compile-time indexing, length (`Sized`), membership (`__contains__[T: Equatable]`), comparison, hasher-protocol `__hash__` (conditional `Hashable` — tuples serve as Dict keys and Set elements, and a tuple satisfies `Hashable`/`Equatable`/`Writable` generic bounds through its declared conditional conformances), concatenation, reversal, pack round-tripping, and dependent-handler `consume_elements` — whose handler element `var element: values.Ts[index]` (or the deprecated `element_types` spelling) is, as upstream, an opaque `Movable`-bounded linear value inside the handler (`len`/`print`/conversions reject; abandoning it or moving it into an abandoned local reports `abandoned without being explicitly destroyed`); `write_repr_to` writes upstream's `Tuple[<element types>](<element reprs>)` frame. Tuple lifecycle markers are conditional on every element; a `deinit` transform implicitly copies an `ImplicitlyCopyable` tuple but requires `^` for move-only elements (`assets/type_error/tuple_reverse_requires_transfer.mojo`). `reverse` and `concat` are declared in `std/builtin/tuple.mojo` with upstream's signatures (2026-10-05), where the checker typed both in Rust and the elaborator synthesized their bodies: `def reverse(deinit self, out result: Tuple[*Self.Ts.reverse()])` and `def concat[*OtherTs: Movable](deinit self, deinit other: Tuple[*OtherTs], out result: Tuple[*TypeList._concat[Self.Ts.values, OtherTs.values]()])`, so `pair.concat[Bool](Tuple(True))` binds the pack explicitly, a tuple literal operand infers it, and `reverse[Int]()` and `concat(3)` are rejected as at the pin (`assets/ok/tuple_reverse_concat.mojo`, `assets/type_error/tuple_reverse_explicit_parameter.mojo`, `tuple_concat_non_tuple.mojo`). Each body marks `result` initialized with the `__mlir_op` statement and writes each element through `Pointer(to=result[i]).unsafe_write(...)`; the element is transferred out of the private storage where upstream moves it through a rebound pointer (roadmap R343), and a view element (`Tuple(StringSpan(s), 1).reverse()`) loses its loan on the storage it views, so the VM stops with a use after deallocation where the pin prints (roadmap R355). A specialization keeps a transform only where a checked call asks for it, and a call that reaches a `Tuple` no specialization serves yet is typed against the declaration's shell. A transform on a `Tuple` over a pack still open runs too (`return t^.reverse()` in `def flip[*Ts: Movable](var t: Tuple[*Ts]) -> Tuple[*Ts.reverse()]`), as do a literal subscript of one (`other[0]`) and a static member on one, served by `Tuple`'s own `@staticmethod` (`Tuple[*Ts].__len__()`, also over `*Ts.reverse()`, a `TypeList._concat`, and a struct's `Self.Ts`, in a body or a `comptime` initializer). Indexed transfer of a non-`ImplicitlyCopyable` public tuple element stays rejected; `consume_elements` is the legal consuming API. `Int` and `Scalar[DType.int]` are one checked type; SIMD is value-level, not machine-vector code generation. The CPU-visible SIMD surface is semantically complete for the proof subset: `SIMDLength` is the width-parameter spelling (the removed `SIMDSize` alias rejects); explicit construction converts runtime scalars (integers wrap to any integer width and convert to float lanes, floats adjust precision, any `Intable` value constructs integer scalars through `__int__`, and `Scalar[DType.x](arg)` lowers as ordinary width-1 construction) while implicit contexts stay literal-exact; an exact literal splats implicitly into a multi-lane vector wherever one is expected — a field, an annotated binding, an assignment, an argument, a default, or a return, as upstream's implicit `SIMD(IntLiteral)`/`SIMD(FloatLiteral)` does (`var v: SIMD[DType.int32, 4] = 1`, `assets/ok/simd_literal_splat.mojo`), and a runtime scalar splats the same way into a vector of its own dtype, as upstream's implicit `SIMD.__init__(Scalar[dtype])` does (`var v: SIMD[DType.int32, 4] = Int32(9)`, an `Int` into a `DType.int` vector, a `Float64` into a `float64` one; `assets/ok/simd_scalar_splat.mojo`) — a scalar of another dtype and a `Bool` do not (`assets/type_error/simd_scalar_splat_dtype_mismatch.mojo`); unary negation, `cast[DType.target]()` (float→int truncates toward zero; bool casts deferred), bool-mask `select`, the mask splat `SIMD[DType.bool, N](fill=b)` (`Bool` is not a `Scalar`, so a multi-lane mask takes no positional `Bool` splat), the elementwise comparison methods `lt`/`le`/`gt`/`ge`/`eq`/`ne` (upstream's spelling, since its infix `<`/`<=`/`>`/`>=` are `Scalar`-only and its `==`/`!=` compare whole vectors; bool lanes order `False` below `True`; float lanes use the ordered predicates, so a NaN lane answers `ne` False too, while scalar `!=` stays IEEE), the `reduce_add/mul/min/max` and mask `reduce_and/or` reductions (collapsing to canonical native scalars), compile-time-mask `shuffle[*mask]()` (one index per receiver lane), and the width-changing gathers `slice[width, offset=o]()` (consecutive lanes) and `join(other)` (a same-typed vector appended, at twice the width) execute elementwise with bit-accurate wrapping; and a `def` may take its width as a `[w: SIMDLength]` value parameter — each call monomorphizes and an invalid bound width rejects during checked elaboration, with a MIR-verify backstop for assembled artifacts (struct-parameter widths stay deferred). A vector argument solves a `DType` binder and a `SIMDLength` width binder over `SIMD[dt, w]` together, a native scalar as a width-one vector and a caller's open `SIMD[dt, n]` forwarding its own slots, while an `Int` width binder in the slot is left unresolved and rejects, as in the pin; upstream's `v: SIMD[_, _]` parameter is exactly those two infer-only binders, on a `def`, a method, or a trait requirement (`assets/ok/simd_lane_binders_inferred.mojo`, `assets/type_error/simd_int_width_binder_not_inferred.mojo`). The native `Int` and `Float64` lack the lane reductions at such a binder (R335). `len(v)` answers a vector's lane count (`1` for a scalar alias and `Float64`) on both backends, and a `SIMD[dt, _]` local annotation takes its width from the initializer (`var v: SIMD[DType.int32, _] = SIMD[DType.int32, 4](...)` declares a four-lane vector; the element type must still match). The §6 owning family APIs run across the bundled containers: linear-capable `deinit_with(deinit self, handler)` on List, Array, Dict, Set, StringDict (and Tuple, where `deinit_with` is `consume_elements`' family spelling over the same dependent handler), `clear_with(mut self, handler)` on Dict and Set (drain-and-reuse, front-to-back like the audited head), and displacement-returning `insert` on Dict, Set, and StringDict (Dict/StringDict move the displaced ENTRY out as an `Optional[DictEntry]`, Set the displaced element — nothing is destroyed in place, so no `Deinitable` bound). Element handlers are `var`-convention `def(var …) capturing[_]` funargs (a `deinit` convention is only valid on struct method arguments) — a consumed receiver's write-back state feeds the caller's residual consumption, so drained containers do not double-free. Tuple `==`/`!=` (and the explicit comparison dunders) compare user-struct elements through the instance's methods; the comparison, `__len__`, and `__contains__` dunders are typed structurally on the canonical template. Tuple's static `__len__()` (`Tuple[Int, Int].__len__()`, upstream's `Self.__len__()`) coexists with the instance one: a receiver-overloaded method's static overload keys as its own symbol. `Tuple` is `Defaultable` when every element is — `Tuple[Int, Bool]()` prints `(0, False)`, and a `T: Defaultable` bound's `T()` reaches it — through a per-specialization nullary constructor that default-constructs each element (upstream's `Ts[i]()`; a `SIMD[d, w]` element defaults to zero lanes), so the variadic `*args: *Ts` constructor is now one overload beside it and every backend selects it by its runtime-pack collector rather than by arity. A `Tuple[...]` application resolves in every type-argument position — a nested element (`Tuple[Int, Tuple[Int, Bool]]()`), `Optional[Tuple[Int, Bool]]`, a `TypeNames` element, a `T: Defaultable` bound — and bare `Tuple(...)` calls nest without an annotation (`Tuple(Tuple(1, True), 2)`): a specialization key spells a minted Tuple element canonically, so the symbol is stable across passes. Integer lanes also carry `//` and `%` with upstream's integer-`SIMD` rule (floor semantics; a zero divisor yields a zero lane, no trap), so `UInt64` code spells division as upstream does; `UInt64` multiplication wraps mod 2^64 on both backends. An initializer list is current Mojo's (2026-10-05): `{}` and `{a, b}` at a contextual type other than `Set`/`Dict` are the construction `T()` / `T(a, b)` of that type (`var s: String = {}`, `take({1, 2})` with `take(var b: Box)`, `Pointer(to=x).unsafe_write({})`, `var p: T = {}` over a `Defaultable` binder, `var t: Tuple[Int, Bool] = {}`; `assets/ok/initializer_list_contextual.mojo`), which the checker spells and checks under an identity derived from the brace's and the checked arena hands HIR in the brace's place (`SemanticAdjustment::InitializerList`); a brace with no contextual type reports upstream's `cannot emit initializer list without a contextual type`. A nullary `Tuple[T0, ...]()` is the default construction. Not accepted: keyword entries (`{a, kw=1}`) and a `comptime` binding to `{}` (R342). |
| Types | `Optional[T]` owning container | Run | Self-hosted in `std.collections.optional` over one owned heap slot with current Mojo's owning-container surface: `T: AnyType` with declared `Boolable`/`Defaultable` and conditional `Copyable`/`Movable`/`Deinitable`/`Equatable`/`Hashable`/`Writable`/`Iterable`/`IterableOwned` conformances; keyword `init_with=` placement construction (a zero-parameter factory result lands directly in storage — no `Movable` requirement, including `Movable where False` payloads); upstream's `@implicit` value constructor (`Movable`; `var x: Optional[Int] = 5` and an `Optional[Int]` argument accept a bare value — an `@implicit` constructor may take its argument by `var`) and the `copy:`/`deinit move:` lifecycle constructors; an `@implicit` `NoneType` constructor so bare `None` coerces to the empty `Optional[T]` in binding, argument, subscript-key (`table[None]` on a `Dict[Optional[Int], _]` is a subscript, not a parameter application), and parameter-default positions (the upstream `arg: Optional[T] = None` signature pattern — the parameter default-value check falls back to `@implicit` converting constructors, and an omitted-arg default materializes on the VM by running that constructor; native lowering of a converting-constructor default is a recorded gap, while an explicit `None` argument lowers natively); `__eq__`/`__ne__(rhs: Optional[T])` (both-empty equal, one-empty unequal), hasher-protocol `__hash__` feeding upstream's `UInt8` presence tag before the payload (Dict keys; native Dict-of-Optional is a recorded native residue), `write_to` printing the payload or `None`, `__bool__`, the identity operators `opt is None` / `opt is not None` through `__is__`/`__isnot__(self, other: NoneType)` (the two-word `is not` is one comparison-precedence operator; `not opt is None` reads as `not (opt is None)`, as in Python; scalars and structs without `__is__` reject), upstream's consuming `or_else(deinit self, var default: T)` (`Movable & Deinitable`; an `ImplicitlyCopyable` receiver place is implicitly copied through its copy lifecycle, a merely `Copyable` one needs `^`), the aborting interior-reference `value(ref self)` and its unchecked twin `unsafe_value`, the aborting `take(mut self)` (`Movable`) and unchecked `unsafe_take`, and `bounds()`; linear-capable `deinit_with(deinit self, handler)` with a `var`-convention handler (the handler consumes the payload — no lifecycle bound on the element) and the `deinit_assert_empty(deinit self)` named destructor for the empty case; consuming `map[U]`/`and_then[U]`; and borrowed iteration (value-yielding — Optional `for ref` is a recorded subset gap) plus owned iteration under the `Movable & Deinitable` bounds. The VM's intrinsic Slice-bound bridge constructs the nominal Optional through the constructor its read names: the bound read (`field.get`) names the nullary and positional one-argument instances in concrete MIR, so they live only as long as a body that reads a bound. A lambda return annotation naming a module-imported nominal type (`-> Optional[Int]` under `from std.collections.optional import Optional`) is a recorded lambda-resolution gap — spell the factory as a nested `def`. The raising empty subscript `opt[]` (upstream's `__getitem__(ref self) raises EmptyOptionalError[Self.T] -> ref Self.T`) reads and writes the payload (`opt[] += 1`) and raises `EmptyOptionalError[T]` — a `Writable` struct printing `EmptyOptionalError[SIMD[DType.int, 1]]()` for `Int`, as upstream — on the empty case; a nominal receiver's zero-parameter `__getitem__` is what the empty-subscript syntax dispatches to. Recorded subset gaps: `__eq__`/`__ne__(rhs: NoneType)` (`opt == None`; same-arity dunder overloads are selected by arity — use `opt is None`), `__invert__` (no `~`), and `copied`. `OptionalReg[T: TrivialRegisterPassable]` (prelude-exported, also `from std.collections import OptionalReg`) is the subset shape — `Boolable`/`Defaultable`/`TrivialRegisterPassable`, the `@implicit` value and `None` constructors, `is None`, `value`/`unsafe_value`/`or_else` — over an `Optional` payload (no `DevicePassable`/`get_type_name`); a struct declaring `TrivialRegisterPassable` counts as implicitly copyable. The `std.collections` package re-exports `List`/`Dict`/`Set`/`Optional`/`OptionalReg` for upstream's import spellings. |
| Types | `ParameterList` | Run | Self-hosted in `stdlib/std/builtin/variadics.mojo` and exported by the prelude: the zero-sized, `TrivialRegisterPassable` `ParameterList[type: AnyType, //, values: _MLIR.KGENParamListType[type]]` a homogeneous value pack is when read at run time, declared over upstream's one raw-list parameter: `_MLIR.KGENParamListType[T]` is upstream's `__mlir_type[`!kgen.param_list<`, T, `>`]` alias, the compile-time-only raw list `KGENParamList[T]`, whose element is bounded by upstream's `_MLIR.KGENTypeType` (`!kgen.type`). A pack's `vs.values` (a struct's own `Self.values.values`) is that list: `ParameterList[vs.values]()` reads it, `ParameterList[vs.values].size` sizes it, a `comptime raw = vs.values` binding applies as the list, and a user's `raw: _MLIR.KGENParamListType[t]` parameter (after `from std.builtin.variadics import _MLIR`) binds it and infers `t` (`assets/ok/parameter_list_raw_values.mojo`, `assets/ok/parameter_list_raw_parameter.mojo`). `ParameterList[1, 2]` and `ParameterList[*vs]` are rejected as at the pin (`assets/type_error/parameter_list_explicit_elements.mojo`, `assets/type_error/parameter_list_spread_pack.mojo`); a raw list read at run time (`var x = vs.values`, which the pin materializes) is rejected (roadmap R1149), and a struct parameter read through a value (`pl.values` of a `comptime pl`, a runtime `s.n`) is not supported (roadmap R1148), while one read through a type (`S[7].n`) is, with the compile-time `size` member (read through the value too, `vs.size`, as upstream's documentation spells `args.size`), `__len__` returning it, `get_span` (a `Span[type, ImmStaticOrigin]` over the elements), `__getitem__` returning `ref[ImmStaticOrigin]`, a `Copyable`-gated `__iter__` over `_ParameterListIter`, and upstream's conditional `Writable` conformance (`Writable where conforms_to(type, Writable)`): `write_to` writes the tuple-like `(1, 2, 3)`, so `print(vs)` and `String(vs)` work, and `write_repr_to` writes upstream's `ParameterList[<type>]((…))` text by hand rather than through `FormatStruct`'s callback overload (roadmap R813) (`assets/ok/value_pack_parameter_list_writable.mojo`; a pack of a non-`Writable` element is not `Writable`). The elements live in a constant array laid out once per program (`MirInstr::ParamListAddress`: a never-freed VM allocation, a private constant global natively), which `get_span` addresses through the bundled module's private `__param_list_address[values]()` where upstream calls `global_constant` (roadmap R492). `__iter__` returns upstream's `_ParameterListIter[*ParameterList[Self.values]()]` without its `rebind`/`downcast` laundering (roadmap R223) and constructs it as `(0)` rather than `{0}` (roadmap R495). `of` and `empty_of` are upstream's aliases (`comptime of[type: AnyType, //, *values: type] = ParameterList[type=type, values.values]`, `comptime empty_of[type: AnyType] = Self.of[type=type]`), read through the unbound `ParameterList`: `type` is inferred from the values, the type a literal materializes to (`Int` for `ParameterList.of[4, 5, 6]`), or given by keyword (`of[type=Int8, 1, 2]`), a read through either is a type (`Ints.size` of `comptime Ints = ParameterList.of[4, 5, 6]`), a construction through either (`ParameterList.of[4, 5, 6]()`, `Ints()`, `ParameterList.empty_of[Int]()`) is a `ParameterList` value the elaborator spells as the struct's own construction, and a closed list's `.values` is a raw list a module or local `comptime` binds (`comptime raw: _MLIR.KGENParamListType[Int] = ParameterList.of[7, 8].values`) and `ParameterList[raw]` takes, `!kgen.string` elements included, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/parameter_list_of_alias.mojo`); a `String` element type (`ParameterList.of["foo", "bar"]`) is rejected (roadmap R448), and `comptime n = ParameterList[c].size` in a compile-time expression stops at the elaborator's positional binding (roadmap R1175). A struct's parameter reads through a type in a compile-time expression too (`comptime c = S[7].n`; `assets/ok/struct_parameter_comptime_read.mojo`). `__getitem_param__` (roadmap R800), `reduce`/`any`/`all`/`contains` (roadmap R810), `tabulate`/`splat`/`_concat` (roadmap R811), and `map_to_type` (roadmap R812) are not ported. Inside the bundled struct alone, `len(Self.values)` is the raw list's length, the stand-in for upstream's `#kgen.param_list.size`. A value pack's length is this `size` in every position (a parameter-position `len(vs)` is a compile-time application of `__len__`), and a runtime subscript calls `__getitem__` even at a compile-time index, as upstream's docstring says; a compile-time subscript (`comptime e = vs[1]`, `Box[vs[0]]`) is still a checker-compiled list element rather than `__getitem_param__` (roadmap R800). Not yet: `len(vs)` in a signature type (`vs.size` works, roadmap R804), a `where` clause over it (roadmap R803), and a user struct's `comptime` member over `len(Self.values)` (roadmap R802) (`assets/ok/value_pack_parameter_list_members.mojo`). |
| Types | `DType` values | Run | `DType.<name>` is a runtime value of the builtin `DType` type as well as a `SIMD`/`Scalar` element type and a `[dtype: DType]` argument: it binds, passes, returns, defaults (`d: DType = DType.int`), and stores in struct fields, `List`, `Dict` keys, `Optional`, and tuples. `print`/`String(x)`/`write_to` write the name (`float32`) and `repr` the `DType.float32` spelling; `==`/`!=` are its only operators (ordering, `is`, and cross-type comparison reject); `hash` hashes upstream's one-byte code as a `UInt8`, so the value matches the pin. `is_integral`/`is_floating_point`/`is_signed`/`is_unsigned`/`is_numeric`/`is_float8`/`is_half_float` answer from upstream's code masks at run time and fold in `comptime if`; a `comptime` dtype binding, including a compile-time call returning a `DType`, keys `SIMD[c, n]` and `[dt: DType]` specializations. A runtime value cannot key a parameter list or a `comptime if`. A `SIMD` type or value names its lane dtype as `dtype` (`Int32.dtype`, `SIMD[DType.int16, 4].dtype`, `v.dtype`, `f().dtype`, which still runs `f`), folded to a constant at run time and at compile time (`comptime d = Int32.dtype`, `SIMD[Float32.dtype, 2]`); `Bool` has none, as upstream. `DType.mantissa_width`/`max_exponent`/`exponent_width`/`exponent_bias[dt]()` fold to the float format's `Int`, and a non-float dtype rejects with upstream's `dtype must be floating point`; over a `DType` binder (a keyed `def`'s, or a generic struct's `Self.dt` in a method) the query is the static method's application, which a template-served body carries and each instance answers, a non-float instance failing its instantiation with the same constraint, as at the pin (`assets/ok/dtype_float_query_template_served.mojo`). A query as a type argument (R332) or over a value's `dtype` (R333) still rejects. Only Mojito's thirteen dtypes exist: an upstream name without one (`uint`, `bfloat16`, `int128`, the `float8` family, …) rejects as unsupported, as does `UInt.dtype` (`docs/roadmap.md` R65). Natively a `DType` is an `i8` holding the code. |
| Indexing | Competing setter value shapes | Run | A `__setitem__` pair whose assignment value is positional in one overload and keyword-only in the other over the same index types is rejected at struct declaration, matching current Mojo; lone positional or keyword-only setters keep ordinary right-hand-side validation. |
| Indexing | Projected reference results | Run | A field below a nominal reference-returning subscript extends the accessor's one-evaluation hidden handle; subsequent Pointer, SIMD, and private-storage indices retain their typed dynamic projection. Direct `ref` bindings, dynamic `mut`/`ref` actuals, and reference-return forwarding preserve the selected call, projected origin, and caller place instead of becoming raw nominal storage projections. The field projection is shared differential evidence; the focused Pointer projection is Mojito-only until the current allocation/dereference spelling lands, while SIMD/private-storage steps currently have lowering/verifier rather than separate differential coverage. |
| Indexing | Augmented nominal subscripts | Run | `receiver[arguments] OP= rhs` evaluates receiver/index sources once and retains call-local conversions, effects, places, and complete accessor contracts. A value getter runs after the RHS, then the operator and independently adapted positional/keyword setter; a getter-mutated index place is reloaded before that setter. A mutable-reference getter establishes the lvalue before the RHS and writes through it directly with no setter. For a user-struct element, the operator step dispatches the element's dedicated in-place dunder (`__iadd__`, …) through both paths — a value getter materializes the element into a mutable temporary and sends the mutated result through `__setitem__`, a mutable-reference getter applies it through the handle — while a native scalar element keeps the binary read-modify-write. |
| Types | Collection displays and comprehensions | Run | Homogeneous list, set, and dictionary displays plus nested/filtering CPU comprehensions infer their element types, preserve key-before-value and clause evaluation order, and enforce hashing/ownership requirements. A string literal element or key materializes the nominal `String`, as upstream (`["a", "b"]` is `Array[String, 2]`, `{"a": 1}` is `Dict[String, Int]`). An uncontextualized list display materializes as fixed-size `Array[T, len]` through one nominal variadic literal-constructor call; an expected type with a list-literal constructor (notably `List[T]`) still controls contextual materialization, and assignment targets count as context (`self.items = [7, 8, 9]` stays `List[Int]` for a List-typed field). An uncontextualized display subscripted or method-called as a temporary (`[1, 2][0]`, `[1, 2].__getitem__(0)`) is materialized in a hidden slot the statement destroys, as a call result is (`assets/ok/array_display_temporary_subscript.mojo`). Comprehensions still produce `List`; contextual `List` displays keep the constructor-plus-`append` lowering, set displays and dictionary comprehensions lower to the nominal collection constructors plus ordinary `add`/`__setitem__` method calls, and a non-empty dictionary display lowers to `Dict`'s `__dict_literal__` constructor over two `List` literal constructions, as upstream (see the Destruction row). Comprehension `for` clauses share the statement loop's borrowed-source lowering: a named source — collection or user iterable — is reference-bound (borrowed, not copied) with its whole-place or interior `element` loan re-established on the distinct iterator-object slot, so the source stays usable after the comprehension and mutating it mid-comprehension is rejected; a borrowed temporary is retained in its own slot and destroyed exactly once, after the comprehension. A clause's target destructures each element as a `for` statement's does (see the Control flow row): a tuple target, nested and with `var`/`ref` leaves, binds the element to a hidden immutable binder whose extraction plan MIR lowers right after it, and a later clause or filter reads the leaves (`[a * 2 for a, _ in pairs]`, `{k: v for k, v in pairs}`, `[a + b for a, row in grouped for b in row]`: `assets/ok/comprehension_destructuring_targets.mojo`); a sequence or place target in a clause is rejected, because the comprehension lowers in MIR as an expression rather than as upstream's nest of `for` statements (R1227). A `comptime` binding of a set or dictionary display is a compile-time value (`CtValue::Dict`/`CtValue::Set`: insertion-ordered, structurally deduplicated, typed like the display under the default hasher, or as its annotation / the explicit literal constructor `Dict[K, V, default_comp_time_hasher](keys, values, None)`); the Comptime rows describe what reads it and how it crosses to runtime. There are no collection-construction MIR opcodes or VM-native public collection values. `TyKind::ComptimeList`/`Value::ComptimeList` are restricted to CTFE; `TyKind::Tuple`/`Value::Tuple` and `MakeTuple` are restricted to compiler-private heterogeneous pack storage. |
| Types | `std.utils.Variant` tagged unions | Run | Self-hosted in `stdlib/std/utils/variant.mojo` in upstream's shape: `struct Variant[*Ts: AnyType]` over the compiler-private `__VariantStorage[*Ts]` storage primitive (the intrinsic tagged union, reachable only from bundled sources), with the conditional conformances `Copyable`/`ImplicitlyCopyable`/`Movable`/`Deinitable`/`Equatable`/`Hashable`/`Writable where Ts.all_conforms_to[..]()`. Construction (`@implicit` positional — a string literal payload materializes to the `String` alternative — and the `init_with=factory` placement constructor whose zero-parameter factory's result selects the alternative), `isa[T]()`, static `is_type_supported[T]()` (on the parameterized type or through a value), the projection `v[T]` (the type-keyed `__getitem_param__[T](ref self)` reference, a place in every position: `v[Int] += 5`, `v[List[Int]][0] = 9`, `ref r = v[T]`; writing `v[T] = x` replaces the payload, so references into the previous payload's interior expire), `unsafe_get[T]()`, `set[T](value)` and `set(init_with=factory)` (available only when every alternative is `Deinitable`; a call on another Variant reports the failed availability clause), consuming `unwrap[T]()`/`unsafe_unwrap[T]()` (a plain-local receiver without `^` is an implicit copy, legal only when the Variant is `ImplicitlyCopyable`, as upstream), `replace[Tin, Tout](value)`/`unsafe_replace`, and the linear-capable consuming `deinit_with(handler)`. The template serves every instance and every call, as upstream elaborates a struct generator: each storage operation selects the alternative upstream's `_get_type_index[T, *Ts]()` names, which MIR carries as a `VariantIndex` and `native::mono` closes per instance, so a generic body calls `Variant` operations over its own binders (`v.isa[T]()` in `def pick[T]`, a `Variant[*Self.Ts]` field of a variadic struct: `assets/ok/variant_generic_def_operations.mojo`, `assets/ok/variadic_struct_over_variant_method.mojo`), an alternative may repeat (`Variant[Int, Int](3)` selects the first, `assets/ok/variant_duplicate_alternatives.mojo`), and a `T` that is no alternative fails the instantiation with upstream's "constraint failed: Type does not exist in Variant." (`assets/type_error/variant_operation_non_member.mojo`); the instance type is spelled element by element (`Variant[Int, String]`). Porting `Self._check[T]()`, whose `comptime assert` now runs, is R411. `__eq__`/`__ne__`, `__hash__` (the discriminant, then the payload), `write_to`, and `write_repr_to` (upstream's `Variant[<alternatives>](<payload repr>)` frame) are Mojo bodies written as `comptime for i in range(Self.Ts.length): comptime T = Self.Ts[i]`, so they run on both backends. The storage primitive keeps the tag-checked MIR operations (a wrong-tag projection traps deterministically rather than upstream's debug assert). A field `Variant[*Self.Ts]` over a pack not bounded `Deinitable` is accepted where the pin rejects it (R408). |
| Types | Origin-carrying parameter reference types | Check | Named/place origins, semantic-only `Origin` parameters, and fixed/symbolic mutability declarations are checked. Upstream's `ImmOrigin` / `MutOrigin` aliases are accepted as bare binder bounds (`o: ImmOrigin` records as `Origin[mut=False]`) and as `std.origin` imports. The capability cast `ImmOrigin(o)` (equivalently `Origin[mut=False](o)`) resolves in struct and `Pointer` origin arguments, `unsafe_origin_cast` targets, and `ref[...]` clauses, keeping `o`'s provenance and rejecting the upgrade; it is enforced on `Pointer` origins and `Origin[mut=True]` binders, and in a struct origin argument it keeps `o`'s identity in the struct's origin tail while recording the binder immutable. A mutable-origin `Pointer` coerces to the `ImmOrigin(o)` pointer of the same provenance, as upstream's `@implicit` capability-dropping constructor does. A hand-written constructor or a free function binds its own origin binder from a `Pointer[T, o]` argument, and a `MutOrigin` binder rejects an immutable pointer. A free function's binder bound from an argument, at the top of it or nested in it (`Span[Int, o]`, `Holder[Span[Int, o]]`), is bound in its result too, so `f(Span(xs))` has type `Span[Int, origin_of(xs)]` (`assets/ok/free_function_origin_binder_result.mojo`). An explicit origin argument binds the origin slots of the callee's parameter and result types as well, so `slots[origin_of(xs)](n)` over `-> Pointer[Span[Int, o], MutUntrackedOrigin]` points at spans of `xs` (`assets/ok/unspelled_loan_carrying_type_argument.mojo`). The removed `Origin[mut=...].cast_from[o]` spelling is rejected with a migration diagnostic. |
| Types | Origin-carrying return reference types | Run | Parameter/receiver projections, unions, call substitution, invalid-local escape rejection, and returned alias execution are implemented. A module `def`'s reference result is read through wherever a value is wanted, as a method's is: `var r = pick(a, b)` owns a copy and demands `ImplicitlyCopyable` (the pin's rule), and `print(pick(w))`, `pick(x, y) + 1`, and `pick(a, b).copy()` read the referent. The read is taken from a hidden handle slot that loans the places the result's origin names, so an argument at its last use stays alive for it. A method's result takes the same slot when its origin names an argument, as one returned through an origin binder does: `pick[o: Origin](self, ref[o] x: Self.T) -> ref[o] Self.T` keeps `w` alive across `print(words.pick(w))` (`assets/ok/origin_binder_result_keeps_argument.mojo`). `ref r = pick(a, b)` and a returned `pick(a, b)` keep the handle. A returned method call forwards its accessor's contract with the accessor's `self` bound to the receiver, so `return self.items.unsafe_get(index)` under `ref[origin_of(self.items)._get_owned_interior["element"]]` stays within the declared origin as the subscript `self.items[index]` does, and so does `xs.unsafe_get(0)` over a `ref xs` parameter (`assets/ok/reference_return_forwarded_accessor.mojo`). A returned place must be its declared origin itself, as the pin compares them: a field is a different origin from its owner and an element interior from its container, so `return self.item` needs `ref[origin_of(self.item)]` and `return self.items[i]` needs `ref[origin_of(self.items)._get_owned_interior["element"]]`, and the owner-wide spellings are rejected with the pin's "cannot return reference with incompatible origin" (`assets/type_error/return_origin_widening_owner_field.mojo`). A field of a reference-returning call's result is a place within the call's result origin, so `inner(w).t` under `inner(ref w: W) -> ref[w] W` is `w.t`: it returns under `ref[w.t]` (or `origin_of(w.t)` over read parameters, or `self.whole().u` in a method) and writes through, while `ref[origin_of(w)]` rejects it as the pin does (`assets/ok/ref_return_call_result_field.mojo`, `assets/type_error/return_origin_widening_call_result_field.mojo`). A returned pointer dereference names the pointer's own origin, as upstream's `Pointer.__getitem__(self) -> ref[Self.origin]` does: `return Pointer(to=self.x).unsafe_origin_cast[origin_of(self)]()[]`, the body of upstream's `Optional._unsafe_get_enum_payload`, returns under `ref[self]`, as does a cast held in a local (`assets/ok/ref_return_pointer_dereference.mojo`). A union declares each of its members, an untracked origin accepts any place, and `Tuple`, `MaybeUninit`, and `Variant` keep `ref[self]` over their compiler-private storage, which upstream reaches through a raw pointer. A call returning a mutable reference, a module `def`'s or a method's, is an assignment target: `bump(k) = 9`, `bump(k) += 1`, `p.x_ref() *= 3`, and `pick(p).y = 4` write through the returned reference, and a call returning a value or an immutable reference is rejected as the pin rejects it. An in-place operator dunder applies through either kind of reference result: `text(s) += "d"` over a `String` and `n.name_ref() += "b"` both call `__iadd__` on the referent. A write through a `ref` binding or a reference-returning call destroys the old referent, so one over a value the body cannot implicitly destroy — a `T` not proven `Deinitable`, an `@explicit_destroy` type — is rejected as abandoned, as upstream (`assets/type_error/reference_binding_write_linear_parameter.mojo`, `assets/type_error/reference_call_write_linear_parameter.mojo`, `assets/type_error/reference_write_explicit_destroy.mojo`). A result — a `ref [...]` clause, a struct origin tail (`-> Span[T, origin_of(items)]`), or a parameterized associated type (`-> C.IteratorType[origin_of(items)]`) — names `origin_of(p)` only when `p` has memory to borrow, as the pin judges it: a `ref` parameter or receiver always, any other only when its type is proven not `RegisterPassable`. A type parameter, an associated type, a trait's `Self`, or a struct whose `RegisterPassable` conformance is conditional (`Optional`, `Tuple`, `MaybeUninit`, even over a closed argument) "might expand to a RegisterPassable type"; a proven one (`mut items: Int`, `Optional[Int]`, `T: RegisterPassable`) "has RegisterPassable type"; and a read trivially register-passable one "doesn't have a memory origin" (`assets/type_error/generic_result_origin_read_param.mojo`, `assets/type_error/result_origin_read_register_passable.mojo`, `assets/type_error/trait_result_origin_read_self.mojo`). A read argument such a result names is borrowed, as upstream passes a memory-only `read` argument by address: the callee reads the caller's local, field, or list element, and a temporary lives as long as the reference (`print(field(W(4)))`, `ref r = field(W(5))`), across free calls, methods, statics, keywords, function values, and nested defs (`assets/ok/ref_result_through_read_param.mojo`). A static method's reference result is read through like a module `def`'s, and a method's result through a read parameter is immutable whatever the receiver is (`assets/type_error/reference_method_read_param_assignment_target.mojo`). The native backend runs every one of these, raising or not, a function value and a nested def returning the referent's address as a compiled callee does. `Pointer(to=)` a call returning a reference points at the place the reference names (`Pointer(to=w.get())`, `Pointer(to=o.unsafe_value())`), so a method may return its accessor's place through an origin-cast pointer (`assets/ok/pointer_to_reference_call.mojo`); an owned call result is still rejected as no place (roadmap R1234), where the pin materializes it. |
| Structs | Fields, fieldwise/manual construction, methods, copy/move/drop | Run | Structs have value semantics, including checked consuming copies of projected Copyable fields. A `@fieldwise_init` constructor takes its fields by position or by keyword, as the synthesized `__init__` names each parameter after its field: keywords bind in any order after leading positionals, arguments evaluate in source order, and a generic or variadic struct binds or infers its parameters the same way (`Pair[Int, Bool](storage=(1, True))`, `Pair(storage=(2, False))`: assets/ok/fieldwise_keyword_construction.mojo), and a copy or move constructor spelled `__init__(out self, *, copy: Self)` leaves the fieldwise constructor in place (assets/ok/fieldwise_init_with_spelled_copy_constructor.mojo). The fieldwise constructor is a synthesized `nodebug` `S.__init__`, as upstream's: it takes each field that is not register-passable-trivial as an owned `var` argument and moves it in, so a copied place is copied and then moved, and a temporary or transfer is moved once, as the pin prints (assets/ok/fieldwise_init_owned_arguments.mojo); it inlines like a written one, so a generic function that builds a struct over its parameter and reads nothing else of it keeps one instance (assets/ok/fieldwise_constructor_instances.mojo). A copy or move constructor, written or synthesized, restates the struct's parameters as an `__init__` does, each an `__init__` overload upstream, so `C[n](copy=c)` passes `n` as its constructor's argument and a copy constructor that reads no struct parameter keeps one instance (assets/ok/construction_unread_struct_parameter_instances.mojo). A generic `Copyable` struct with a `String` field does not compile yet: `inline-parametric` leaves its copy constructor reading the parameter it removed (`docs/roadmap.md` R1279). Both backends run the constructor a call names and resolve none by arity, as upstream's take the call's symbol; a call naming the struct itself remains only for a struct with a `ref` field, which still builds its value fieldwise without a synthesized `__init__` (`docs/roadmap.md` R1278), and for `T(copy=x)` at a struct, which copies through the struct's recorded copy constructor (R1283); a `ref` field's read passed to a `mut` or `ref` parameter, a written constructor's included, binds the field's referent on both backends (extension: assets/extensions/ok/ref_field_argument_at_ref_parameter.mojo); an unknown, repeated, or missing field is rejected (assets/type_error/fieldwise_unknown_keyword.mojo). `Self(...)` as a constructor inside a method is still rejected (`docs/roadmap.md` R58). |
| Generics | Typed parameters, defaults, constraints, and packs | Run | Type and typed scalar/aggregate value parameters support positional/named binding as at the pin (positional arguments fill the slots that are not infer-only in order, then each keyword binds its parameter by name, an infer-only binder included, wherever it is written (`f[T=Int, 3](1)`, `C[b=2, 1]`: `assets/ok/infer_only_parameter_by_keyword.mojo`), a keyword for a slot a positional argument filled is "unexpected keyword parameter" (`assets/type_error/keyword_parameter_already_bound.mojo`), and a positional argument for an infer-only binder is "unexpected parameter"), inference (a type parameter inferred from a literal binds the type the literal materializes to — `Int`, `Float64`, or `String` for a string literal, as at the pin: `assets/ok/generic_param_string_literal_materializes.mojo`; a list, set, or dictionary display argument is checked against its parameter's solved type on every generic call path, so `Dict[String, Int](["a"], [1], None)` and `W[String](["a"], None)` over `List[Self.K]` construct `List`s, and an open element parameter is solved from the display's elements, `f(["a", "b"])` binding `K` in `List[K]`, as at the pin: `assets/ok/generic_display_argument_typed_by_parameter.mojo`, `assets/ok/generic_display_argument_infers_parameter.mojo`), dependent defaults, infer-only declarations, trailing `where` predicates, type equality, and heterogeneous compile-time packs including `conforms_to(Ts.values, Trait)`. The retained `(condition, "diagnostic message")` form works on functions/methods, structs, conditional conformances, associated and trait `comptime` members, and non-generic top-level `comptime` declarations; concrete projection/specialization reports the message, while implication and conformance truth ignore it. Origin parameters are semantic facts and may appear before or after packs without shifting ordinary specialization arguments, and an inferred origin-mutability Bool can discharge a callable constraint after the ordinary generic parameters erase. Value parameters extend to `[dtype: DType]` (on defs and structs; `Scalar[dtype]`/`SIMD[dtype, w]` positions resolve concretely per instance) and to **struct-typed value parameters** on structs and `def`s (`[e: Extent]`): the fieldwise construction of a non-generic `@fieldwise_init` struct from compile-time values (`Extent(2, 3)`) is a frozen `CtValue::Struct`, and any other argument (`Extent.square(4)`) evaluates through VM-backed CTFE and is frozen to that construction before the check; the frozen value is the instance's identity (`Tagged[Extent(2, 3)]`), and a field read (`Self.e.rows`, `e.cols`) folds per instance. A `UInt` struct value parameter reads at `UInt` (`Self.n + 1` over `Counter[n: UInt]`), and an integer or float literal bound to a sized-scalar value parameter (`lane[300]()` over `def lane[x: Scalar[DType.uint8]]`) materializes into its lane, wrapping to `44`, as at the pin (`assets/ok/uint_and_scalar_value_parameters.mojo`). A **tuple-typed value parameter** (`def g[p: Tuple[Int, Int]]`, called `g[(1, 2)]()`) reads in its body the same way: a whole read is the nominal `Tuple` constant and `p[0]` borrows it materialized into a temporary, on the VM and natively (assets/ok/comptime_for_aggregate_binder.mojo). A tuple or struct value parameter may hold `String` elements or fields (`g[p: Tuple[Int, String]]` called `g[(1, "a")]()`, a default `(7, "d")`, a nested tuple, `g[q: Q]` over a struct with a `String` field): the argument is typed against the declared type as a run-time argument is, a literal element converted to `String`, and each read materializes the frozen value as one typed constant, as upstream's `kgen.param.constant` does, the parameter's slot owning nothing, so a destructor runs once per materialized use and an `__init__` that transforms its arguments runs only at compile time, as at the pin; a module or local `comptime` value and a `comptime for` element holding a `String` materialize the same way (assets/ok/value_parameter_string_aggregate.mojo, assets/ok/value_parameter_materialized_per_use.mojo, assets/ok/comptime_value_materialized_without_constructor.mojo). A compile-time list (`comptime L: Array[M, 2] = [M(1), M(2)]`) is one `Array` the elaborator's CTFE builds and freezes, and each `materialize[L]()` materializes it with no element initializer run, as at the pin (assets/ok/comptime_array_materialized_frozen.mojo). **Limits:** an `Array` value parameter (`def show[xs: Array[Int, 3]]()`) is rejected (R901), and a struct's `String` value parameter read as `Self.s` is mistyped (R790). A closed bracket argument (`tup[(1, Tag(8, "x"))]()`, `s[1 + 2]()`) is compile-time data on the call, as at the pin: the caller runs no code for it and builds nothing to destroy. A method's own tuple or struct value parameter works the same way, on an instance or a static method (`h.show[(1, Tag(8, "x"))]()`, `H.st[(2, Tag(9, "y"))]()`; assets/ok/value_parameter_method_aggregate.mojo). A struct instantiation's argument (`Counter[4](1)`), an inferred value, and a string argument still lower as run-time constants that nothing reads (R474). A field read of a struct value parameter is upstream's struct extract on the parameter, never a read of the whole struct: `q.a` and `q.i.v` are constants the elaborator folds per instance, a field owning something (`q.s`, `q.i`) is constructed alone into the temporary its consumer owns, `var j = q.i` takes that value with no copy and requires only the field to be `ImplicitlyCopyable`, and `q.s^` is rejected as at the pin; a `comptime for` element's field read in a loop the elaborator unrolls folds the same way, except a `String`, float, `UInt`, or tuple field, which still materializes the element (R472) (assets/ok/value_parameter_field_read.mojo, assets/type_error/parameter_field_transfer.mojo). As Mojo's call carries a parameter attribute, a closed bracket argument — of a generic call or of a construction (`Counter[4](1)`), a string (`f["x"]()`, `external_call`'s callee) included — and a value parameter inferred from an argument's type (`size(Counter[4](1))`, a `UInt` one too: `name(S[UInt(3)]())`) emit no run-time code at the call: the value rides the call's instantiated arguments (`assets/ok/value_parameter_inferred_from_argument_type.mojo`). **Limits:** a `String(...)` construction in the argument is rejected (R461), as is a tuple argument over an enclosing binder (`g[(n, 2)]`, R462), and a struct's tuple-typed parameter cannot be read in its methods (R463). `where` predicates over frozen struct methods are deferred. Constraints are plural: a declaration may carry repeated trailing `where` clauses on functions, methods, structs, trait requirements, associated and trait `comptime` members, and `comptime` declarations — a method's clause refines its own signature as well as its body (`-> Need[Self.T] where conforms_to(Self.T, Copyable)` on a `T: Movable` struct), an associated `comptime` alias resolves under its own clause and under the condition of a conditional conformance that requires it (`Iterable where conforms_to(T, Copyable)` → `IteratorType`), and a conditional `Copyable` conformance verifies its fields under its condition; each clause is retained independently and the first failing clause reports its own message (per-trait conditional-conformance conditions stay single-clause). Generic top-level `comptime` aliases (`comptime Pair[T: Copyable & Movable]: AnyType = Tuple[T, T]`) lower once into a checked alias registry and expand per application in any type position — directly, through another alias, or as a generic argument — validating arity, bounds, defaults, and the alias's own clauses through the same contract as a struct application; imported aliases expand identically. A variadic alias parameter binds as a pack, as on the pin (R1205): a spread of it expands (`comptime T2[*Ts: Copyable] = Tuple[*Ts]` makes `T2[Int, Bool]` the tuple `Tuple[Int, Bool]`), its `Ts.values` list binds (`L[Bool, Int]()[1]` over `comptime L[*Ts: AnyType] = TypeList[Trait=AnyType, Ts.values]`), a value body reads it (`comptime Len[*Ts: AnyType] = Ts.length`, `comptime First[*vs: Int] = vs[0]`, folded by the elaborator below MIR), a caller's own pack forwards whole (`T2[*Ts]`, `Len[*Ts]`), a template's own parameters bind it while still symbolic (`Of[T, U].length` in `def count[T: AnyType, U: AnyType]`), and a struct's parameterized member binds its own pack the same way (`M.T3[Int, Bool]`; `assets/ok/generic_alias_type_pack.mojo`); a construction through such an alias stays R60, and a value body over a construction (`TypeList[...]().length`) R1194. A Bool-bodied alias is a **predicate alias** (`comptime IsSmallCopy[T: AnyType] = conforms_to(T, Copyable) and IsTriviallyCopyable[T]`): the body lowers into the checked constraint algebra and an application inlines it wherever `conforms_to`/`IsTrivially*` propositions are valid — `where` clauses, conditional-conformance conditions, and `comptime if` — including a predicate alias expanding an earlier one; aliases pre-register like struct shells, so a synthesized conditional method (e.g. `copy()`) may reference one regardless of declaration order. Predicate-alias parameters take no bounds beyond `AnyType` (spell requirements in the body), no defaults, and no packs, and the builtin `IsTrivially*` spellings are unshadowable. The current-Mojo `TypeList` vocabulary runs as a compile-time subset in the same positions: `TypeList.of` is the bundled struct's upstream alias (`comptime of[Trait: type_of(AnyType), //, *Ts: Trait] = TypeList[Trait=Trait, Ts.values]`), read through the unbound `TypeList`, so `TypeList.of[Trait=AnyType, T1, ...]()` is a construction of the list it names (propositions over closed elements fold eagerly), its `.values` is a raw list (a struct member's body, a construction's bracket argument), a subscript of the construction is a type (`var z: TypeList.of[Trait=AnyType, Bool, Int]()[1] = 5`), `comptime L = TypeList.of[...]` binds the type, and a template applies it to its own parameters (`TypeList.of[Trait=AnyType, T, U].length`; `assets/ok/typelist_of_alias.mojo`), while `Trait` left to inference (`TypeList.of[Int, Bool]()`) is rejected (R1176), and a `TypeList[...]()` construction binds its bracket list as the bundled struct's, so `Trait=`, `values=`, and a raw list a struct's `comptime` member holds (`S[Int, Bool]._types`, or `T._types` through a bound `T: HasTypes`, whose length the elaborator answers per instance, in a `comptime if` too) run beside a pack's `Ts.values` (lowering to the same checked pack-constraint forms as `conforms_to(Ts.values, ...)`; `assets/ok/typelist_over_member_raw_list.mojo`). A member's read has its annotated type, and a raw-list member whose body does not convert to its annotation is rejected at the declaration, as at the pin (`assets/type_error/raw_list_member_bad_annotation.mojo`). **Limits:** a member read on a struct applied to a forwarded pack (`S[*Ts]._types`, R1193), `TypeList[Ts.values]().length` in a local `comptime` of a generic `def` (R1194), and any other member body checked against its annotation (R1195). An earlier raw-list member is a list argument wherever a value goes, as at the pin: `TypeList[Self._types].length` in a later member, `TypeList[Trait=AnyType, Self._cases]()[id]` in a parameterized member's body, and `TypeList[Trait=..., Self._types]()[0]` in a method's body, signature, or a trait's requirement and default method, and an unannotated member over `Self.Ts.values` or `TypeList.of[...].values` keeps its raw-list type (`assets/ok/typelist_over_earlier_member.mojo`); `length` (deprecated `size` alias) is an Int operand, `any`/`all` reduce a per-element predicate (an `IsTrivially*` spelling or a one-parameter predicate alias), `all_conforms_to[Trait]()` is the trait form, and `contains[T]()` tests membership. A compile-time TypeList value binds (`comptime tl = TypeList.of[...]()`), measures with `len`, indexes with compile-time bounds checking (`tl[2] == Float64` in a `comptime if`), and drives `comptime if`; its `length` and `len` fold in a runtime position too (`print(tl.length)`). A list reverses with `.reverse()` and lists concatenate with `TypeList._concat[A.values, B.values]()`, over closed lists (`assets/ok/typelist_reverse_concat.mojo`) and over packs still open, where the result is upstream's `param_list.tabulate` / `param_list.concat` (`ParamKind::ListTabulate`, `ListConcat`): it measures, indexes, and spreads into a `Tuple` type (`Tuple[*Ts.reverse()]`, `Tuple[*TypeList._concat[Self.Ts.values, OtherTs.values]()]`), which MIR carries (schema 1.29) and the elaborator closes per instance (`assets/ok/pack_reverse_concat_types.mojo`). `all`/`any`/`contains`/`all_conforms_to` over a list computed from packs still open are rejected, and a spread of a computed list binds only `Tuple` (roadmap R345, R348); the map/filter/reduce/slice/splat family, generator-typed predicates, and other runtime-position TypeList values are recorded gaps. A value-bodied generic alias (`comptime Twice[n: Int] = 2 * n`, or annotated `comptime plus_one[n: Int]: Int = n + 1`) is a template over its parameters, which each application binds positionally or by keyword after checking arity, bounds, and its `where` clauses, at a literal or at a parameter still open (`plus_one[k]` in `def f[k: Int]()`), as a runtime value, inside another alias's body, as a struct's parameter argument, and in a local constant (applications nest: `Twice[Twice[5]]`; `assets/ok/comptime_alias_value_parametric.mojo`, `assets/type_error/comptime_alias_value_where_violated.mojo`). A struct's `comptime` value member takes parameters the same way (`comptime get[idx: Int]: Int = Self.values[idx]`, `comptime plus[k: Int] = Self.n + k`), applied as `S[1, 2, 3].get[1]`, `Self.get[1]`, `P[m].plus[k=5]`, or by a later member, and an unparameterized member reads an element of the struct's pack (`comptime first = Self.values[0]`; `assets/ok/parameterized_value_member.mojo`); a trait's parameterized value requirement (`comptime get[idx: Int]: Int` read as `T.get[0]`) and a Bool-bodied alias read as a value over a type parameter (`IsSmallCopy[T]`) are rejected (roadmap R1146, R1147); origin parameters, function-body declarations, and constructor-through-alias calls (`Pair[Int](...)`) are rejected subset gaps, and forwarding a symbolic value parameter into another parameterized struct (`= Fixed[n]`) shares the pre-existing value-parameter forwarding gap recorded under parameterized associated types. A bound type pack is itself a `TypeList` in every compile-time position, so upstream's pack spellings work alongside the adapter: `Ts.length`, `Ts[i]`, `Ts.contains[T]()`, `Ts.all_conforms_to[Trait]()`, and `Ts.all[P]()`/`Ts.any[P]()` in `where` clauses (struct conformances and method availability), `comptime if` conditions, and `comptime` bindings. A struct's own pack is spelled bare only in its header — the parameter list, the conformance clauses, and the trailing `where`, where `Self` is not available — and `Self.Ts` in every member (field types, `comptime` members, method signatures, availability clauses, and bodies), as upstream requires: a bare member spelling reports upstream's `unqualified access to struct parameter 'Ts'; use 'Self.Ts' instead` (`assets/type_error/pack_*_unqualified.mojo`, `assets/ok/pack_bare_in_struct_header.mojo`), while a `def`'s or a method's own pack stays bare.
