A struct's `comptime` member reads its own pack as `Self.Ts.length`, `len(Self.Ts)`, and `Self.Ts[i]` (an index may read an earlier member, `Self.Ts[Self.width - 1]`), each fixed by the application's elements, so `Row[Int, Bool].width` is `2` and `Row[Int, Bool].First` is `Int`, as at the pin (`assets/ok/struct_member_reads_type_pack.mojo`). A **trait-valued parameter** (`def g[Tr: type_of(AnyType)]()`, `struct W[Tr: type_of(Copyable) = Copyable, //, n: Int]`) is a value parameter of upstream's trait metatype, displayed `AnyTrait[...]`: it binds a trait or a `&` composition positionally or by keyword, accepts a trait that refines its bound (`ImplicitlyCopyable` for `type_of(Movable)`, any trait for `type_of(AnyType)`), forwards (`f[Tr]()`, `W[Self.Tr]`), infers from an argument's type, and is part of an instance's identity (`W[Copyable]` is not `W[Movable]`), on the VM and natively; a trait its bound does not accept, a type, or a value is rejected with the pin's "parameter 'Tr' has 'AnyTrait[Copyable]' type, but value has type ..." text, and since a trait has no run-time form, `var x = Tr` and `print(Tr)` are rejected as at the pin (`assets/ok/trait_valued_parameter.mojo`, `assets/type_error/trait_parameter_*.mojo`). It bounds a type binder of its list, as upstream's symbolic `ParamType`: `T: Tr`, `*Ts: Tr`, and `T: Tr & Hashable` see `Tr`'s declared bound in the body (`x.copy()` only under `Tr: type_of(Copyable)`), a value of one may be abandoned while a field of one must still be `Deinitable`, and each use checks every argument against the trait `Tr` receives (`g[Tr=Copyable, Int]()`, `S[Tr=Copyable, Int]`, forwarded as `g[Tr=Tr2, *Us]()`), rejecting one with the pin's "'g' parameter 'T' has 'Copyable' type, but value has type 'AnyStruct[NC]'". A trait is a type where KGEN holds one: a raw list's element may be a trait or `Tr` (`` __mlir_type[`!kgen.param_list<`, AnyType, `>`] ``, `_MLIR.KGENParamListType[Tr]`), a type pack's `Ts.values` is such a list of its bound, an infer-only `Tr` solves from the list it is given (`L[Ts.values]()`), and a list converts to a list of a trait its own refines (`L[Tr=AnyType, Ts.values]` over `*Ts: Copyable`), while a list of values or of a trait `Tr`'s bound does not accept is the pin's "value passed to 'values' cannot be converted from 'KGENParamList[...]'" (`assets/ok/trait_bounded_type_parameter.mojo`, `assets/type_error/trait_bound_*.mojo`, `assets/type_error/raw_list_*.mojo`). **Limits:** a trait `comptime` alias (`comptime MyTr = Copyable & Movable`, R1171), inferring `Tr` from the types it bounds (`g[Int, Bool]()`, `f(3)`, R1176), a struct's trait parameter as a method binder's bound (`T: Self.Tr`, R1177), overloads that differ only in it (R1172), a discarded read `_ = Tr` (R1173), and `type_of` of a value or a struct type (R134). A parameter typed by upstream's KGEN metatype `` __mlir_type.`!kgen.type` `` (displayed `__TypeOfAllTypes`) binds a type, as at the pin: spelled directly, through a module alias (`comptime KT = __mlir_type.`!kgen.type``), or through a struct's member alias (`elt_type: Self.KGENTypeType`, `T: Meta.KGENTypeType`), it is a type binder every type satisfies, a forwarded `AnyType` binder and the metatype itself (`member[Meta.KGENTypeType]()`) included, while it implies no trait, so `List[U]` over one is rejected as at the pin (`assets/ok/kgen_type_type_parameter.mojo`, `assets/type_error/kgen_type_type_binder_not_any_type.mojo`); `__TypeOfAllTypes` is not a spelling (`assets/type_error/type_of_all_types_spelled.mojo`). A parameter typed by upstream's `` __mlir_type.`!kgen.string` `` (spelled directly, through a module alias `comptime KGENString = __mlir_type.`!kgen.string``, or through a struct's member alias) is a value binder of a KGEN string, a `StringLiteral`'s `value` parameter: a compile-time string literal's `.value` (`"None".value`, `("So" + "me").value`, a module, member, or local `comptime` of one) folds to one, a binder forwards it (`bind[s]`, `bind[Self.name]`), a `*names: KGENString` pack's elements (`names[0]`) and raw list (`names.values`, read by `ParameterList[names]().size`) bind, a default `s: KGENString = "x".value` applies, and two of different text key different instances (`Tag["None".value]` is not `Tag["Some".value]`), on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/kgen_string_literal_value.mojo`). A string literal does not convert to one, and a KGEN string implements no operator (`==`, `+`), as at the pin (`assets/type_error/kgen_string_*.mojo`). Spelled directly, `conforms_to(KGENString, Tr)` is `False` for every trait, while a trait-bounded binder binds it and answers by its shape, as at the pin. **Limits:** a KGEN string read at run time (`var x = "a".value`, a runtime `!kgen.string` argument, a `List[KGENString]`), which the pin materializes, is rejected (R1190); a `comptime` string constant in a bracket argument (`bind[lit]`, `(lit + "me").value`, R1191); and `StringLiteral[v]` and a symbolic `s.value` over a `[s: StringLiteral]` binder, since `StringLiteral` is compiler-modelled (R1192). A struct's type member is an explicit type argument (`g[M.E]()`), and a trait or a composition binds one too (`k[Copyable]()`, `k[Copyable & Hashable]()`). **Limits:** an alias of it on an imported package's struct (`T: _MLIR.KGENTypeType` outside the bundled library, R1174), and a member-alias argument inside a parameter annotation (`raw: M.ListOf[M.KGENTypeType]`, R806). A type pack is upstream's `TypeList`: the prelude exports the bundled `TypeList[Trait: type_of(AnyType), //, values: _MLIR.KGENParamListType[Trait]]` with its `length`, `__getitem_param__`, and `__len__`, and a pack's `Ts.length` and `Ts[i]` (`Self.Ts.length` and `Self.Ts[i]` in a struct's members) read those members at the pack, so `TypeList[Ts.values].length`, `len(TypeList[Ts.values]())`, and the annotation `TypeList[Ts.values].__getitem_param__[0]` run too, and a parameter typed by a pack element (`x: Ts[0]`) has the pack's bound in the body, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/type_pack_reads_through_typelist.mojo`). A subscript of a `TypeList[...]()` construction is a type, the construction's `__getitem_param__[i]` as at the pin: a parameter, a `ref` result, a local annotation, and a bracket type argument spell `TypeList[Trait=AnyType, Ts.values]()[i]`, over a pack, `Self.Ts.values`, or a struct member's raw list, with a symbolic or a constant index, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/typelist_value_subscript_type.mojo`); a struct's parameterized type member over its own pack (`comptime E[id: Int]: AnyType = Self.Ts[id]`, or the same through `TypeList[Trait=AnyType, Self.Ts.values]()[id]`) closes at an instance, `W[Bool, Int].E[1]` being `Int` (`assets/ok/struct_pack_parameterized_type_member.mojo`); the construction alone is rejected as a value (`assets/type_error/type_annotation_is_a_construction.mojo`), and a generic body's element stays symbolic (`assets/type_error/typelist_value_subscript_symbolic_init.mojo`). Through a bound's raw-list member (`TypeList[Trait=AnyType, T._types]()[0]`, or its `__getitem_param__[0]` spelling), the element keeps its dependent type in the body with the trait the member's list declares as its capabilities, whatever `Trait=` the construction spells, and closes to the conformer's element at a call, in a function, a method, and a requirement called through the bound (`x.get[0]()`) (`assets/ok/typelist_element_through_bound.mojo`, `assets/type_error/typelist_element_through_bound_not_writable.mojo`, `assets/type_error/typelist_element_through_bound_mismatch.mojo`). A trait requirement whose signature reads an element of the method's own pack (`ref x: Us[1]`, `ref x: TypeList[Us.values]()[1]`) is matched by a witness reading the same element, whatever it names its pack, and not by one reading another, as at the pin (`assets/ok/trait_requirement_indexes_own_pack.mojo`, `assets/type_error/trait_requirement_pack_element_mismatch.mojo`); a `TypeList` proposition over the pack in such a signature (`B[Us.contains[Int]()]`) is rejected in any signature (R1220). **Limits:** the member spelled out in a compile-time expression (`comptime E = Ts.__getitem_param__[1]`, or `comptime T = TypeList[Ts.values]()[1]`, R1178), over a named `comptime` value (`tl[1]`, R1203), `TypeList`'s list vocabulary, which is still compiler-known (R1181, R1182), and a type pack as a `TypeList` value (`var x = Ts`, `len(Ts)` as `__len__`, R1169). In a runtime expression position, a `def`'s or a method's own pack answers `Us.length`, `len(Us)`, `Us.contains[X]()`, and `Us.all_conforms_to[T]()` as the pin does: a template-served body carries each as a parameter constant (`Const::Param`, schema 1.20) the elaborator folds per instance, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/pack_length_runtime_position.mojo`); A nested `def` reading its enclosing `def`'s pack fails MIR verification (R288), and a specialized variadic struct's method folds only `length` and `contains` there. An infer-only type parameter also solves through a callable-bounded sibling (`[T: AnyType, //, F: def() -> T]` with an `F` argument binds `T` from the callable's result type). A `def` value parameter infers from an argument's type as upstream's does: `size(Counter[4](1))` against `def size[n: Int](c: Counter[n])` binds `n = 4`, for an ordinary or infer-only parameter, a nested `def`'s, one forwarded from an enclosing binder (`size(c)` over `c: Counter[m]`) or an `Int` expression over one (`Counter[m + 1]`), and a method's struct parameter (`Counter[Self.k]`), in a static method as in an instance method (`assets/ok/static_method_reads_struct_value_parameter.mojo`); the checker records the inferred values and the erased call passes them as named compile-time arguments (`assets/ok/value_parameter_inferred_from_argument_type.mojo`). A constructor's `where` clause is an availability condition of the candidate: an `__init__` whose clause fails for the constructed type is not selected, and a call only it would have matched reports the clause (`Box[Plain]()` over `def __init__(out self) where conforms_to(Self.T, Defaultable)`, `Tuple[Plain, Int]()`; `assets/type_error/unavailable_initializer.mojo`, `assets/type_error/tuple_default_element_not_defaultable.mojo`). A struct member's `where` clause reaches MIR with the struct's declared conformances and lifecycle rows, and the elaborator decides it per instance when it asks: an instance whose arguments disprove the clause does not have the member (`assets/ok/conditional_member_availability.mojo`). No compilation answers a struct for a trait ahead of use: a library struct's conformance to a library trait, to a compiler-known trait the library never asks (`Absable`), or to an importer's trait, through its refinements, is decided from the struct's declared conformances and the trait declarations' parents when a `comptime if` or a clause asks it, as upstream decides it (`assets/ok/conforms_to_library_types_unasked_traits.mojo`). The elaborator decides every clause form the checker compiles, not only `conforms_to`: an `IsTrivially*` predicate from per-struct trivial-lifecycle rows the checker builds by the rule `is_trivially` applies (a declared `TrivialRegisterPassable`, or the base capability with no user lifecycle member and every field trivial), a value comparison over the instance's value arguments (`where Self.n > 2`, `where Self.n + 1 == 3`), and a `TypeList` `all`/`any`/`contains`/`length` over a bound pack, while a `Tuple` or `TString` with no specialization answers by its elements (`assets/ok/conditional_member_trivial_value_clauses.mojo`). `IsTrivially*[Self.T]` in a member's clause names the struct's binder, as at the pin. A member's clause over `TypeList[Self.Ts.values]()` (`.all[IsTriviallyCopyable]()`, `.contains[Int]()`, `.length == 2`) is decided per receiver (`assets/ok/typelist_self_pack_member_clause.mojo`). **Limits:** an `all`/`any` through a predicate alias stays undecided in the elaborator (R1197). `T()` over a parameter bound to a SIMD type is the zero vector, so `Array[c_char, 4]()` default-constructs (`assets/ok/simd_parameter_default_construction.mojo`). |
| Generics | Symbolic value arguments and parameter-expression equality | Run | A value argument is a typed canonical parameter expression (`docs/notes/param-expr-attributes.md`), so a generic declaration is checked with its value parameters symbolic: `Buf[n + 1]` is the type `Buf[1 + n]`, `Buf[(n + 1) * 4]` is `Buf[n * 4 + 4]`, and `Buf[n + n]` is `Buf[2 * n]` before any value is supplied, a free `def`'s own value parameter may appear in a type argument (`Array[Int, n + 1]`), and `Array[Int, 3]` is `Array[Int, Int(3)]`. A non-parameterized associated alias binds the instance's value parameters too (`S[Int, 3].Alias`). A type parameter is identified by its declaration, not its spelling, and two overloads of one generic `def` or struct method are two declarations — to the elaborator as to the checker, which give each binder one id, numbered past the erased origin parameters: a callable contract's own binder spelled like the enclosing `def`'s (`apply[T, F: def[T](T) -> T]`) stays the contract's, and a trait witness whose binder is spelled differently from the requirement's (`push[X: Hasher]` for `push[H: Hasher]`) conforms. Native monomorphization solves and substitutes a type or value binder by the same identity, and MIR names the binder a `ConstructTypeParam` constructs and the binder a forwarded type argument (`hash[Self.H](key)`) carries by it too (MIR text schema 1.3). A compiled `where` clause names each operand by its declaration's identity too, as does a callable default naming an earlier parameter (`g: def() thin -> Int = f`): the clause is bound at its declaration — its own binders first, then the enclosing ones — and judged under arguments keyed by binder, and both are written to MIR text by `owner`/`slot` (schema 1.4). A deferred slot — a callable-value parameter's, or a value argument only the elaborator folds — names the binder whose slot it fills by identity as well, so an enclosing value binder of the same spelling no longer closes it, and the elaborator's markers for a name that is no parameter are their own compile-time value; MIR text writes the slot by `owner`/`slot` and the marker as `ct_marker` (schema 1.5). A bound-pack query's pack (`TypeList[Ts.values]().length`) is named by identity too, and written the same way (schema 1.6). A value argument built from the caller's own value parameters (`successor[n, 1 + n]()`, a method's `below[Self.n, k]()`, `Counter[Self.length](i)`) is recorded on the MIR call as that parameter expression beside its runtime register, and native monomorphization evaluates it under the caller instance's bindings, so the call compiles natively as it runs on the VM (`assets/ok/value_argument_from_caller_parameter.mojo`, schema 1.7); a nested generic `def` building one from its own parameter (`scaled[k + 1]()` inside `inner[k: Int]`) is specialized only per call site binding that parameter (`assets/ok/nested_def_value_argument.mojo`). A struct constructed over one (`Counter[1 + Self.length](i)` in a method, `Counter[n + 1](i)` in a generic `def`) has its bracket argument typed as a generic call's is, so the template verifies and its instances run on both backends (`assets/ok/symbolic_instance_construction.mojo`). Reading the enclosing function's value parameter inside a nested `def` is rejected. What a clause guarantees inside its declaration's body (`where conforms_to(T, Copyable)`, `where IsTriviallyCopyable[T]`), and what a `comptime if conforms_to(...)` arm proves, refines the binder it names by identity, and a struct's conditional conformance (`Copyable where Ts.all_conforms_to[Copyable]()`) is judged as its compiled condition under the struct's own binders, wherever the query is made. A method call keeps its own compile-time arguments and its struct's by declaration: its availability clause binds each list to its own binders position by position (a variadic struct's lone pack to the whole element list), so a method binder never shares an argument with a struct binder of the same spelling. An operand no declaration binds, an origin's mutability parameter or an associated member's own parameter, is identified by its spelling. The comptime elaborator's parameter metadata names each binder by its declaration too — the template, as the checker's owners do, or `Struct.method` for a method's own — though its environment is still keyed by name. The canonical form is the pinned Mojo's and no stronger: integer `+`, binary `-`, and `*` are a sum of products and a left shift by a constant is a multiplication, while unary `-`, `//`, `%`, and `**` are opaque atoms, so `Buf[-(-n)]` is not `Buf[n]` and `Buf[n * n]` is not `Buf[n ** 2]`. As at the pin, substitution re-folds the polynomial part and leaves an opaque atom unfolded (`Buf[n // 2]` at `n = 8` is not `Buf[4]`), while a value default over the same expression evaluates. `ParamContext::rebuild` rebuilds an operator from its parts as the canonical constructors kept it, an unfolded atom (`8 // 2`) and a cancelled term holding a partial atom included, so the Pliron A1 parameter payload exports every canonical node with its identity. An arithmetic `where` operand (`where n + 1 == m`) is retained and discharged at the application: a violated clause reports its message, and an unproved one is rejected as lacking evidence unless the enclosing declaration's own `where` states the same proposition, in either operand order. Compile-time machine `Int`/`UInt` arithmetic wraps at 64 bits and `//`/`%` floor toward the divisor's sign on every evaluator. A module constant whose initializer applies a function (`comptime B = f(A)`, `comptime C = B * 2`) is its application in a type, never the folded value, as at the pin (decision D3, `docs/notes/ctfe-request-path.md`): `SIMD[DType.float32, C]` matches itself on both sides of a call (`assets/ok/comptime_applied_constant_in_signature.mojo`) and rejects `SIMD[DType.float32, 8]` (`assets/type_error/comptime_applied_constant_mismatch.mojo`), while the body runs on the folded value. **Limits:** a nested generic `def`'s value argument built from its own parameter is VM-only; parametric MIR accepts a symbolic vector lane or width and a symbolic pack element in a register type (`docs/notes/param-expr-attributes.md` §Register types), but no body carries one into MIR before the `comptime for`, pack, and `DType` stages of `docs/roadmap.md` (R1, R2, R3); an applied module constant shapes a vector width only — in a struct argument it is rejected, and one whose initializer applies more than one function, or a function with a non-`Int` result, keeps the folded value's identity (`docs/roadmap.md` R138, R139); a value parameter is inferred from a direct reference only (`Buf[n + 1]` against `Buf[4]` needs the explicit `[3]`, as at the pin); no function-valued parameter application; an arithmetic `where` operand is compiled for free `def`s and struct methods, and stays the explicit unsupported operand on a trait method, an alias, or a Bool-bodied predicate alias; and a compile-time `//` or `%` by zero is an error where the pin yields `0`. |
| Generics | Value parameters typed by a type parameter | Run | A value parameter or value pack whose type is an earlier type parameter of the same list (`struct S[T: ImplicitlyCopyable & Writable, //, v: T]`, `def f[T: …, //, *vs: T]`, upstream's `ParameterList` shape) or, on a method, the enclosing struct's (`def pick[v: Self.T](self)`) is a value parameter of that type, as at the pin; so is one typed by a struct's member (`def h[x: M.A]()`, `struct S[x: M.A]`, `raw: _MLIR.KGENParamListType[t]`), whose struct shell waits for the member's struct when both are in one module, though only with an identifier argument (`M.L[Int]` is roadmap R806). The infer-only `T` is solved from the value as a runtime argument solves its parameter: a literal materializes (`f[3]` solves `Int`, `f[2.5]` `Float64`), a forwarded pack (`P[*xs]`) solves from its element type, a default solves when no argument is given, an argument type (`s: S[v]` against `S[3]`) solves it on inference, and CTFE (`comptime x = f[3]()`) binds it the same way. The first value fixes `T`, so a later value of another type is a mismatch reported against its own parameter, and the solved type must satisfy `T`'s bounds. Such `def`s and methods are served by their templates on the VM and natively. Not yet: a value that solves `T` to `String` (`f["lit"]()`, rejected with an explicit unsupported error), a keyword-supplied infer-only binder (`f[T=Int, n=3]`), a `UInt` value inferred from an argument type, and the elaborator still classifies a `Self.T`-typed binder as a type parameter (see `docs/roadmap.md`). |
| Generics | A generic `def` instantiates from its template's MIR | Run | A plain trait-bound generic `def` (uniquely named; type parameters, or scalar `Int`/`Bool` value parameters no runtime parameter type names, which an application binds; a type pack too, its collector read or owned, when the body binds no element's type under its loop and spreads the pack only into `print`, another served `def`, a method with a type-pack collector, or a constructor whose `__init__` has one (`assets/ok/pack_template_served.mojo`, `assets/ok/pack_method_template_served.mojo`, `assets/ok/pack_constructor_spread_served.mojo`); a value pack (`*values: Int`, `*flags: Bool`, `*kinds: DType`), when the body reads it only as its length (`len(values)`, `values.__len__()`, `values.size`, each the bundled `ParameterList`'s `size` member) or an element at a compile-time index (`values[i]`, under a served `comptime for` too: a runtime read calls `ParameterList.__getitem__`, a parameter-position read is a list element the elaborator folds per instance) (`assets/ok/value_pack_template_served.mojo`; a `comptime for` over the pack itself carries the pack as the loop header's sequence, `assets/ok/value_pack_comptime_for_served.mojo`; a struct's own value pack reads the same ways in its methods as `Self.values`, `materialize[Self.values[i]]()` included, `assets/ok/struct_value_pack_method_read.mojo`; a pack spreads whole into another application's brackets — `Pack[*vs]()`, `total[*vs]()`, `Sink().count[*vs]()`, `Pack[*Self.qs]()` — `assets/ok/value_pack_bracket_spread.mojo`; and a runtime read — `for v in values`, `values[i]` at a runtime index, `var l = values`, `for v in Self.values` — is the zero-sized `ParameterList[*values]()` the pin types it as, checked as that construction in the read's place, whose `__getitem__` reads the element from a constant array laid out once per program, `assets/ok/value_pack_runtime_read.mojo`; a call that leaves the pack empty is rejected, roadmap R490); no `DType` or layout-dependent parameter, reaching no compile-time-keyed `def`, applying no struct specialized whole over its parameters) mints no clone at any closed call, inferred (`show(n)`) or explicit (`show[Int](n)`): the call keeps the template's name, MIR records the arguments the checker solved (`MirInstr::Call::instantiated_args`), and `native::mono` instantiates the template's MIR for both backends — a parameter no runtime parameter or result spells (`bytes[Int]()`) included. Every generic `def` is served so, whatever its binders (`assets/ok/template_served_def_closed_call.mojo`: `total` over `Tuple[Int, T]`, `rep[T, n: Int]` with its `n` bound from the application; a `Tuple`- or struct-typed value binder and a value pack typed by one included): nothing above the check mints a clone. As upstream, callers differing only in origins share one instance, whose bound types carry no origin (R866) and whose own origin parameters stay symbolic. |
| Generics | A method's own compile-time parameters instantiate from its template's MIR | Run | A method with compile-time parameters of its own — on a non-generic struct or a generic struct's instance, a static, a generic constructor (`C("hi")` binds `T` at `StringLiteral`, as the pin does; `assets/ok/generic_constructor_literal_argument.mojo`), a type or `Int`/`Bool` binder, a `DType` or lane binder of its own (a body constructing at that lane included), a binder no runtime argument spells (`s.name[Int]()`), a type pack of its own (`*a: *Ts`, a `comptime for` or `comptime if` over `Ts.length` and a method spreading its pack into another included; `assets/ok/pack_method_template_served.mojo`), and a `comptime if` or `comptime for` reading only its own binders (`assets/ok/template_method_own_comptime_if.mojo`) — mints no per-call clone: the call keeps the template's name, MIR records the arguments the checker solved (`MirInstr::MethodCall::instantiated_args`, schema 1.22), and `native::mono` binds them beside the receiver's for both backends. A member of a vector- or struct-keyed struct specialized whole is served the same way where its template serves it (the hashers' `_update_with_simd`, `AHasher`'s included). No method mints a per-call clone: a `comptime for` over its own binders is kept in the template whatever its body holds — a binding with a converting annotation (`comptime t: String = "s"`), a range bound or binding that applies a function whatever its result (`range(len(mk(n)))`, `comptime e = mk(n)[i]`), a sequence a compile-time application builds (`comptime for x in mk(n)`, or over `comptime l = mk(n)`) — and a body that still fails over its own binders reports the failure where a call reaches it (`assets/ok/comptime_for_annotated_binding.mojo`, `comptime_for_applied_bound.mojo`, `comptime_for_applied_sequence.mojo`). A trait's value member read through a type binder in such a loop's bound (`range(T.K)`) is rejected (R500). |
| Generics | A generic struct's method instantiates from its template's MIR | Run | No method of a generic struct mints a clone per instance: `Optional[Int].get` is the template's MIR, which `native::mono` substitutes for both backends, and nothing is checked or derived for the instance, whatever compile-time construct the body holds or reaches. Lifecycle members and constructors are included, a variadic initializer by the call's element count. A `comptime if` or a `comptime for` over the struct's parameters (`comptime if Self.T == Int`, `comptime for i in range(Self.n)`) stays in the template, as a generic `def`'s does, and `native::mono` decides it per instance; the struct's parameters are binders of the method's body as `Self.`-qualified names only (`assets/ok/struct_method_comptime_template_served.mojo`). A `rebind` stays in the template too (see `rebind` below), and so does a nested `def` or lambda, which the elaborator instantiates under each instance (`assets/ok/template_method_nested_def.mojo`). Every `comptime for` in a generic body stays in its template, as the pin's parameter `for` does, a method's and a `def`'s alike: a bound that reads a type's value member through a binder (`range(T.K)`, `range(Self.T.K)`) or a field of a struct-valued parameter (`range(Self.e.w)`) is a parameter expression, and a loop over a module constant list of struct values forces the constant at the loop header (`assets/ok/template_type_member_loop_bound.mojo`). No `def` is compile-time-keyed either, so a method calling a generic `def` reaches its template. A type name over the struct's parameters (`_unqualified_type_name[Self]()`, the `write_repr_to` of `List`, `Dict`, `Set`, `Array`, and `Optional`) clones nothing: the template carries the type in `MirInstr::TypeName` (`type.name`, text schema 1.11), and the elaborator writes the name from the substituted type as a string constant. An associated type no signature spells is solved the same way, below the waist: MIR records each struct's unparameterized associated types (`MirStructDeclaration.associated_types`, text schema 1.12), and the elaborator reads `C.Element` off the struct `C` is bound to, so a generic `def` that names the member only in its body (`for item in items` over `C: Iterable`) is instantiated from its template at a loan-carrying argument (`assets/extensions/ok/template_served_iterable_def.mojo`). An instance whose argument carries a loan or a callable is served the same way, and so is an instance only a template body builds (`Box[List[Self.T]]`, the `EmptyOptionalError[Self.T]` that `Optional.__getitem__` raises), which `native::mono` finds from the body (`assets/ok/generic_struct_template_reach.mojo`). The elaborator resolves per instance: a `!=` on a type that declares only `__eq__` dispatches `__eq__` and negates it, a comparison of sized scalars the template typed `Bool` converts its mask, and a static call on a spelled receiver binds the struct's parameters from the receiver type MIR records on the call (`Pair[Self.U].count()`, `Call.receiver`, text schema 1.8). A call site keeps a callee's transfer effect only where a loan can ride it: a symbolic value read out of the receiver's own storage lends that storage latently, so `shelf.dup(0, 1)` on `Shelf[Int]` leaves no loan on `shelf`. A conversion in such a method is selected once, on the declaration (`assets/ok/implicit_conversion_bound_on_declaration.mojo`). A `^` transfer of a value of a parameter type needs a bound that proves `Movable` (`Movable`, `Copyable`, `ImplicitlyCopyable`, `TrivialRegisterPassable`, a trait refining one, or a `where` clause), as the pin requires of the declaration (`assets/type_error/template_method_transfer_requires_movable.mojo`). |
| Generics | A `DType`-, lane-, vector-, or struct-value-keyed struct is a generator | Run | A struct keyed on a `DType` (`_SequentialRange[dtype]`, a user `Walker[dt: DType]`) or on a value parameter its fields or members use as a lane width (`Lanes[w: Int]` holding `SIMD[DType.int32, Self.w]`) is an ordinary generic struct: its members are checked once with the binder symbolic, cross MIR as templates, and `native::mono` mints each instance for both backends. No instance is specialized whole in the AST, so such a struct applied at a symbolic argument has its fields (`Lanes[n](3).v` in `def owned[n: Int]`, `Lanes[i]` at a `comptime for` index; `assets/ok/struct_generator_lane_symbolic_width.mojo`), and a lane-keyed `def` applying one over its own binder is served by its template (`show[dt: DType]` building `Walker[dt]`, `assets/ok/template_method_struct_lane.mojo`). A scalar `range(...)` (`range(Int32(4))`) constructs the range struct at the argument's dtype. A struct keyed on a vector value (`AHasher[key: U256]`) or a struct-typed value (`Tagged[e: Extent]`) is a generator the same way: a closed vector or struct parameter read at run time is a parameter-value constant (`Const::Value`, MIR schema 1.27), and a field of a struct-typed value (`Self.e.rows`) is a parameter expression that folds per instance in a `comptime if`, a lane width, or a runtime read (`assets/ok/struct_generator_vector_value.mojo`, `assets/ok/struct_generator_struct_value.mojo`). Every variadic struct, `Variant` and a struct over it included (`struct Row[*Ts]`, `assets/ok/pack_element_default_construction.mojo`; `struct Pair[*Ts]` holding a `Tuple[*Self.Ts]`, `assets/ok/pack_struct_pair.mojo`), is a generator too, its pack bound per instance. No struct is specialized whole, and the elaborator, its clone traces, and the checker's template derivations keep no machinery for one. |
| Generics | `Tuple` and `TString` are struct generators | Run | The public `Tuple[*Ts]` (`std/builtin/tuple.mojo`) and the lazy `TString[*Ts]` (`std/format/tstring.mojo`) are ordinary variadic struct templates, as in the pin: each is checked once with its pack symbolic, crosses MIR as a template, and `native::mono` mints every instance for both backends, so no `Tuple` or `TString` is re-declared per element list and the driver derives no tuple request (Hello World's instantiation census reads 0 clones, from 175). A tuple's type is the nominal `Tuple[Int, String]` everywhere; a display's literal elements name the instance their materialized types name. `t[k]` is the accessor `__getitem_param__[k]`, whose reference result a temporary lends from a hidden slot (`make()[1]`, `var a, b = make()`), so unpacking reads each element through it and the per-index accessor clones and their value twins are gone. The consuming members (`reverse`, `concat`, `consume_elements`, `deinit_with`) move each element out with `Pointer(to=self[i]).unsafe_take_pointee()` in a `comptime for` the template keeps and end the storage with upstream's `__mlir_op.`lit.ownership.mark_destroyed`` (`MirInstr::MarkDestroyed`, MIR schema 1.30); both spellings are admitted only in the bundled modules that reach compiler-private storage. A `t"…"` occurrence is the checker's own call of `__make_tstring`, built in one pass; the driver derives no t-string request. Members and conformances of both come only from these declarations, as at the pin: no Rust-side member surface, element-wise conformance answer (`Hashable`, `Comparable`, `Equatable`, `Copyable`, `ImplicitlyCopyable`, `Defaultable`, `Writable`), or clone-naming coercion remains, and a check with no linked prelude has no `Tuple` members. A user variadic struct over either (`Bag[*Ts]` storing a `Tuple[*Self.Ts]`, a method spelling `comptime T = Self.Ts[i]` under a `comptime if`) is served by its own template too (`assets/ok/tuple_template_served.mojo`, `assets/ok/pack_struct_element_alias_condition.mojo`). A named `out` result, which the transforms build, is transferred to the caller, so its destructor runs once (`assets/ok/named_result_transferred.mojo`). |
| Generics | `rebind[Dest](value)` | Run | Upstream's static retyping: while the operand's type is symbolic the expression has type `Dest`; once instantiated the operand must already have exactly that type (a mismatch is rejected) and the call is erased before lowering, so the operand keeps its place — `ref y = rebind[Int](x)` views `x`, `return rebind[T](self.storage[i])` returns the element's reference, and `rebind[T](self.storage[i]) == value` compares it (the bundled `Tuple.__contains__` spells its guarded comparison this way; the guard itself narrows nothing, so the unrebound comparison is rejected from the template: assets/type_error/pack_element_narrowing_without_rebind.mojo). The operand is borrowed, never transferred (`rebind[T](x^)` is rejected). As an assignment or augmented-assignment target (`rebind[Tracked](x) = Tracked("new")`, `rebind[Int](x) += 1`) it writes the operand in place when upstream selects its `ref[src]` overload, i.e. when the operand's declared type is not `TrivialRegisterPassable` (`mut x: T` with `T: Copyable`, a `Self.T` field, a `String`); a `TrivialRegisterPassable` operand (`var v: Int`, a `T: TrivialRegisterPassable` parameter) is rebound by value and rejected as the destination, judged on the template, so a specialization keeps its template's verdict. A rebound whole variable is reassigned as `x = value` is, destroying the value it replaces. A generic `def` or a generic struct's method holding a `rebind` is served by its template, as upstream elaborates `kgen.rebind`: the template reads the operand at `Dest` through a value rebind (`MirInstr::Rebind`, `value.rebind`, text schema 1.23) or a place's `rebind` step typed `Dest` (`Proj::Rebind`, text schema 1.37, upstream's `kgen.rebind` on the reference its `ref` overload returns), and `native::mono` asserts the two types equal for each instance a call reaches, after deciding its `comptime if`s, then erases the rebind. A projection after a rebind addresses `Dest` and is judged like a whole one: `rebind[S](x).f` reads, augmented-assigns, writes, and calls through `S`'s field on `x`'s storage (`assets/ok/rebind_place_projection.mojo`), and an instance whose operand is not `S` fails as at the pin (`assets/type_error/rebind_place_projection_mismatch.mojo`). The equality is nominal, where the pin compares lowered types and so accepts a layout-identical struct (R514). An untaken arm's `rebind` and an instance no call reaches are never judged (`Box[String]` beside a `rebind[Int](self.value)` it never calls runs: `assets/ok/rebind_method_template_served.mojo`), and a reached mismatch fails the instance with the pin's "rebind input type 'String' does not match result type 'Int'" (`assets/type_error/rebind_method_instance_mismatch.mojo`). A `def` nested in a body, and a compile-time evaluation's subprogram, still clone and assert the equality on each clone. |
| Generics | Variadic-generic structs (`struct S[*Ts: Bound]`) | Run | Every variadic struct is a generator, `Tuple`, `TString`, and `Variant` included: its template is checked once with the pack symbolic and `native::mono` binds the pack per instance. An instantiation is explicit (`Pair[Int, Bool](...)`) or inferred by the checker from the constructor a bare construction selects, as current Mojo infers it: `Pair((1, True))` solves the pack from a `Tuple[*Self.Ts]` field against the display, `Bag(7, "x", False)` from a `var *args: *Self.Ts` collector, inside a generic body too (`return Pair((x^, 1))`); a constructor naming the pack nowhere (`Variant(3)`) is rejected as uninferable rather than minting the empty pack (assets/ok/pack_struct_inferred_args.mojo); an explicit application inside a validated body is matched against the template's constructor with the pack bound from its `[...]` arguments, a collector taking exactly the bound elements (assets/ok/pack_struct_explicit_in_generic_body.mojo, assets/type_error/untaken_comptime_if_pack_construction.mojo) — and a generic body may apply or construct it over its own parameters (`Variant[*Ts]` returned from `def f[*Ts]`, `Variant[T, String]` from an inferred `def g[T]` call, a `Variant[*Self.Ts]` field of `struct Outer[*Ts]` constructed as `Outer[T, Int](Variant[T, Int](x^))`: assets/ok/variadic_pack_forwarding_generic_def.mojo; the template serves each such `def`, a pack spread into its result type and a call of an overloaded generic constructor included, assets/ok/pack_spread_result_overloaded_init_served.mojo). Pack-typed members expand to the concrete element list per instance (a member spells the struct's own pack `*Self.Ts`, current Mojo's form; the bare `*Ts` is the header's spelling and is rejected in a member), and `s.storage[i]` has the exact per-index element type at a compile-time-constant index. Instances copy/move/drop as ordinary structs, and a pack constructor (`def __init__(out self, var *args: *Self.Ts)`) checks each argument against its per-index element type with exact arity. A pack element's default construction — `Self.Ts[i]()`, or a `def`'s own `Ts[i]()` — is checked once with the pack symbolic, where the pack's bound or a `where` clause must make the element `Defaultable`, and each specialization constructs the element it binds (assets/ok/pack_element_default_construction.mojo). In a template-served `def` the construction is MIR's element form of `type.construct` (`Ts[i]()` at a loop index or a literal one), which the elaborator writes as the selected element's default construction — a scalar's zero, a SIMD zero vector, a struct's or a `Tuple` specialization's nullary constructor — and a binding of an element under the loop (`var value = Ts[i]()`, `var first = args[i]`) takes a slot per unrolled copy at that copy's element type; a `Defaultable` pack keeps no storage in an instance (assets/ok/pack_element_binding_served.mojo). A local annotated with the element's type (`var value: Ts[i] = …`) is still rejected (roadmap R296). Current Mojo's dependent accessor `def __getitem_param__[i: Int](self) -> Self.Ts[i]` serves `s[k]` at a compile-time `k` from its template, so an ordinary value read has the exact element type; an explicit `ref alias = s[k]` retains the returned handle. General `__getitem_param__` methods receive the source index as a checked value parameter. Runtime-varying and out-of-range dependent pack indexing remain rejected. Public `std.builtin.tuple.Tuple[*Ts]` uses this machinery and a private `__RuntimeTuple[*Ts]` field; exact generic callable elements cross generated declarations through opaque compiler-only ids backed by checker-seeded semantic types, so callable defaults, generic declarations, captures, and effects are not reconstructed from a lossy source spelling. Its `consume_elements` handler type indexes `Ts` by the handler's compile-time index. A bound-violating struct element is rejected by conformance verification, while a def-pack violation is diagnosed at the requesting call. Inside a specialized method a `comptime T = Self.Ts[i]` (or `Ts[i]`) binding names the element type for the rest of the `comptime for` body, and type values compare with `==`/`!=` in `comptime if` (`comptime if T == Int`, `comptime if Self.Ts[i] == String`) — upstream's spelling, and the only one (the Mojito-only `is_same_type[T, U]()` builtin was removed in 2026-09). A method with its own compile-time parameters (`def find[T: AnyType](self) -> Int`, `def get[T](ref self) -> ref[origin_of(self)] T`, a `@staticmethod` `has[T]()`, an `__init__[T: AnyType, //, F: def() -> T](out self, *, init_with: F)` constructor, a pack method `count[*Us: Writable](self, *args: *Us)` inferred from its overflow arguments) is served by its template: every call — explicit `[T]`, inferred from an argument, or an infer-only `T` solved through a callable-bounded sibling's contract — binds the method's own parameters beside the instance's, and its `comptime if T == Self.Ts[i]` folds per instance. A `@staticmethod` is also reachable through an instance (`value.has[Int]()`); a method whose pack-dependent `where` clause is false for a specialization is unavailable there, and a call reports upstream's note with the clause as declared (`violated constraint; constraint declared here evaluated to False, expected 'Ts.all_conforms_to[Deinitable]()'`); a sole-candidate call failing a message-less `where` clause reports the same note (`invalid call to 'show': violated constraint; …, expected 'conforms_to(T, Copyable)'`), and a `(condition, "message")` clause keeps `constraint failed: message`. A struct declaring a type-keyed `__getitem_param__[T]` subscripts by type (`v[Int]`, `v[String]`, `v[List[Int]]`) in value and place positions. Requires the compiler's whole-program handoff (`# requires: discovery` in corpus fixtures). An explicit `p.__getitem_param__[k]()` with a compile-time `k` is the same accessor call as the subscript sugar `p[k]`; a runtime `k` is rejected as upstream rejects a dynamic value in a parameter list. Any other method whose one `Int` binder selects its result from the pack (`def item[i: Int](self) -> Self.Ts[i]`, or a `ref[self.storage] Self.Ts[i]` result) is served the same way (`assets/ok/pack_index_named_method.mojo`). |
| Generics | Closed instances of ordinary generic structs | Run | A closed application of an ordinary generic struct (`Optional[Int]`, `List[String]`, `Dict[String, Int]`, a user `Box[T]`) runs its template's methods: the template is checked once with the struct's parameters symbolic, as Mojo's generic pre-check does, and `native::mono` instantiates its MIR per instance for both backends. `_unqualified_type_name[Self]()` spells the instantiation (`Box[SIMD[DType.int, 1]]`), a `comptime if Self.T == Int` folds per instance, and lifecycle methods, constructors, and `Self.T()` constructions are instantiated the same way (assets/ok/struct_type_param_construction_clone.mojo, assets/ok/type_param_copy_construction.mojo); a positional argument to `Copyable`'s initializer is rejected as the pin rejects it (assets/type_error/type_param_positional_construction.mojo), and construction through a user trait's `__init__` requirement is reported unsupported (R283). An instance whose argument carries a loan through an origin-slotted struct (`Bag[Span[Int, origin_of(xs)]]`, `List[Span[Int, origin_of(xs)]]`) is served by the template too, whose transfer summary carries the stored type for the call to close with the receiver's arguments (assets/ok/template_served_loan_carrying_instance.mojo, assets/ok/loan_carrying_instance_clone.mojo, assets/ok/bundled_instance_loan_carrying_argument.mojo). A method whose body fails to elaborate for an instance, over the struct's parameters or its own, fails as the pin's `function instantiation failed`, at compile time, and only when a call reachable from the entry needs that instance (assets/type_error/struct_method_instance_clone_fails.mojo, assets/type_error/struct_method_per_call_clone_fails.mojo, assets/ok/struct_method_instance_clone_unreached.mojo). No instance mints a per-instantiation clone, and the checker, the VM, and `native::mono` look none up. Requires the compiler's whole-program handoff (`# requires: discovery`). |
| Metaprogramming | Unified `reflect[T]` and declaration selection | Check | Struct facts, named field indexes/types, dependent reflected types, and declaration-producing `comptime` branches elaborate before checking. Current `.field[name]` and `.field_at[index]` return chainable handles whose selected type is `.T`; the removed `field_type` spelling is rejected. ABI byte offsets belong to native backends. |
| Traits | Requirements, nominal conformance, monomorphic associated comptime facts | Run | Includes the protocols exercised by the self-hosted library. A monomorphic associated member declaration/projection carries a name; a parameterized member additionally retains its parameter list (see the parameterized row). A compile-time value member reads at run time and in compile-time expressions, as at the pin: through a bound type parameter (`T.K`, `Self.T.K`, `comptime k = T.K + n`), where it is a parameter value the elaborator answers per instance from the struct's checked members, even one computed from the instance's own parameters (`comptime K = Self.n + 1`); on a struct by name (`A.K`, `B[4].K`, `V[A.K]`) or in its method (`Self.K`), folded by the checker; and by the leading-dot form (`return .RED`). A value member is a bracket value argument wherever a value goes, spelled `Self.n` as a type is, alone, by keyword, or in an expression: in a later member's body (`comptime m = Box[Self.n].w`), a parameterized member's body, a method's body, a field's `SIMD` width, a member application's argument (`Self.E[Self.n]`), and through a bound (`Box[Self.T.K]`); a trait reads its own requirement the same way, in a default method, a `comptime` default, and a requirement's signature (`def get(self) -> Box[Self.K]`), which a conformer's witness matches and a call through a bound reads at `T.K`; a callee signature reading one (`Box[T.K]`, `SIMD[DType.int32, T.K]`) closes at a call with a concrete conformer, in a function's or a method's parameter and a result, and a bounded struct's instance method reads one through its own parameter (`Box[Self.T.K]`) at a concrete receiver (`assets/ok/bound_value_member_in_signature.mojo`); a static method's such parameter stops the elaborator (R1219), and a field typed by one the pre-check conformance oracle (R1218), while a parameterized member read (`Box[T.get[0]]`) does not close (R1217), and a template folds one per instance in a `comptime if` condition, a lane width, and a method's `comptime for` range bound (`assets/ok/trait_value_member_comptime_position.mojo`), while a type member or type parameter there is rejected (`assets/ok/struct_member_bracket_value_argument.mojo`, `assets/type_error/struct_member_type_as_value_argument.mojo`). A module `comptime` reading such a member (`comptime x = S.m`) is rejected (roadmap R1206). Int, Float, Bool, String, and struct-valued members read on the VM and natively; a literal witnesses the type it materializes as (`comptime NAME = "a"` for `comptime NAME: String`), and a read of a member the bound does not require is the pin's "'HasK' value has no attribute 'Z'" (`assets/ok/trait_value_member_through_type_param.mojo`, `assets/ok/struct_value_member_runtime_read.mojo`, `assets/type_error/trait_value_member_not_required.mojo`). A requirement may declare a default (`comptime K: Int = 3`, `comptime E: AnyType = Int`), which a conformer that omits the member takes, as the pin synthesizes the conformer's witness: it is read with `Self` as that conformer (`comptime K2: Int = Self.K * 2`), after the conformer's own members, and a refining trait's conformer takes its parent's default (`assets/ok/trait_comptime_requirement_default.mojo`). A parameterized requirement's default is taken the same way (see the parameterized row); a conformer's own member may read a defaulted one (`comptime X = Self.K + 1`), and a default's body is checked against its annotation only at a conformer (roadmap R1195). A concrete `__next__ -> ref[o] T` may satisfy an abstract `__next__ -> T` only for an identical `Copyable` referent; abstract dispatch retains an explicit read/copy adapter while concrete calls retain their exact ABI. Overloaded requirements (`def pick[T](self, a: T)` beside `def pick[T](self, a: T, b: T)`) each own their binders under their signature-qualified symbol, as overloaded struct methods do. A requirement may declare a literal default (`def scale(self, value: Int, factor: Int = 2)`), which a call through the bound may leave out. A default naming a module constant (`factor: Int = TWO`, `name: String = GREETING`) or a compile-time `Int` expression over them (`-SIX + 1`) folds, at the trait, to the value it has in the trait's scope, so a local of the same name at the call does not capture it (`assets/ok/trait_requirement_constant_default.mojo`). A witness may default differently, add a default, or omit one, as current Mojo allows: a call through the bound runs the requirement's default, which the checker spells at the call when some witness declares it otherwise (`checker/bound_defaults.rs`), and a call on a nominal receiver runs the witness's own (`assets/ok/witness_default_differs_from_requirement.mojo`). A default may also read the method's own value parameters (`factor: Int = n`, `by: Int = n * TWO - m`): module constants in it fold at the trait, and a call through the bound runs it with its binding of the parameters, explicit, keyword, defaulted, or inferred, including a caller's own binder or an enclosing struct's parameter (`Self.w`) (`assets/ok/trait_requirement_parameter_default.mojo`, `assets/ok/trait_requirement_default_from_caller_parameter.mojo`). Every signature holds such a default as an attribute, so an imported trait's requirement reads it from the package without syntax. A default reading a call or a type parameter is rejected. A requirement may declare a `*args` collector, homogeneous (`*xs: Int`) or a pack (`*a: *Ts`), read or `var`, and a call or a spread (`s.take(*a)`) through the bound dispatches to the witness; the witness may rename the collector but must match its element type and convention (`assets/ok/trait_requirement_variadic_collector.mojo`). A witness may also rename a regular parameter, as the pin allows, which a call through the bound binds by position (`assets/ok/trait_witness_renames_parameter.mojo`); a keyword-only parameter's name must match. Binding such a renamed parameter by keyword through the bound (`u.f(x=4)` for a witness `f(self, y: Int)`) is rejected as unsupported (roadmap R420), where the pin binds it by the requirement's name. A trait's signature or `comptime` requirement may read a member of a struct declared anywhere in its module (`_MLIR.KGENParamListType[AnyType]`, `M.X`): the trait waits until those structs' members and method signatures register, and so do its conformers, which take its `comptime` defaults; a member no struct declares is still rejected (`assets/ok/trait_reads_module_struct_member.mojo`, `assets/type_error/trait_reads_missing_struct_member.mojo`). A requirement's body may be a docstring followed by `...` (`assets/ok/trait_requirement_docstring.mojo`). |
| Traits | Parameterized associated comptime types and values | Run | Trait and struct associated members retain type, value, and origin parameter lists (with the `//` infer-only boundary), as in `comptime IteratorType[iterable_mut: Bool, //, iterable_origin: Origin[mut=iterable_mut]]: Iterator`. A trait may require a parameterized compile-time value, as on the pin (`comptime get[idx: Int]: Int`, upstream's generator type `__generator_type[idx: Int] Int`): a conformer's member witnesses it when its parameters match position by position in kind, declared type, and infer-only and variadic markers, names aside (`get[i: Int]` conforms), and its result is of the requirement's type (an unannotated or literal body included); one of another arity, parameter type, or result, an unparameterized one, or one adding a `where` clause the requirement does not state is rejected (`assets/type_error/trait_parameterized_value_*.mojo`). A read through a bound (`T.get[0]`, `T.get[idx=5]` with a keyword binding by the requirement's name, `T.get[i]` under `comptime for`, `Self.T.get[3]` in a struct over the bound, `Self.get[1]` in a trait default method) binds its bracket list against the requirement's own parameters and `where` clauses and is answered per instance from the conformer's member (`StructDeclaration::parameterized_associated_values`), on both backends (`assets/ok/trait_parameterized_value_requirement.mojo`); an unapplied `T.get` is rejected. A parameterized requirement, value- or type-valued, may declare a default, as on the pin: a conformer that omits the member takes it with the requirement's parameters and `where` clauses, read with `Self` as that conformer (`comptime get[idx: Int]: Int = idx + Self.K`, `comptime E[id: Int]: AnyType = Int`), by name, through a bound, and under a refining trait, on both backends; upstream's `EnumLike._enum_elt_type_for_case[id: Int]: AnyType = TypeList[Trait=AnyType, Self._enum_case_types]()[id]` reads the conformer's raw list of case types (`assets/ok/trait_parameterized_comptime_default.mojo`, `assets/type_error/trait_parameterized_default_where.mojo`). A parameterized value member's body reads a sibling value member (`Self.K`), and a raw list of types over the struct's own parameters (`TypeList.of[Trait=AnyType, NoneType, Self.T].values`) witnesses a `KGENParamListType[AnyType]` requirement; one over another element trait does not (roadmap R1200), and a local `comptime` reading such a member (`comptime k = A.get[2]`) is not elaborated (R1206). Binding one unapplied as a generator (R808) and a declared type that reads the member's own parameters (R1208) are not supported. A parameterized application such as `Self.IteratorType[origin_of(self)]` is validated against the declared explicit (post-`//`) parameter arity, and the checked `TyKind::Assoc` carries the application's arguments (type, value, and origin, the last first-class in `TyArg` and erased from the runtime ABI like a pointer origin). A struct's parameterized member is read through the struct as at the pin (R806): in type position, `S[Int].Of[Bool]`, `P.Pair[A, B]`, `P.Buf[n]`, and a parameter typed `_MLIR.KGENParamListType[Int]` or `M.ListOf[M.KGENTypeType]` bind the bracket list as an alias application does (arity, keywords, and value-versus-type kinds); in an expression, `P.Two[1, b=2]`, `P.sz[Int]`, `comptime A = S[Int].Of[Bool]`, and the type argument of `size[S[Int].Of[Bool]]()` are subscripts of the member, `M.Of[Int]()[0]` subscripts a construction through it, and a parameterized member's body may apply another type's member (`comptime Same[T: AnyType] = P.Pair[T, T]`) (`assets/ok/member_alias_application.mojo`); an unparameterized member's body that does (`comptime Same = R.Of[Bool]`) is roadmap R1178. A member is read through a struct left unbound as well, as at the pin (R1204): `S.n`, `S.plus[4]`, `S.Of[Bool]`, and `comptime B = S.Of[Int]` read a member that does not depend on the struct's parameters, no default filling one (`assets/ok/unbound_struct_member_read.mojo`), and a member that does is rejected with upstream's "cannot access comptime member 'Y' with unbound parameter 'D.T'" (`assets/type_error/unbound_struct_member_dependent.mojo`). A local `comptime` binding of a member whose body reads a sibling member (`comptime b = Self.a + 1`) is not elaborated, bound or unbound (roadmap R1206). Constructing through the member (`S[Int].Of[Bool](3, 4)`, `B.Of[Int]()`) constructs the struct it names, as at the pin: the elaborator expands the application to the struct's own construction (`assets/ok/struct_member_alias_construction.mojo`); a construction through an unparameterized member (`Holder[7].Same()`) or a module alias (`W[Int](8)`) is not supported (roadmap R60). A struct's `comptime` members read one another in any order, as at the pin: each resolves after the siblings it reads through `Self`, so a member reads one declared after it, a later parameterized member's application, and a trait's default (`assets/ok/struct_comptime_member_read_order.mojo`). Conformance enforces a parameterized member's declared bound by instantiating the definition's template with placeholder explicit arguments (an explicit value parameter keeps the arity-only contract), discharging conditional cases through the struct's conformance assumption. When a conforming struct instantiates the member, the application resolves concretely by substituting those arguments into the member's lowered template: a type-parameterized member (`C.Wrap[T]` → `List[T]`) resolves through checked declarations, specialization, HIR, typed MIR, verification, and the register VM. An `origin_of(self)` origin argument resolves too: a trait method's abstract `Self.IteratorType[origin_of(self)]` lowers its receiver origin to a symbolic self-origin (erased at runtime), so a conforming struct's origin-parameterized associated member resolves concretely and conformance succeeds. A conformer may also spell the application directly as its own return type (`def make(ref self) -> Self.IteratorType[origin_of(self)]`): with the concrete struct substituted for `Self`, the indexed application resolves through the struct's member. The elaborator answers an application at a generic use as well, from the conformer's declared member (`StructDeclaration::parameterized_associated_types`): `C.IteratorType[origin_of(xs)]` with `C` bound to `List[Int]` is `_ListIter[Int, origin_of(xs)]`, so a borrowed `for` over a generic `C: Iterable` holds its iterator in a slot of that type, and `var it = items.__iter__()` in such a body elaborates and runs. Calling the iterator's methods through the bound (`it.__next__()`) is still rejected (R665), and a member with a value parameter of its own is not elaborated (R666). Origin arguments also apply DIRECTLY to a struct (2026-08): `EntryIter[K, V, some_origin]`, `View[origin_of(self)]`, bare in-scope origin-parameter names, and `Self.origin` spellings are accepted in every annotation position and at constructor expressions — validated (a concrete `Origin[mut=True]` slot rejects a provably immutable argument; a non-origin argument in an origin slot falls back to the erased-declaration arity diagnostic) and kept as the struct's **origin tail** (2026-09-14): `TyKind::Struct` carries one `TyArg::Origin` per explicit origin slot after its declared arguments, so `P[origin_of(xs)]` and `P[origin_of(ys)]` are different types. A constructor binds the tail from the arguments that bind its binders (a `Pointer[T, Self.o]` or `ref [Self.o]` parameter, a struct-typed parameter or `@fieldwise_init` field carrying `Self.o`), a view-returning call binds it from its return contract at the call site, and a slot left to inference is `Origin::Unbound`, which any origin coerces into. Rebinding a view local across origins (`p = P(Pointer(to=ys))` over a `P[origin_of(xs)]`), storing a box over another origin into a `RefBox[Self.origin]` field, and passing a call result over one receiver where another's is held all reject with upstream's `cannot implicitly convert 'P[origin_of(ys)]' value to 'P[origin_of(xs)]'`. The tail erases from the runtime ABI (mangling, layout, MIR verification, and monomorphization ignore it, and instantiation records reset it to unbound). Argument exclusivity (`checker/origins/exclusivity.rs`) judges the origins a callee's declared parameter types carry: `stash(sink, RefBox(Pointer(to=view)))` over `mut sink: List[RefBox[o]], var box: RefBox[o]`, `f(xs, RefBox(Pointer(to=xs)))` over a `mut xs`, and a `mut self` whose binder is bound to the same storage reject with upstream's `aliasing values passed mutably to 'sink' argument and passed mutably to 'box' argument in 'stash' call`, while a generic `append(mut self, var value: Self.T)` names no origin and is accepted; comptime alias bodies may bind origin arguments, and a struct's own comptime aliases are visible to its field types (associated members resolve before fields, in the checker and the specialization conformance oracle), enabling upstream's monomorphic-alias-in-field iterator shape. Forwarding a value parameter into another parameterized struct (a pre-existing generic gap) and a generic free function returning `C.IteratorType[origin_of(c)]` (a declaration-time value-parameter-origin gap) remain later iteration work. Storage annotations (struct fields and local `var` types) must bind explicit origin slots, mirroring the pinned upstream: a partial application that omits an origin slot fails to infer it in every storage position, and a bare origin-slotted generic name is not concrete on a struct field or an uninitialized local — while an initialized local may leave the name bare and infer the whole parameter list from its initializer (upstream-attested via `var v: StringSlice = ...`). Only the outermost application may leave a slot to inference: an origin-slotted struct nested as a type argument (`List[RefBox]`, `List[RefBox[_]]`) is not concrete in every position, parameters included, as at the pin (`assets/type_error/nested_omitted_origin_parameter.mojo`); a compiler-generated (`$`-mangled) declaration rebinds checked spellings and keeps its position's leniency. The placeholder spellings `_` and `...` mark an origin slot explicitly inferred (pin-attested): `var s: Span[Int, _] = xs` and function-parameter placeholders accept (the application counts as complete, the origin infers from the initializer or call), while struct fields and uninitialized locals reject the placeholder exactly like an omitted slot; parameterized-alias applications keep the existing rejection. Constructor value arguments remain the origin-inference context. Function parameters follow the initialized-local rule (pin-attested 2026-08-29): a bare origin-slotted generic parameter infers per call, a partial application rejects with the placeholder hint, and `_`/`...` accept. Return annotations require applied origins: `-> EntryIter[origin_of(self.entries)]` accepts (signature-position origin arguments resolve syntactically — naming parameter/`self`-rooted places or in-scope binders — and erase), while bare and placeholder returns reject as not concrete; `$`-mangled generated specializations keep their reconstructed annotations exempt. Alias declarations stay lenient pending probes. A method called on a value typed by an associated projection resolves through the projection's declared bound, recursively (`items.__iter__().__next__()` under `C: Iterable` answers `Iterator`'s methods, and `C.IteratorType[o].Element` is `Movable`). A trait method's `origin_of(self)` argument names the receiver's place at the call (`C.IteratorType[origin_of(items)]`), so the iterator borrows its source: moving or mutating `items` while it lives is rejected. A signature may name a `ref` parameter's origin in the application (`-> C.IteratorType[origin_of(items)]`, upstream's `iter()` shape), and, as in the pin, the application's origin converts to no other origin: `ref[o] items` returning `items.__iter__()` as `C.IteratorType[o]` is rejected (`assets/ok/generic_iterable_explicit_iter.mojo`). A raw-list member a generic conformer spells over its own parameter (`TypeList.of[Trait=AnyType, NoneType, Self.T].values`), read through a bound, folds at each instance: its length and an element as a reference result's type (`assets/ok/bound_member_list_generic_conformer.mojo`). A one-argument application of a member with an infer-only parameter (`ParameterList.of["A".value]`, `TypeList.of[Trait=AnyType, NoneType]`) binds as a whole bracket list does. |
| Traits | Refinement and default method bodies | Run | Requirements/capabilities inherit. A default is checked once, in its trait, over the trait's `Self` (so `self.extra()` naming only a conformer's method, or an ill-typed default nobody conforms to, is rejected as at the pin), and a conformer gets a forwarding wrapper per inherited overload, which its check builds from the default's resolved signature, for a library trait's default as for its own; a parameter default of a default method is its member's, held once and evaluated over the conformer's instantiation (`n * TWO` for `def g[n: Int](self, x: Int = n * TWO)`, `assets/ok/trait_default_parameter_defaults.mojo`); explicit methods override per overload, and conflicting defaults, a default parameter named like a struct parameter, and `Trait.method(x)` are rejected as at the pin. A default is its trait's member `Trait.method`, checked after every module declaration, so its body calls a function declared after the trait or its conformers, as at the pin (`assets/ok/trait_default_calls_later_function.mojo`). A default's `comptime if` is decided per conformer when its member is instantiated (`reflect[Point].field_count() == 2` folds). Every wrapper is `nodebug`, as upstream synthesizes it, so a statically resolved call to an inherited default is inlined before elaboration into the call of its trait's member, and only a call by trait dispatch reaches a wrapper instance, as at the pin, a call by keyword or omitting a defaulted argument included. A default with a homogeneous collector (`var *xs: Int`) is rejected at its conformers (R548), and so is one whose signature names an origin (R571). A conformer method of a default's parameter shape suppresses it even when it does not satisfy it, where the pin keeps both (R570). |
| Traits | Monomorphic associated-type composition and conditional conformance | Run | Associated bounds merge across refinements; per-conformance `where` predicates are evaluated after type/value specialization. |
| Protocols | Indexer, Hashable, Hasher, Writer, and Writable | Run | `Indexer` requires upstream's `__mlir_index__(self) -> __mlir_type.index` (the only `__mlir_type` spelling accepted; the checker represents the index as `Int`, and `Int.__mlir_index__()` is the identity), and a `-> Int` requirement is a conformance error. When an `Int` subscript contract is selected, a non-Int index is evaluated once and normalized through the checker-selected `__mlir_index__`; a direct overload accepting the source index type takes precedence. `Hashable`'s single requirement is current Mojo's `__hash__(self, mut hasher: Some[Hasher])` (also spelled `__hash__[H: Hasher](self, mut hasher: H)`), with a reflective field-by-field default synthesized for conformers without a hasher-fed `__hash__`; any other `__hash__`, such as a value-returning `__hash__(self) -> UInt`, is an ordinary overload beside the default, as upstream. `Hasher` is upstream's `__init__`, `_update_with_bytes(Span[Byte, _])`, `_update_with_simd(SIMD[_, _])`, `update(Some[Hashable])`, and `finish(var self) -> UInt64`: the wildcard vector parameter is upstream's pair of infer-only dtype and `SIMDLength` binders, which each hashed vector solves (`native::mono` instantiates the method at every leaf type it reaches, at any width, so `hash(v)` over a vector whose dtype or width is a binder runs in a lane-keyed `def` or a `DType`-keyed struct's method: `assets/ok/lane_keyed_hash.mojo`; the retired `UInt64` leaf spelling is a conformance error with a hint), every leaf reaches it as its own vector type with `-0.0` folded first, `SIMD.to_bits[dtype]()` and `.length` are the lane accessors (`to_bits()` defaults to the unsigned dtype of the lane's width, over a symbolic lane too: `assets/ok/simd_to_bits_default_symbolic.mojo`), and width>1 vectors hash lane pairs. `std.hash` ships upstream's keyed `AHasher[key: U256]` (`default_hasher` is the zero-keyed specialization; `repr(set)` spells it `AHasher[[0, 0, 0, 0] : SIMD[DType.uint64, 4]]`), `Fnv1a` (`default_comp_time_hasher`), `hash_seeded`, `hash_seeded_bytes`, and both `hash` overloads — `hash[T: Hashable, //, HasherType: Hasher = default_hasher](x)` and `hash[HasherType](bytes: ImmPointer[UInt8, _], n: Int)`; `List`/`Array`/`Set`/`Dict`/`Optional`/`Variant`/`Tuple` conform conditionally on their elements with upstream's bodies (List/Array feed elements without a length prefix, Set XORs the default hasher's element hashes, Dict mixes per-entry hashes under a fresh caller-typed hasher, Optional/Variant tag with a `UInt8`), and `comptime CT = hash[...](x)` folds through VM CTFE for scalar, Bool, Float64, and string arguments — every value matches current Mojo on both backends. Leniencies: `Hasher` (like `Writer`) resolves without its import, and `StringLiteral` is hashable as the `String` it materializes to; a bare placeholder-origin pointer parameter (`Pointer[T, _]`) reads as the immutable alias: any provenance binds and the body reads through it, while a write or a mutable-demanding forward needs `MutPointer[T, _]` (upstream infers the origin per call and reaches the same verdicts); outside parameter position the bare placeholder is not concrete, as upstream. `x.write_to(writer)` on a `Writable`-bounded type parameter, a built-in Writable value (`Int`, `Float64`, `Bool`, literals, SIMD, `StringSpan`), or the nominal `String` writes its formatted text through the writer's `write_string` (the checker records the operand swap, and MIR names the host primitive `$write_formatted`, which never re-enters the writer's own `write`; a user struct's own `write_to` body dispatches directly), and `x.write_repr_to(writer)` on the same receivers is `writer.write(repr(x))` (upstream's `Wrap(Int(8))`/`Wrap('hi')` through `self.value.write_repr_to(writer)`); a struct parameter's bound may name a user trait declared later in the module. `Writer` is upstream's library trait, bundled in `std/format/__init__.mojo` and exported by the prelude: its requirement is `write_string(mut self, string: StringSlice)` (a `String` or `StringLiteral` payload does not conform; the witness may name the payload otherwise), and its default `write[*Ts: Writable](mut self, *args: *Ts)` writes each argument through its `write_to`, which a conformer may override (`assets/ok/writer_write_override.mojo`); `String` declares upstream's two `write` overloads, `[*Ts]` and `[T]`. A `w.write(...)` call resolves to that declared method — the conformer's on a concrete writer, the requirement through `W: Writer` or `Some[Writer]` — so a pack-keyed `def` spreading its collector into it is served by its template (`assets/ok/pack_spread_into_writer_write.mojo`); `String.write_to` and `StringSpan.write_to` are Mojo bodies that hand the writer a view, and a `StringLiteral` exposes upstream's `byte_length()` and `ptr() -> Pointer[Byte, ImmStaticOrigin]`. Display/repr and String fields stream through Writer with reflective defaults: a `Writable` struct with no `Writer`-fed `write_to` displays as `Name(field=value, ...)` on both backends, and a `write_to` taking any other parameter is an ordinary overload beside that default, as upstream. |
| Protocols | `Sized` and `SizedRaising` | Run | Upstream's library traits from `std/builtin/len.mojo`, declared in the bundled `stdlib/std/builtin/len.mojo` and exported by the prelude, each requiring `__len__` (`SizedRaising`'s raises). A bound's `x.__len__()` resolves through the requirement, and `len(x)` accepts an opaque type that conforms to either, directly or through a refining trait. A struct conforms only by declaring one — every bundled struct with upstream's `__len__` (`List`, `Array`, `Set`, `Dict`, `StringDict`, `Span`, the ranges, the string iterators, `Tuple`, `ParameterList`) lists `Sized` as the pin's do, and an undeclared struct is the pin's "does not conform to trait 'Sized'" — while the compiler-modelled `SIMD` values (so `Int`, `UInt`, and `Float64`, whose length is one), tuples, and packs conform as upstream's structs declare; `Bool`, `String`, and `StringLiteral` do not (`assets/ok/sized_library_traits.mojo`, `assets/type_error/sized_bound_undeclared_struct.mojo`). `len` is still a checker builtin rather than upstream's overload pair, so `len(s)` on a struct with an undeclared `__len__` runs (roadmap R191) and `len(x)` over a `SizedRaising` value does not raise (R988). A trait body may hold `pass` (`trait Measured(Sized): pass`). |
| Protocols | `format` on strings | Run | `String.format` and `StringSpan.format` are upstream's declared `format[*Ts: Writable](self, *args: *Ts) raises -> String`, whose body is the bundled port of upstream's `_FormatUtils` (`collections/string/format.mojo`, kept in `std/string.mojo`): the template parses into `_FormatCurlyEntry`s at run time, automatic (`{}`) or manual (`{0}`) indexing with the `!r`/`!s` conversion flags and `{{`/`}}` escapes, and a malformed template raises upstream's error texts ("Automatic indexing require more args in *args", "Index :>5 not in kwargs", "Cannot both use manual and automatic indexing", ...); format specifiers are rejected, as at the pin. A string literal's `format` does not raise, as upstream's `StringLiteral.format`: while a literal declares no methods (R213) the checker types it as the static call of the bundled stand-in `_FormatUtils.format_literal(literal, args...)`, and a malformed literal template aborts at run time where the pin rejects it at compile time (divergence `format-literal-template-runtime`). Every receiver names a declared callee, so a pack-keyed `def` spreading its collector into `format` is served by its template, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/string_format_declared.mojo`). Gaps: natively, a struct argument formatted through `{}` takes the reflective default instead of its own `write_to`, and one without its own `write_repr_to` is rejected; the port respells several upstream constructs (see the roadmap's `mojo-shape` entry). |
| Protocols | Scalar operation traits | Run | Operators, comparisons, conversions, and rounding are typed through checked operation traits (`Addable`/`Subtractable`/…/`Comparable`/`Negatable`/`Intable`/…); the traits work as generic bounds, and user structs dispatch prefix operators and `Int`/`Float64`/`Bool`/`abs`/`round` through their dunders; an `Equatable` struct declaring `__eq__` without `__ne__` gets the trait's default `__ne__` (`a != b` dispatches `__eq__` and negates the result), as upstream, while a struct that does not conform to `Equatable` has no `!=`; a binary operator whose dunder reads its operands (`__eq__(self, other: Self)`) borrows a place operand where it lies rather than taking a lifecycle copy — a named one, or a list or tuple element read through its reference (`xs[0] < xs[1]`, `-xs[2]`, `1 + xs[1]`), under infix, reflected, and prefix dunders alike — and `hash(value)` hashes its argument in place, as upstream (`assets/ok/operator_operand_borrow.mojo`, `assets/ok/struct_operator_subscript_operand_in_place.mojo`); a temporary operand such a dunder reads (`S(1) < S(2)`, `-S(5)`, `1 + S(6)`, an operator result as an operand, argument, or field base, a chain's two ends, a comparison through a bound) dies when the dunder returns, the argument before the receiver, and a linear one is rejected as an abandoned temporary, as upstream (`assets/ok/struct_operator_temporary_operand_drop.mojo`, `assets/type_error/struct_operator_abandoned_temporary.mojo`); a chained comparison reads every operand the same way and copies only its first middle operand, right after the first link, into a temporary the second link reads (a temporary or `^` middle moves in instead), so only that operand must be `ImplicitlyCopyable`, and in a generic body its type must be provably `Deinitable`, as upstream (`assets/ok/chained_comparison_operand_copy.mojo`, `assets/type_error/chained_comparison_middle_not_deinitable.mojo`), though a copy at a binding's last use is not yet turned into a move (roadmap R136); `~` inverts integers and `Bool` (a user struct dispatches `__invert__`); when the left operand has no operator method for the pair, the right operand's reflected dunder answers (`1 + m` → `m.__radd__(1)`, `__rsub__`, `__rmul__`, …); same-arity dunder overloads select by the right operand's type (`String == StringSpan` beside `String == String`, `opt == None`), and an erased `T: Equatable` body's operator dispatches the `Self`-shaped overload at runtime. The scalar built-ins are `Defaultable`: `Int()`, `UInt()`, `Float64()`, and `Bool()` construct their zeros, at run time and in a compile-time constant (`assets/ok/scalar_default_construction.mojo`), and so does every SIMD value: `Float32()`, `Scalar[DType.int16]()`, and `SIMD[DType.int32, 2]()` zero each lane, and a `T: Defaultable` bound to one constructs it with `T()` (`assets/ok/simd_nullary_construction.mojo`). A SIMD scalar (`UInt64`, `Float32`, any width-one `SIMD`) conforms to `Equatable` and `Comparable`, so a tuple of scalars compares (`(UInt64(1), UInt64(2)) < (UInt64(1), UInt64(3))`), `List[UInt8]` equality and `in` run, and a `T: Equatable`/`T: Comparable` bound accepts one; a multi-lane vector conforms to neither, since its infix comparisons are still lanewise (the pin's `==` reduces the lanes and its ordering asserts `Scalar`). A scalar comparison converts implicitly to `Bool` wherever one is wanted (a return, an annotated binding, an argument) through upstream's `@implicit Bool(Scalar[DType.bool])`, and `not` reads it through `__bool__`; `and`/`or` over one still reject (roadmap R97) (`assets/ok/tuple_of_scalars_comparison.mojo`). Builtin scalars execute primitively behind the protocol, while literal-only evaluation stays exact until contextual materialization. |
| Protocols | `EnumLike` (enum-case metadata for `match`) | Run | Upstream's library trait from `std/builtin/enum_like.mojo`, declared whole in the bundled `stdlib/std/builtin/enum_like.mojo` (docstrings included) and exported by the prelude: `_enum_case_names` (`!kgen.string` names), `_enum_case_types`, the defaulted `_enum_is_exhaustive` and `_enum_elt_type_for_case[id]`, `_get_enum_discriminant`, and `_unsafe_get_enum_payload[id]`. `Optional` and `OptionalReg` conform with upstream's members over their existing storage (`ParameterList.of["None".value, "Some".value]`, `TypeList.of[Trait=AnyType, NoneType, Self.T]`, and a payload getter that asserts `id != 0` and returns the unchecked value through an origin-cast `Pointer`; `Optional` gains upstream's `_unsafe_unchecked_value`). A generic `T: EnumLike` body reads a hand-written conformer's or an `Optional`'s discriminant, both list lengths, `_enum_is_exhaustive`, and a payload by reference, on both backends (`assets/ok/enum_like_protocol.mojo`). As at the pin, no case metadata is validated at the declaration (three names over two types and a one-case enum run); a conformer missing a member or method is the ordinary conformance rejection, and `Optional`'s `None` payload is a failed instantiation (`assets/type_error/enum_like_*`). `Bool` conforms with its library port (R1189); enum-case patterns read this metadata (Control flow row). |
| Overloading | Functions, methods, constructors | Run | Ranking minimizes conversions after applying the contextual `Int`/`Float64` defaults to unconstrained literals, then prefers fixed arity, fewer implicit copies into `var` parameters, shorter signatures, and concrete ties. A method is ranked by its own compile-time parameters, so a concrete method beats a generic sibling as a concrete free function does.
