Two same-arity generic overloads of one method that a call specializes alike (`pick[T](self, a: T, b: T)` beside `pick[T](self, a: T, b: Int)`) each mint a per-call clone only for the calls that select them, so `pick(2, 3)` runs the `b: Int` overload as the pin does, on a plain struct, a generic struct's instance, and through a trait bound (`assets/ok/same_arity_generic_method_overloads.mojo`). An overloaded method is declared under the symbol its calls name, whatever binders it carries: its own bounded binders, a callable-bounded one (`apply[F: def(x: Int) -> Int]` beside `apply(x: Int)`), an existential `Some[Writer]` parameter, and a `Scalar[dt]` pattern spell alike at both ends, so a call left on the template path runs — `self.item.write_to(writer)` beside a generic `write_to[U]` rival, `self.item.__hash__(hasher)` through a `Hashable` bound beside a generic `__hash__[U]` rival, and printing through a `Writable` bound a struct whose `write_to` has a same-arity `String` rival (`assets/ok/overloaded_method_own_binder_symbols.mojo`). A free function may overload on a read versus an owned (`var`) parameter of the same type (no other convention difference is an overload): a place selects the read overload, because it costs the owned one the copy it makes, and where the candidates still tie an explicit transfer `x^` or an rvalue argument selects the owned one (assets/ok/overload_var_copy_beside_generic.mojo, assets/ok/overload_var_copy_method.mojo). A constructor set is ranked the same way, with no candidate dropped beforehand: `C(s)` selects `__init__[T: Writable](out self, a: T)` over `__init__(out self, var a: String)` and `C(String("t"))` the `var` one, and a generic candidate needing fewer conversions wins. A generic constructor of a non-generic struct runs as a per-call clone of its own (`C.__init__$y6:String`), which the construction is retargeted to, and two generic constructors baked alike are told apart by the call's arguments, type-pack constructors included (`assets/ok/overload_var_copy_constructor.mojo`, `assets/ok/pack_overload_constructor.mojo`). On a generic struct the instance is keyed by the instance's arguments and then the call's, so `C[Int](7)` runs a generic constructor declared beside another one, on the VM and natively (`assets/ok/generic_struct_generic_constructor_overload.mojo`). Checked `@implicit` constructors participate: when several accept the source, the one whose parameter is exactly the source type (a literal at its default type) wins and other equivalent conversions remain ambiguous, and a raising `@implicit` constructor makes every conversion through it a raising call. An `@implicit` constructor's single argument may be a `ref [origin]` parameter (a view constructor): the selected conversion then records a source borrow beside the constructor target, so the converted temporary's origin refines to the source place (current Mojo's temporary-origin inference). A call inside a generic body is bound once, while the body is checked with its parameters symbolic, as upstream: every instance inherits that overload (`assets/ok/template_overload_binding.mojo`). Every candidate of a free function's overload set, and of an overloaded function value, is checked inside a probe that undoes each fact it records, so only the selected candidate's facts reach the checked program, as upstream's checked IR holds only the chosen call's ops; a method's candidates are still scored without one (R626). |
| Comptime | Constants, `comptime if`, `comptime for`, type facts | Run | Every `comptime if` arm and `comptime for` body of every function body, plain or generic, is checked once in the one check with the declaration's parameters left symbolic (`Checker::check_comptime_condition`, `check_comptime_for`), kept to MIR, and decided or unrolled by `native::mono` (a closed condition folds to its verdict in the check; one over a runtime local is rejected with the pin's "cannot use a dynamic value in 'comptime if' condition", one calling a raising function — a `raises` `__eq__` included — with "cannot call raising function in 'comptime if' condition", and a type compared with a value, `T == 1`, with "'Copyable' does not implement the '__eq__' method"; a struct value compared with `==` runs its own `__eq__`, never a structural comparison, assets/ok/comptime_if_struct_equality.mojo; the elaborator decides `and` and `or` left to right, stopping once the left side decides, and an `elif` only when no earlier arm was taken, so a payload read guarded by its tag never runs over the other variant, assets/ok/comptime_conditions_short_circuit.mojo); a `comptime for` body is a loop body, so a compile-time `break` and `continue` are its own: a type error in an untaken arm, a member the parameter's bounds do not declare, and an unused template's invalid arm reject as upstream rejects them; a guard such as `T == Int` narrows nothing; an arm is block-scoped; a dead arm's compile-time evaluation failure is not an error; the explicit-destruction analysis runs over the same symbolic bodies — an arm whose condition names one of the body's parameters may assume nothing about the instantiation, so such arms join as `if` branches do and a value one arm alone destroys is abandoned (or, used afterwards, uninitialized) even when every instantiation selects that arm, as upstream; a condition naming no parameter folds to one arm, and its arms join as alternatives; a `comptime for` body leaves outer obligations as it found them; a `with` inside such a body is desugared before the analysis; a body keyed on a variadic pack — a `def`'s or method's own, or any method of a variadic struct — is validated the same way, with the element at a compile-time index (`args[i]`, `self.storage[i]`, `Self.Ts[i]`) an opaque dependent type that may use only the pack's declared bound and what a conjunctive `conforms_to(Ts.values, Trait)` / `Ts.all_conforms_to[Trait]()` of the enclosing declaration guarantees (assets/type_error/untaken_comptime_if_pack_def.mojo, untaken_comptime_if_pack_struct.mojo, pack_element_missing_bound.mojo; conformance/probes/pack_*.mojo), a spread beside other type arguments (`Tuple[Int, *Self.Ts]`) rejected, and a variadic struct over concrete types (`Tuple(1, "one")`, tuple `==`, `t[0]`) an ordinary application there; a body that forwards its pack to another callee (`inner(*a)`, `collect(30, *items^, tail=10)`, a method's own pack, `print(*a)`) is validated the same way, with the callee's pack bound to the caller's whole pack, the callee's bound required of the caller's pack, the two collectors' ownership required to agree, and the spread required to be the last positional argument landing alone in a pack collector (assets/type_error/untaken_comptime_if_pack_forwarding.mojo, pack_forwarding_*.mojo), while a method other than `__len__` on the pack still leaves the body to its per-instantiation check; a body reading `reflect[T]` over a parameter (a `def`'s, or `Self` in a generic struct's method) is validated the same way: `field_count()`, `field_index[name]()`, and `len(field_names())` are compile-time `Int`s, `is_struct()` a `Bool`, and a field type under the loop index (`types[i]`, `r.field_at[i].T`) an opaque dependent type with no bound of its own, which a `comptime if conforms_to(FT, Defaultable & Writable):` arm licenses for that arm and that element alone — as the pin licenses a `conforms_to` arm for a plain parameter and a pack element too (assets/ok/reflection_symbolic_fields.mojo; assets/type_error/untaken_comptime_if_reflection_def.mojo, untaken_comptime_for_reflection_body.mojo, untaken_comptime_if_reflection_method.mojo, reflected_field_type_needs_proof.mojo, reflected_field_proof_is_positional.mojo; conformance/probes/reflection_*.mojo); a body keyed on a `DType` parameter, or on a vector width naming its own parameter, is validated the same way with `Scalar[dt]`/`SIMD[dt, width]` symbolic (typed slots on `TyKind::Simd`): identity is structural in the pin's normal form (`SIMD[dt, n + 1]` is `SIMD[dt, 1 + n]`, `v.join(v)` is `SIMD[dt, 2 * width]`, a `width == 4` or `dt == DType.float64` guard narrows nothing), the whole SIMD surface is licensed and a dtype gate (`&` on a float lane) is the instantiation's to check, an integer or float literal splats into a symbolic lane where a concrete scalar (`Int(2)`, `Int32(1)`, a `Bool`) does not, and a `DType`-keyed struct applied in a validated body — at a concrete dtype or the body's own — registers with its lane symbolic (assets/ok/simd_symbolic_surface.mojo, simd_symbolic_width_hooks.mojo; assets/type_error/dtype_keyed_def_untaken_arm.mojo, simd_width_keyed_def_untaken_arm.mojo, dtype_keyed_struct_method_untaken_arm.mojo, simd_width_keyed_struct_field_untaken_arm.mojo, dtype_struct_applied_in_validated_body.mojo, dtype_struct_applied_symbolically.mojo, simd_symbolic_width_mismatch.mojo, simd_symbolic_scalar_operand_rejected.mojo); `value.to_bits()` with its defaulted target over a symbolic lane is typed at the unsigned dtype of the lane's width, a parameter expression over the lane's dtype that each instance folds. A generic `def` whose `comptime if` keys on its parameters is served by its template: the executable check types every arm with the binders symbolic and records each condition the constraint compiler closes (`SemanticAdjustment::ComptimeCondition`), MIR carries the region as the `comptime_branch` terminator — a branch whose condition is a parameter expression (schema 1.15) — the ownership analysis and drop elaboration decide it as the `if` of the same shape, and `native::mono` decides the condition under the instance's bindings, keeps the taken arm with the destroys placed at its entry, and erases the untaken arms before any call in them is enqueued (`select_comptime_branches`); a value-keyed `scale[n]`, a type-keyed `label[T]`, an `elif` chain, a region inside a `try`, and a value one arm alone consumes, destroyed at the other arm's entry, run on one template (assets/ok/comptime_if_template_served.mojo, comptime_if_generic_specialization.mojo, type_predicate_comptime_if.mojo), explicit (`show[Int](3)`) or inferred (`show(3)`: assets/ok/comptime_if_inferred_def.mojo), uniquely named or an overloaded family's member (assets/ok/comptime_if_overloaded_def.mojo). A condition the constraint compiler does not close — one applying a function (`comptime if is_even(n):`), alone or as a leaf under `not`/`and`/`or` beside compiled leaves — is lowered as the thunk `$comptime$<owner>$<k>` over the binders in scope at the condition, the enclosing `comptime for` indices included, which it reads as parameter references, as it reads a local `comptime` binding over a binder (`comptime m = n + 1`, a chain of them, or one beside a loop index) as the parameter expression it denotes (assets/ok/comptime_if_condition_applies_def.mojo), so each unrolled copy demands it at its own index — in nested loops, two loops of one name, an index shadowing another or a struct parameter, a display loop, and a loop inside a `try` alike (assets/ok/comptime_if_condition_reads_index.mojo); the elaborator demands it: the instance and every pending instance are materialized, the completed output is verified as the fragment the VM runs (`VmBackend::call_concrete`), a callee reaching `print` or `input` is refused, fuel is one counter per compilation (`mojito_vm::crossing::CTFE_FUEL`), the result is cached by instance, and a demand on an instance being materialized is upstream's "function instantiation in parameter domain that recursively requires itself" (assets/ok/comptime_if_condition_applies_def.mojo). A generic `def` holding a `comptime for` over a `range` of parameter expressions — literals, binders, `Self.` members, arithmetic over them — whose body declares no local `comptime` binding is served by its template too: the executable check types the body once with the variable an alias of the element over a symbolic iterator binder and records the loop (`SemanticAdjustment::ComptimeIteration`), HIR and MIR carry it as the `comptime_for.iterator` header of upstream's iterator protocol (below), the only header form, which, like upstream's `kgen.param.for`, holds no slot for the variable (a compile-time `Tuple`, which has no `__iter__`, is rejected by that protocol's own rule, assets/type_error/comptime_for_tuple.mojo), its `range(...).__iter__()` a thunk over the binders — the ownership analysis and drop elaboration decide it as a loop with its trip count unknown, and `native::mono` unrolls it under each instance's bindings (`unroll_comptime_loops`): the body opens with upstream's `comptime if` on `has_next` over the iterator, whose else arm breaks, and the body is copied N+1 times, one copy per iterator `has_next` accepts and one over the exhausted iterator, each deciding that test first and keeping its taken arm alone, so the last copy is the stop arm; each live copy has fresh registers, the index's reads folded, its types substituted, the `comptime if`s over it decided, the loops nested in it unrolled in turn, a `continue` reaching the next copy and a `break` the exit, the drops the analysis placed coming with each copy. A local the body declares is a binding of its own in each copy, one shadowing an enclosing `var` of its name included (assets/ok/template_loop_local.mojo). A local whose type names the index (`var v = SIMD[DType.int32, i](7)`, `var l = Lanes[i](3)`) is a fresh slot in each copy at that iteration's type, as each clone of upstream's unrolled region allocates its own, and an owning one is destroyed within its own iteration, on the VM and natively (assets/ok/comptime_for_index_typed_local.mojo); a struct whose field is a vector over its own parameter (`Lanes[i](5).v`) runs too, since such a struct is a generator (assets/ok/struct_generator_lane_symbolic_width.mojo); so does an annotated one with a numeric literal initializer (`var v: SIMD[DType.int32, i] = 7`), which the checker records as a literal materialization at the symbolic vector type and each copy splats at its own width, as it does at a body's own symbolic width or dtype (`var v: SIMD[dt, n] = 2`) and for an argument (`take[n](3)`) (assets/ok/simd_symbolic_width_literal_binding.mojo). Arithmetic bounds, a loop nested over the outer index, a value parameter applied to the index (`tag[j]()`), a loop inside a `try` region, two loops of one name, and a loop-local `var` per iteration run on one template (assets/ok/comptime_for_template_served.mojo, comptime_for_body_scope.mojo, template_loop_local.mojo, template_scalar_loop.mojo, template_folded_value.mojo; assets/ownership_ok/comptime_for_break_continue.mojo, comptime_for_per_iteration_value.mojo). A loop over a list, set, or dictionary display of `Int`, `Bool`, or string literals is served the same way, as upstream's `parseParamFor` spells every iterable as its `__iter__()` call: the checker spells the display so, the loop steps that iterator by upstream's protocol (below) — a set's distinct elements, a dictionary's distinct keys — `native::mono` unrolls one copy per element, a bare `Bool` variable is a `comptime if` condition of its own, and a compile-time `break` or `continue` is the loop's, in a generic `def` (a pack-keyed one included) and a generic struct's method alike (assets/ok/comptime_for_collection_template_served.mojo). A loop over a named closed collection — a module `comptime` constant (a list, a dictionary's keys, strings), which is one application the elaborator below MIR evaluates, or a local `comptime` binding of a display, which the check binds as its application too — iterates its `__iter__()` the same way (assets/ok/comptime_for_named_collection.mojo); a `Float64` element binds as well, in a literal display or a constant. A list, set, or dictionary display of scalar expressions over the binders (`[n, n + 1]`, `[i, i + 10]` under an enclosing loop, `[Self.n, Self.n + 1]` in a generic struct's method, `[b, not b]`, `{n: 1, n + 1: 2}`) is evaluated per instance, as upstream runs the display on its interpreter: MIR lifts the display's `__iter__()` call as a function over the binders in scope — the owner's, then the enclosing loops' variables — and `native::mono` demands it, runs it on the VM, freezes the iterator with the nominal `Array`, `List`, `Set`, or `Dict` it borrows in compile-time memory, and steps it (assets/ok/comptime_for_display_over_binder.mojo). An element may also call a function or a method that returns an `Int`, `Bool`, `String`, or `Float64` without raising (`[twice(n), n]`, `[m.twice(n), n]`, `[label(n), "z"]`, `[P(n).get(), n]`, `[S[n].g(n), n]`, `[Self.g(Self.n), Self.n]`, `[String(n).upper(), "x"]`, a method on a local `comptime` receiver), read such a field off a call's or a method's result (`[P(n).twin().v, n]`, `assets/ok/pack_def_unkept_loop_template_served.mojo`), construct a scalar (`Float64(n) * 0.5`, binding a `Float64` element), or subscript a closed compile-time list, a module constant or a local literal display, at an index over the binders (`[SQUARES[n], SQUARES[twice(n)]]`): source validation types each call with the binders symbolic, the crossing pass spells a named list as its display there, and the check reads a list display subscripted in a compile-time position as the parameter expression `list[index]` (`SemanticAdjustment::ParamValue`), which the elaborator folds per instance (assets/ok/comptime_for_display_element_reads.mojo, comptime_for_display_method_reads.mojo). A tuple or non-generic fieldwise struct of numbers, booleans, and strings is served the same way (`[(1, 2), (3, n)]`, `[P(1, n), mk(n)]`, `[(n, "a"), (2, "b")]`, `[Q(n, "z"), mk(n)]`, nested tuples and struct fields included): the loop variable is a parameter of the element's type whose every read materializes it — a constant where its leaves are numbers and booleans, a construction where one is a `String` — and a borrow of it (`p[0]`, `h(p)`, `q.s`, a method receiver) materializes it into a temporary the ownership analysis destroys (assets/ok/comptime_for_aggregate_binder.mojo, assets/ok/comptime_for_string_aggregates.mojo). A list display that mixes `String` and string-literal elements is a list of `String`, at run time as at compile time. A local `comptime` binding of such a display (`comptime L = [n, n * 2]`, in a generic `def` or a generic struct's method) is served the same way, as the pin's `comptime` alias is a compile-time value: the binding has no runtime form (`SemanticAdjustment::ComptimeDisplay`), MIR lifts its display once, over the binders in scope where it is declared, for the type and parameter arguments that read an element or the length, and a loop over the name iterates the binding's `__iter__()`; a display, bound or in a header, may read a local `comptime` value bound before it (`comptime k = n + 1`, then `[k, k * 2]`), which its thunk reads as the parameter expression the binding denotes (assets/ok/comptime_for_display_binding.mojo). Every other compile-time read of the binding is evaluated per instance too: a `comptime if` condition (`L[0] == 3`, `3 in L`), a `range` iterable over it (`range(len(L))`, lifted as its iterator's thunk), and an `Int` or `Bool` read by another binding or crossing (`comptime e = L[1]`, `comptime(len(L))`, `materialize[vals[i]]()`) are each lifted as a function that begins by binding the display, whose application the elaborator demands and runs — in a parameter constant and a loop bound as in a condition (`Specializer::applied`) — and a later condition, bound, or display reads such an `e` as that application; `materialize[L]()`, and a value read off the display that is no `Int` or `Bool` (`comptime t = (L[0], L[1])`), build the display where they cross to runtime; a lifted function likewise begins with every other local `comptime` binding it reads that denotes no parameter expression (the tuple `t` above in `comptime if t[0] == 4:`), evaluated again per reader (roadmap R378), while an `Int` or a `Bool` one (`comptime big = n > 2`, then `comptime if big:`) denotes the application of its own lifted function; a display may read another (`comptime M = [L[1], len(L)]`); and a runtime read of the binding (`print(L[1])`, `len(L)`, `for b in L`) is rejected as at the pin, with `cannot materialize comptime value of type 'Array[Int, Int(2)]' to runtime because it is not 'ImplicitlyCopyable'` (assets/ok/comptime_display_binding_reads.mojo, assets/type_error/comptime_display_binding_runtime_read.mojo). A local `comptime` alias of such a binding (`comptime A = L`, or `comptime B = A`) is another name of it: a loop over the alias, an element or the length read in a condition or a type argument, and `materialize[A]()` read the display itself, served by the template in a `def` and in a generic struct's method alike (assets/ok/comptime_display_binding_alias.mojo). `comptime(expr)` over the binders of a generic body (`comptime(n + 1)`) is left by the crossing pass for the check, which requires an operand over compile-time bindings alone, and is the runtime value of that operand. A loop over a value pack (`comptime for v in vals` over `*vals: Int`, or a struct's `Self.values`) or over a reflected field-name list (`comptime for name in reflect[T].field_names()`, or a `comptime` binding of one) steps its `__iter__()` iterator — the pack's `ParameterList` iterator, the list's `Array` iterator — by upstream's `paramfor_*` protocol, as every `comptime for` does; a pack of `Copyable` structs that are not `ImplicitlyCopyable` is rejected as at the pin, and so is a runtime variadic (`comptime for a in args` over `*args: Int`), as a dynamic iterator expression (assets/ok/value_pack_comptime_for_served.mojo, assets/ok/comptime_for_value_pack_iterator.mojo, assets/type_error/comptime_for_value_pack_not_implicitly_copyable.mojo, assets/type_error/comptime_for_runtime_variadic.mojo). A loop bounded by a reflection count (`range(reflect[T].field_count())`, `range(r.field_count())` over a bound handle, `range(len(names))`) carries the query, which the elaborator answers per instance, and a field type under the index decides a `comptime if` by identity or by conformance (`types[i] == Int`, `conforms_to(types[i], Writable & Copyable)`: assets/ok/reflection_comptime_for_served.mojo). A `comptime` binding in the loop body that is a literal, a parameter expression over the binders and the loop variable (`comptime j = i * 2`), an alias of a type binder, or an element of a named compile-time list (`comptime name = names[i]`) is bound once with the variable symbolic (assets/ok/comptime_for_body_binding_served.mojo). An element by position or the length of such a binding in a type or a parameter argument, alone or under integer arithmetic, and a local `comptime` value bound to one, is a parameter expression the template carries and the elaborator evaluates per instance (`SIMD[DType.int32, L[0]]`, `Buf[vals[1]]()`, `g[len(vals) + 1]()`, `comptime e = L[1]` then `g[e]()`, in a generic `def` and in a generic struct's method: assets/ok/comptime_display_binding_arguments.mojo); two reads of one binding are one value and none is the element the display spells, as at the pin (assets/type_error/comptime_display_element_identity.mojo). An `Int` or a `Bool` a type or a parameter argument computes some way the parameter domain does not express — a call, a comparison, a boolean operator, a conditional, over the body's parameters, a `comptime for` index, a display binding, or a local `comptime` value (`g[h(L[0])]()`, `flag[L[0] > 2]()`, `SIMD[DType.int32, h(n)]`, `Buf[h(n)]()`, `comptime e = h(n)` then `g[e]()`) — is the application of a function the check names for the expression (`SemanticAdjustment::ComptimeApplication`): MIR lifts the function over the binders the expression reads, the template carries the application, and the elaborator runs it per instance, in a generic `def`, a generic struct's method, and a plain `def` (`g[h(3)]()`) alike; arithmetic over applications stays arithmetic (`h(L[0]) + h(L[1])`), two spellings of one expression are one type, and the application is not the value it computes (`h(n)` is not `n * 2`), as at the pin (assets/ok/comptime_application_argument.mojo; assets/type_error/comptime_application_identity.mojo, comptime_application_runtime_operand.mojo). A call of a plain module `def` over `Int` and `Bool` arguments is the application of that `def` to what its arguments denote, with no function lifted, so it is the same value wherever it is spelled: a signature or a struct field type takes it (`def make[n: Int]() -> SIMD[DType.int32, h(n)]`, `var v: SIMD[DType.int32, h(Self.n)]`, under arithmetic or a comparison too, and over a function declared below), a body that spells the call again names it, a caller that binds the parameter spells it by the argument (`var v: SIMD[DType.int32, h(2)] = make[2]()`), another generic body spells it over its own parameter (`h(m)` for `make[m]()`), and a call infers the parameter argument by argument (`total(v)` solves `n` from `h(n)` against `h(2)`); the elaborator runs the function on the argument values per instance, in a struct instance's field types too, and the call is not the value it computes (`make[2]()` is no `SIMD[DType.int32, 4]`), as at the pin (assets/ok/comptime_call_signature.mojo; assets/type_error/comptime_call_signature_identity.mojo). The callee may be any callable over `Int` and `Bool` values the call names: a generic `def` given its own parameters (`twice[n]()`, by keyword too), an overloaded one, selected by the arguments, one with a default, a keyword, or a `var` parameter, where a declared default stands for an argument the call omits (`d(1)` is `d(1, 3)`), and a static method of a struct or of a generic struct's instance (`S.f(n)`, `S.t[n]()`, `G[n].w()`, and `Self.w()` in the struct's own signatures and field types), the instance being the application's first argument, so `Self.w()`, `G[Self.k].w()`, and a caller's `G[2].w()` are one value and a call infers a parameter through it. `not` and a conditional expression compile to the parameter domain's own operators: `not (n == 9)` is `n != 9`, `not not b` is `b`, a conditional over a closed condition is its taken arm, and `not (n > 9)` is not `n <= 9`. A raising callee reports upstream's `cannot call raising function in type parameter`, as at the pin (assets/ok/comptime_call_callee_shapes.mojo; assets/type_error/comptime_call_raising.mojo, comptime_not_identity.mojo). A signature that calls a function over a value that is no `Int` or `Bool`, or a method on a value, is still rejected (roadmap R396), one generic over a type too (R397), an expression computing a `String` or a float too (R386), one whose function prints too (R387), and a diagnostic spells a lifted function's name (R388) or an applied callee's symbol (R398). An equal display under a second binding is a different value (roadmap R380, and R390 for an application over one), a capitalized `L[0] * 2` does not parse in an argument (R381), and an element in an untaken `comptime if` arm is still evaluated (R376). Every other loop stays in the template too, as the pin's parameter `for` does: a display element that applies a function, subscripts a value, or computes a float, a `def` that materializes a reflected list whole (`materialize[names]()`), and a loop nested in a kept loop's body or a kept `comptime if` arm, in a generic `def` or a generic struct's method alike (assets/ok/comptime_for_unserved_nested.mojo, assets/ok/template_type_member_loop_bound.mojo); a raising callee in such a display is accepted where the pin rejects it (R404); a collection of tuple or struct elements (a module or local constant, or a display in the header) iterated in a generic `def` or a generic struct's method keeps one loop in the template, its variable a parameter of the element type passed whole, copied, subscripted, and read by field, method, and `comptime if`, each run-time use a fresh value — one with a `String` leaf constructed at the use, and its binder slot owning nothing (assets/ok/comptime_for_aggregate_binder.mojo, assets/ok/comptime_for_string_aggregates.mojo); a transfer out of the variable, as out of any value parameter (`p^`), is rejected as at the pin (assets/type_error/parameter_transfer.mojo); a loop in a non-generic `def` or a non-generic struct's method is unrolled in the AST too (assets/ok/comptime_for_tuple_elements.mojo, assets/ok/comptime_for_struct_elements.mojo); a compile-time `break` or `continue` in such a loop is rejected with an explicit message, where the pin accepts it (`docs/roadmap.md` R245). A method so keyed specializes per call, static or instance, on a plain or parametric owner (`S.show(3)`, `B[Int].show(3)`: assets/ok/comptime_if_inferred_static_method.mojo). A method keyed by its own `DType` or SIMD-width value parameter specializes the same way and spells that parameter in its signature types (`def double[dt: DType](self, a: Scalar[dt]) -> Scalar[dt]`, `def total[w: SIMDLength](self, v: SIMD[DType.int32, w])`), applied explicitly or with the lane read off the argument, on a plain owner or on a generic one (assets/ok/dtype_keyed_method.mojo); its body may also construct at that lane (`Scalar[dt](x)`, `SIMD[DType.int32, w](x)`), each instance at its own lane. The body may forward the lane to a `DType`-keyed `def` (`helper[dt](x)`, `pair[dt, 4](x)`), each instance reaching that def's instance for its own lane (assets/ok/dtype_keyed_method_forward.mojo); forwarding it to a sibling keyed method (`self.twice[dt](a)`, `docs/roadmap.md` R85) is still rejected. On a struct keyed on a value (`Width[n: Int]`), such a method is served by its template too, its signature may spell the owner's value (`SIMD[dt, Self.n]`), and an own `Int` binder is served the same way (assets/ok/dtype_keyed_method_value_struct.mojo). An abstract generic body may call such a `def`, inferred (`show(x)`) or explicit (`show[T](x)`), even when the caller is never called: the call checks against the callee's signature, and each instantiation of the caller reaches the callee's instance (assets/ok/comptime_if_abstract_body_call.mojo). A generic struct's method reaches one through its template (assets/ok/comptime_if_generic_struct_method_call.mojo), and so does a generic `def` nested in another body: reaching such a callee is itself what makes the nested `def` specialize per call, in ordinary code, in a generic `def`, and in a generic struct's method alike (assets/ok/comptime_if_nested_def_call.mojo). A nested `def` whose own `comptime if` keys on its parameter now takes the checker's inferred arguments too, where only an explicit `[...]` application used to resolve (assets/ok/comptime_if_nested_keyed_def.mojo). Such a `def` may also be overloaded with a type-pack one (`def kind[T: Copyable](a: T)` holding a `comptime if`, beside `def kind[*Ts](*xs: *Ts)`: assets/ok/comptime_if_overload_beside_pack.mojo), in either declaration order — the specialization class is a property of a declaration rather than of a name, and the checker's recorded instantiation says which declaration a call selected, by the selected overload's runtime parameter names, then its mangled parameter types, then its `*args` collector's position and element key, then whether the request's arguments bind that declaration's parameters at all (the last of these is what tells a type pack from a nullary sibling, since a variadic parameter is named by neither of the first two keys: assets/ok/comptime_if_overload_pack_zero_arity.mojo). Two type-pack declarations may share a name with no keyed sibling at all, told apart by arity, a regular parameter's type, a keyword-only parameter, the pack's bounds, or the collector's position (assets/ok/pack_overload_*.mojo, and assets/ok/pack_overload_method_arity.mojo for methods); among otherwise tied variadic candidates the checker prefers, as the pinned Mojo does, one whose collector takes at least one argument, then — the one term every candidate pays, variadic or not — fewer implicit copies into `var` parameters, then more arguments bound by value (a read parameter of a trivially register-passable type, or a `var` parameter taking an rvalue), then fewer `ref` parameters (`assets/ok/pack_overload_by_value_tiebreak.mojo`), so a regular `String` parameter beside the pack is ambiguous (`assets/type_error/pack_overload_string_regular_ambiguity.mojo`, `assets/type_error/pack_overload_homogeneous_string_ambiguity.mojo`), a string literal a pack absorbs costs its conversion to `String`, and a call both packs' bounds admit is ambiguous (assets/type_error/pack_overload_ambiguous_bounds.mojo). Still rejected with "requires compile-time parameter": a function-value use of a `def` whose abstract body makes such a call (as the pin rejects it), and one keyed by a `DType` or SIMD-width parameter. A `DType`-keyed `def` may share its name with a keyed, type-pack, concrete, or trait-bound generic `def`: the `DType` member alone makes the name a family, its explicit application binds against its own signature (`kind[DType.float64](1.7)`), a numeric literal binds the lane at its default type (`kind(3)` solves `DType.int`, `kind(2.5)` `DType.float64`, as a lone such `def` also accepts), a `Bool` binds `DType.bool` through its conversion into `Scalar[DType.bool]` (assets/ok/dtype_keyed_bool_lane.mojo), and among candidates tied on every rank term an argument with a lane selects the `Scalar[dt]`/`SIMD[dt, 4]` pattern over a bare type parameter — while `Optional[T]` and `List[Scalar[dt]]` stay ambiguous beside `T`, a concrete `Float64` still beats the pattern, and a `Bool` still selects `T`, as the pin does (assets/ok/dtype_keyed_overload_family.mojo, dtype_keyed_overload_beside_plain.mojo). An overloaded `DType`-keyed `def` is served by its template per declaration, as a uniquely named one is: the checker selects the overload, and a call selecting the lane member is left as written even beside a compile-time-keyed sibling (assets/ok/dtype_keyed_overload_beside_keyed.mojo). A served body's call over its own binder into such a family (`kind(a)` in `inferred[dt: DType]`, `kind[n](a)` in `by_value[n: Int]`) is served by the declaration the checker selected while the call's arguments are still symbolic, so the body is served by its template too, and a body whose call selects that member over its own binder is served by its template too (assets/ok/template_served_body_call_into_overload_family.mojo); a losing overload's probe of a value argument as a type leaves nothing on the winning call; method overloads break the same tie the same way (`c.kind(x)` beside `def kind[T: Copyable](self, a: T)`: assets/ok/method_simd_pattern_beside_bare.mojo). A parametric nested `def` named as a value rejects too, as at the pin, but reports its qualified marker as an undefined variable. A local that shares a module constant's name — a `var`, a declaring unpack target such as `var i, j = 100, 1`, a loop variable — shadows the constant for the rest of its block, in a generic `def`'s body as in a plain one (`assets/ok/comptime_shadowed_locals.mojo`). Each unrolled `comptime for` iteration, like a selected `comptime if` arm, is its own scope, as the pin's: a `var` in the body is one binding per iteration, may shadow a local of the enclosing block, and an owning one is destroyed within its iteration (assets/ok/comptime_for_iteration_scope.mojo). Elaboration then folds the selected arm; direct `comptime for`, VM-backed CTFE, and nominal runtime iteration all treat a zero-step `range` as empty. A `comptime for` over a compile-time Tuple is rejected with upstream's "does not implement the '__iter__' method". A `comptime for` over any other compile-time iterable — a user `Iterator & Copyable`, or a struct whose `__iter__()` returns one, generic ones included — iterates by upstream's iterator protocol: the iterable's `__iter__()` is stepped at compile time by the bundled `std.builtin._stubs` functions `paramfor_has_next` and `paramfor_next_iter`, one body copy per element, the variable each copy's `paramfor_next_value`, with `continue`, `break`, nested loops, `comptime if` over the variable, `String` and struct elements, and nested `def`s reading the variable (assets/ok/comptime_for_user_iterator.mojo, comptime_for_iterable_struct.mojo, comptime_for_iterator_aggregate_elements.mojo); an iterator that borrows a compile-time value iterates the same way — `d.keys()`, `d.items()`, `d.values()`, a list's or a set's `__iter__()`, over a local `comptime` binding, a module constant, or an inline display, and a user iterator holding a `Pointer` to a binding or a temporary — because the evaluation stores the borrowed value in compile-time memory (upstream's `store_to_mem`, `MirInstr::StoreToMem`), which is never destroyed, and the frozen iterator carries the reference as `CtValue::StoreToMem` (assets/ok/comptime_for_borrowing_iterator.mojo); `materialize[it]()` of such an iterator, which the pin accepts, is rejected (R898), and its implicit run-time read is rejected with Mojito's `ImplicitlyCopyable` message rather than the pin's compile-time-origin one (R899); an iterable with no `__iter__`, a non-`Copyable` iterator, and a runtime iterable are rejected as at the pin (assets/type_error/comptime_for_without_iter.mojo, comptime_for_non_copyable_iterator.mojo, comptime_for_runtime_iterator.mojo). `range(...)` iterates by the same protocol, as upstream's `parseParamFor` lowers every iterable: its iterator is the bundled `std.range` struct, the loop variable is upstream's `Scalar[DType.int]` element, a collector's `args.__len__()` bounds it at compile time as `Self.Ts.length` does upstream, a pack element constructed at an index a local `comptime` binding aliases (`comptime k = i`, `Ts[k]()`) is selected per copy, and an element type that names the variable is spelled in diagnostics by its `paramfor_next_value` expression, as the pin spells it (assets/ok/comptime_for_generic_iterator_parameter.mojo, comptime_for_element_alias_index.mojo). A collection display's element need not be a scalar or an aggregate of them: a `SIMD` element iterates too, and a display of non-`Copyable` elements is rejected, since `Array.__iter__` requires `Copyable` — at check time with Mojito's own wording, where the pin reports its `comptime assert` (R903) (assets/ok/comptime_for_display_simd_elements.mojo, assets/type_error/comptime_for_display_not_copyable.mojo). Value packs and reflection lists still iterate by Mojito's own rule (R795), and `comptime for … else` does not parse (R797). Numeric literal calculations retain arbitrary precision, runtime contexts materialize explicitly, and type/reflection handles erase. A compile-time collection (a list, set, or dictionary display, an annotated empty `{}`, or the explicit literal constructors) is not implicitly copyable: `len`, `in`, `keys()`/`values()`, and `comptime for` read it at compile time (a dictionary iterates its keys), any other method call or subscript over a compile-time value evaluates as one VM-CTFE entry (`comptime g = M.get("a").value()`; a dictionary subscript's raising `__getitem__` reports upstream's `cannot call raising function in comptime initializer`), and a runtime use crosses explicitly through `materialize[M]()` (or `materialize[expr]()` over a compile-time expression such as `names[i]`) or `comptime(expr)` — a bare runtime use reports upstream's `cannot materialize comptime value of type 'Dict[String, Int]' to runtime because it is not 'ImplicitlyCopyable'` (a list display spells `Array[T, Int(n)]`, a requested `List[T]` spells `List[T]`). Key identity is structural `CtValue` equality (exact for every prelude key type), and a VM-evaluated result must freeze (a scalar, Bool, String, tuple, fieldwise struct, or display), a fieldwise struct's fields freezing recursively, `String` and nested fieldwise structs included, so `comptime QS = [Q(1, "x"), mk(7)]`, a module or body `Q`, and a field read bound as `comptime` run as at the pin (assets/ok/comptime_struct_string_field.mojo), pointer-backed results also freeze their initialized allocation slots and pointer offsets. `Optional[Int]` (filled or empty), `Optional[String]`, nested pointer-owning structs, and requested `List`, `Dict`, and `Set` results cross through fresh allocations in both the VM and Pliron; unused capacity stays uninitialized. `materialize[xs]()` is explicit for non-implicitly-copyable results, including a list value parameter and nested lists; compile-time length, indexing, membership, and a body-local `comptime C = M.copy()` run without an implicit runtime crossing (assets/ok/ctfe_optional_result.mojo, ctfe_module_optional_constant.mojo, ctfe_pointer_struct_result.mojo, ctfe_list_result_materialize.mojo, ctfe_dict_set_result.mojo, ctfe_list_field_struct_result.mojo, ctfe_collection_result_compile_time_reads.mojo; assets/type_error/ctfe_list_result_implicit_read.mojo). Dead or cyclic pointer memory is explicitly rejected; shared-allocation identity is a recorded limit (R522). The `IsTriviallyMovable[T]`/`IsTriviallyCopyable[T]`/`IsTriviallyDeinitable[T]` predicates fold to compile-time `Bool` (declared `TrivialRegisterPassable` conformance, or base capability plus a compiler-generated lifecycle operation with recursively trivial fields, matching upstream `std.traits`); they also serve `where` clauses and conformance conditions, and are not traits — a bound spelling stays `UnknownTrait`. The pre-rename `Trivially*` spellings no longer resolve. A function-local `comptime` float binding (`comptime x = Float64(2.5)`, `comptime h = 0.25`, `comptime t = 1.-1 / 3.2`) is stored and read at run time as a `Float64`, so it prints and converts as one on both backends (`assets/ok/local_comptime_float.mojo`). A compile-time conversion between `Int`, `UInt`, `Float64`, and `Bool` (`comptime d = UInt(Int(7)) // 2`, `Bool(1)`, `UInt(Float64(2.5))`) folds by the VM's conversion rule, and an out-of-range or non-finite float-to-integer one (`Int(Float64(1e30))`) is not a compile-time value, as the pin refuses it (R856); a nested `def` reading an enclosing function's `comptime` binding is a recorded gap (roadmap R110). A local `comptime` binding over a generic declaration's binders — `comptime m = n + 1`, `comptime count = Us.length` over a type pack, `comptime k = Self.n + 1` in a generic struct's method, `comptime U = T` — is kept by the elaborator for the template, as the pin's `comptime` alias is a parameter expression: the executable check binds a value one to its parameter expression for the rest of its block, so it reads at run time, keys a `comptime if`, sizes a vector, and bounds a `comptime for`, and binds a type one as a scoped type alias with no runtime form; the body stays served by its template (`assets/ok/comptime_local_over_binder.mojo`). In a served `comptime for` body the binding may carry an annotation naming its value's own type (`comptime m: Int = i * n`, `comptime b: Bool = i > 0`; any other annotation is rejected, as the pin rejects the conversion, assets/type_error/comptime_template_binding_annotation_mismatch.mojo), or read an element of a named compile-time list or of a literal display at the index (`comptime q = xs[i]`, `[1, 2][i]`), or apply a `def` returning an `Int`, `Bool`, `String`, or `Float64` without raising to the index (`comptime w = twice(i + 1)`, `comptime s = label(i)`, the application the elaborator runs per copy, `assets/ok/pack_def_unkept_loop_template_served.mojo`), where an instance that reads past the display fails only if it reaches the read; a local type alias of a binder (`comptime U = Self.T`) keys a `comptime if`. A generic struct's method holding any of these is served by its template, a struct over value parameters (`S[n: Int]`) included (assets/ok/comptime_template_body_bindings.mojo, assets/ok/comptime_for_value_struct_method.mojo). A bound reflection handle (`comptime r = reflect[T]`, then `comptime count = r.field_count()` or `r.field_index["y"]()`) runs as a direct `reflect[T]` query does, and a lane-keyed `def` with such a binding is roadmap R261's. An annotated `comptime` binds at its declared type, module-level or local: `comptime ONE: Int32 = 1` is an `Int32`, `comptime ONES: SIMD[DType.int32, 4] = 1` splats the literal across the lanes, `comptime TWO: Float64 = 2` reads as `2.0`, and a literal that does not convert to the annotation is rejected (`assets/ok/comptime_annotated_scalar.mojo`, `assets/type_error/comptime_annotation_mismatch.mojo`); a `UInt` annotation still reads as an `Int` at each use (roadmap R98). The ownership rule for such a region is recorded (`docs/notes/comptime-region-ownership.md`): a `comptime if` is decided as an `if` with its condition opaque and a `comptime for` as a loop with its trip count unknown, which the pin's lifetime check does on its structured compile-time ops. A `comptime if` follows it on the production path: a move in the untaken arm with a use after the join, an untaken arm's read of a moved value, and an unused declaration in the untaken arm that moves all reject (assets/ownership_error/comptime_if_untaken_*.mojo), and a value one arm alone consumes is destroyed at the other arm's entry, the pin's order (conformance/probes/comptime_region_c*.mojo). A template-served `comptime for` follows it too: a move in a `range(0)` body with a use after the loop, a move in a `range(1)` body without a refill, and a move with a use after reject as the pin rejects them (assets/ownership_error/comptime_for_*.mojo), a `continue` destroys the iteration's value at the `continue`, and a value one arm of a `break` consumes is destroyed at that arm's entry, the pin's order (conformance/probes/comptime_region_l*.mojo). A module constant whose initializer is a layout query (`comptime S = size_of[Pair]()`) stays the application through the check, as at the pin: a value read is the query, which the elaborator answers under the compilation's target (`print(S)`, `S * 2`), and `SIMD[DType.float32, S]` in a signature is `SIMD[DType.float32, size_of[Pair]()]`, which a `16`-lane vector does not convert to (assets/ok/comptime_layout_constant.mojo, assets/type_error/comptime_layout_constant_mismatch.mojo); constructing a vector at that width runs, as do a direct `size_of[T]()` width in a generic signature and a matching construction. A literal width remains a different checked type even when its lane count equals the layout result. A `comptime if` whose condition asks a layout query (`size_of[Self.T]()` in a generic struct's method, `size_of[Padded]()` in a plain one, `size_of[Int]()` in `main`) is kept through the AST elaboration like a template's, carried as the MIR `comptime_branch`, and decided by the elaborator under the compilation's target, as at the pin (assets/ok/comptime_if_layout_query.mojo). A scalar `range(Int32(4))`, which the pin types through infer-only `range[dtype: DType, //]` overloads, is spelled by the checker as the construction of the linked range struct at the inferred dtype in the same check (`SemanticAdjustment::SpelledConstruction`), with no later rewrite (assets/ok/scalar_range_{zero_starting,sequential,strided}.mojo). A module constant of a one-lane SIMD type — declared (`comptime S: UInt8 = 2`, the bundled `SEEK_END`) or folded from a call (`hash(Int(1))`, an applied `-> Int32` def) — runs natively as its lane scalar (assets/ok/comptime_sized_scalar_constant.mojo, assets/ok/comptime_hash.mojo). A compile-time operator over sized-scalar constants folds by the VM's own lane rule, as at the pin (R846): `comptime b = a * 2` over a `comptime a: Float32`, `a + 10` over a `UInt8` (wrapping to `4`), and `comptime z = y * 2` over a `Scalar[DType.float32]` value parameter. |
| Comptime | `comptime assert cond[, "message"]` | Run | Upstream's `kgen.param.assert`, in a function body only (at module scope and among struct or trait members the parser reports the pin's "'comptime assert' must be inside a function"). The condition is checked as a `comptime if` condition is (`Checker::check_comptime_assert`; a runtime local is the pin's "cannot use a dynamic value in 'comptime assert' expression") and never judged by the checker: it rides the checked handoff, HIR, and MIR (`comptime.assert`, schema 1.81) to `native::mono`, which decides it with the instance's `comptime if`s — once per unrolled `comptime for` iteration — and fails a reached instance with `constraint failed: MESSAGE`; an untaken arm's, an uninstantiated function's, and an uncalled method's assertion is never judged (`failure::discharge_comptime_asserts`). For the rest of its block the condition is known, as a `comptime if` arm knows its own (`Checker::assume_condition`): its `conforms_to` atoms under `and` license the trait on a parameter, `Self.T`, or a reflected field type, and each conjunct of the residual proposition it states proves a callee's `where` clause; `or`/`not` prove nothing, and nothing before the assertion or outside its block is licensed (assets/ok/comptime_assert_proves_conformance.mojo, comptime_assert_proves_proposition.mojo, comptime_assert_judged_per_instance.mojo; assets/type_error/comptime_assert_*.mojo; assets/parse_error/comptime_assert_*.mojo). A message other than a string literal (a t-string, `String(...)`, `+`) is rejected as unsupported, and an `Int` condition (`comptime assert 3`), which the pin converts to `Bool`, is not yet lowered (both on `docs/roadmap.md`). |
| Comptime | Unified `reflect[T]` queries | Run | Struct detection (`is_struct()` is `True` for every type Mojito names — a builtin scalar, `String`, `NoneType`, a vector, a field handle over a scalar field — as at the pin, where only an MLIR primitive answers `False`: assets/ok/reflection_runtime_query.mojo), field collections, named field indexes, chainable named/by-index field handles, dependent `.T` types, and declaration-producing compile-time selection run; a query in a runtime position (`print(r.field_count())`, `reflect[Point].field_index["y"]()`, `r.field_names()[1]`) is upstream's static `Reflected[T]` call and materializes its compile-time answer where it stands, for a closed subject, a subscript of a list answer folding to the element, and `field_names()` called in place (`var names = reflect[T].field_names()`) being the `Array` of the names sized by the field count; a runtime read through a `comptime` binding of `field_names()` (`names[i]`, `len(names)`) is rejected as the pin rejects any compile-time `Array`, and `materialize[names[i]]()` or `materialize[names]()` crosses explicitly (assets/ok/reflection_field_name_materialize.mojo; a field name materializes as `String`, not the pin's `StringSpan[ImmStaticOrigin]`, and an uncalled template's read is not rejected: R272, R273); over a subject that is still a parameter the same queries validate symbolically (the Comptime control-flow row above), and in a template-served body — a generic `def`'s, or a generic struct's method over `reflect[Self.T]` or `reflect[Self]` — a value read (`field_count()`, `is_struct()`, `field_index[name]()`, `len(field_names())`, `field_names()[i]`, in a runtime position, a runtime `if`, a bound `comptime n`, or a `comptime if` condition) reaches MIR as a parameter constant the elaborator answers per instance from the bound struct, by the policy the checker applies to a closed subject (`ReflectQuery::answer`), and a field the struct lacks fails the instantiation as at the pin (assets/ok/reflection_template_served.mojo, assets/type_error/reflection_template_unknown_field.mojo); a field type a `conforms_to` arm proves `Defaultable` is constructed and annotated there too — `types[i]()`, `FT()` over `comptime FT = types[i]`, `x: types[i]` — as the default of the instance's field type in each unrolled copy, with no clone (assets/ok/reflection_field_type_construction.mojo), though `x: types[i] = {}` is still rejected (R443) and a closed subject's loop in a non-generic `def` (`reflect[Holder]` in `main`) does not see the arm's proof (R444); `materialize[names[i]]()` over a parameter is the runtime value of its operand there, a bare runtime read through the bound list (`print(names[i])`, `print(len(names))`) is rejected there with the pin's diagnosis (assets/type_error/comptime_field_names_runtime_use.mojo), a `comptime if` over a bound handle (`r.field_count() == 2`) compiles as the direct query does, and `comptime for name in reflect[T].field_names()` iterates the names, in a plain `def` over a closed struct too (assets/ok/reflection_comptime_for_served.mojo); a whole field-name list materialized over a parameter (`materialize[names]()`), or the query called in place, is the `Array` of the names each instance constructs from its parameter value, in a generic `def` and a generic struct's method, with no clone (assets/ok/reflection_list_materialized_whole.mojo), while a whole field-type list (`materialize[types]()`) is still rejected where the pin reads its length (roadmap R497); `reflect[T]` is a compiler builtin where upstream's is a stdlib struct (roadmap R324); ABI offsets are native-backend facts and field references use checked projections. |
| Comptime | Compile-time calls through the elaborator below MIR | Run | A call of a module `def`, a struct construction, a static method, or a method of a display in a compile-time position — a local `comptime` binding, a `comptime if` condition, a `comptime for` bound or sequence, a `comptime(e)` or `materialize[...]()` operand, a module constant's initializer — is an application the check binds as a parameter expression (`ParamKind::Apply`; a thunk MIR lifts when the expression is not a bare call, named `$comptime$<name>$module` for a module constant) and `native::mono` evaluates on first demand by running the instance's concrete MIR on the VM, fuel bounded per compilation (`Specializer.fuel`). A scalar or applied module constant is read in a body as a `ParamValue` fact lowered as `Const::Param`, and so is a local `comptime` binding of an application (`comptime s = make(3)`), which has no slot: every read materializes a fresh value that dies after the read, a field read (`s.v`) extracts from the application, and a nested `def` reads the binding without capturing it (assets/ok/comptime_local_application_per_read.mojo; a whole read of one that is not `ImplicitlyCopyable` is the pin's crossing error, assets/type_error/comptime_local_struct_application_crossing.mojo); a `String`, tuple, or fieldwise struct result is constructed at the read's register type, a list result feeds a `comptime for`, a subscript (`XS[0]`), or `materialize[XS]()`, and a raising call is rejected at the binding. No compile-time call is evaluated above the check (`Elab::eval` folds closed values only), and a generic `comptime` alias body that applies a callable is refused (R518). Every module collection display constant (`comptime L = [1, 2, 3]`, `comptime M = {"a": 1}`), closed or not, and every tuple display that applies a callable, is one application, evaluated once whatever reads it, never respelled at a read, and typed by its annotation when it has one (`comptime L: List[Int] = [1, 2]`); a lifted initializer reads it whole (`comptime S = total(L)` over `Array[Int, n]`, `comptime N = size(M)`), where it was undefined before; a closed `DType`, vector, tuple, or struct module constant is a folded parameter value at every read, a parameter default and a bracket argument (`show[V]()`) included, and the elaborator spells no module constant into a body; a module alias over a `DType` constant (`comptime V4 = SIMD[D, 4]`) resolves; a raising subscript of a dictionary constant in a local `comptime` binding of a raising body is the pin's "cannot call raising function in comptime initializer"; a `Dict[K, V, H](...)`/`Set[T, H](...)` constructor binding, module or local, a local display binding (`comptime l = [1, 2, 3]`, `comptime d = {"a": 1}`) in any body, and a closed field-name list (`reflect[P].field_names()`) are applications the check binds as well, so a lifted call (`total(l)`, `size(D)`) or a `comptime(e)` operand reads one whole, and the elaborator spells no collection into a read (assets/ok/comptime_local_display_applications.mojo); a method call over a local display binding is an application whose collection result crosses only through `materialize` (assets/type_error/comptime_local_display_method_crossing.mojo); a run-time read of a whole list, dictionary, or set constant is rejected as at the pin, a body declared before the constant reads it, an alias of an applied constant (`comptime E = D`) is that constant, and a module method call or subscript over a closed collection constant (`comptime C = M.copy()`) is an application too. |
| Control flow | `match`/`case` patterns, `if case`/`while case`, `comptime match` | Run (subset) | The full case-pattern grammar parses (`docs/grammar.md` §match_stmt): literals, `== expr`, `_`, bare/`var`/`ref` bindings, tuple, sequence (`[a, *rest, b]`), struct (`Point(x=0)`), contextual `{x=0}`, enum-case (`.Some(x)`, `Optional.None`), OR, `as`, guards, both case layouts; `case pattern = value` conditions of `if`/`elif`/`while`; and `comptime match`. A `match` statement runs with literal, `== expr`, `_`, binding, `as`, tuple, struct, contextual field, enum-case, and `|` patterns and with guards (`checker/patterns.rs`): a place subject is borrowed once into a hidden `$match<id>` reference and a temporary subject materialized once into a hidden `$match<id>` owner that lasts through the match, cases are tried in order and the first match's body runs, a statement no case matches falls through, a literal or `== expr` pattern is the subject's `__eq__` call, and neighbouring cases whose first command tests a spelled literal or an enum case on one path form upstream's cluster (`checker/clusters.rs`, `findClusterSize`/`emitCaseCluster`): the leading value, and an enum's discriminant, is read once, the test runs once per distinct key in key order — a literal's spelling bytewise, so `match 4` over `case 4` / `case 0x4` runs `0x4`, or an enum case's index — and the matching key's cases run their remaining tests before their bindings, in case order, while an `== expr` after a spelled literal joins its cluster under the empty spelling, which sorts first, and a run led by `== expr` or by a binding stays in case order (`assets/ok/match_literal_clusters.mojo`, `match_enum_tag_clusters.mojo`, `match_cluster_lifecycle.mojo`), and a bare `case name` — `case Int` included, it is a binding, not a type test — binds an immutable reference to the subject that is scoped to its case, so a write to it or a use after the match is rejected; a function whose last case is refutable or guarded is not concluded by returns in every case (`assets/ok/match_literal_cases.mojo`, `match_equality_effects_and_bindings.mojo`; `assets/type_error/match_*`). `case var x` copies the subject by the implicit-copy rule before the guard runs and the copy dies at its last use, which may precede the guard call; `case ref x` and `pattern as x` keep the subject's own capability, writing through a mutable place and reading a temporary, and a `ref` binding of a register-passable temporary subject is rejected as having no memory origin; a guard runs once the case's bindings exist and `False` advances to the next case; `continue`, `break`, and `return` in a case body leave the enclosing loop or function, and an error raised by a comparison, a guard, or a body propagates through the enclosing `try`, destroying a temporary subject on the way out (`assets/ok/match_guards_and_var_bindings.mojo`, `match_ref_and_as_bindings.mojo`, `match_exits_in_loops.mojo`, `match_raises_and_finally.mojo`; `assets/type_error/match_var_binding_not_copyable.mojo`, `match_ref_binding_register_temporary.mojo`). A tuple pattern `(p0, p1)` needs a tuple subject of its arity and compiles each element's pattern over `$match<id>[i]`, so element bindings borrow or copy the element; a struct pattern `Point(x=0, y=yv)` needs a subject of its head's nominal type, takes no positional pattern, names each field once, and compiles each field's pattern over `$match<id>.field`, a field it omits matching anything; `{x=1}` is the same over the subject's own type; `ref` field bindings keep the field's origin; every test of a pattern runs before any of its bindings materializes, so a failed alternative copies nothing; `p0 | p1` tries its alternatives in order as an inner match, the alternatives bind the same `var` names (the first declares, the later assign the same binding, with one type) and `as` after a `|` binds the whole, while a bare or `ref` binding inside an alternative is rejected (R1246) (`assets/ok/match_tuple_patterns.mojo`, `match_or_alternatives_bind.mojo`, `match_literal_forms.mojo`, `match_or_failed_alternative_copies.mojo`, `match_struct_and_initializer_patterns.mojo`, `match_equality_effects.mojo`, `match_noncopyable_subject.mojo`; `assets/type_error/match_tuple_*`, `match_struct_*`, `match_or_*`, `match_binding_scope.mojo`). A place subject is borrowed to read unless a case binds it by `ref` or `as`, so guards and bodies may read the place themselves. Coverage is nonfatal and ports upstream's covering spaces over each case's recorded command shapes (`checker/coverage.rs`): a closed `EnumLike` subject — `Optional`, a conformer whose `_enum_is_exhaustive` is `True`, and `Bool` with the cases `False` and `True` — is its constructors, one per `_enum_case_names` index (a repeated name's later index is its own constructor); an open conformer (`_enum_is_exhaustive = False`) keeps an unknown remainder only an unguarded catch-all closes, so it finds a repeated case but never reports missing ones; a tuple or struct whose every leaf is a closed `EnumLike` is its Cartesian cells, never per-field masks (an omitted struct field is a wildcard); and any other subject, a product with an `Int` leaf included, is the set of root literal spellings compared so far (`4` and `0x4` differ, `== 4` spells `4`, a `Float64` keeps `0.50`), which finds repeats and proves nothing. A constructor or cell is covered only by a pattern matching all of it: `.Some(0)` refines `Some` without covering it, a `.Some(0)` after `.Some(_)` is unreachable, `|` credits each alternative, and a guard adds nothing (a guarded case warns only once everything is covered). A product above 1024 cells is not tracked (its count is checked before each multiplication) and warns `'match' is too complex to check for exhaustivity; add a '_' case` unless a catch-all closes it; an `_enum_is_exhaustive` that does not fold is rejected with the pin's text, and `None` is no literal pattern (`case None` is rejected with the pin's text; `.None` matches the case). A case earlier ones cover warns `case is unreachable; …`, naming the repeated constructor or literal for a simple root pattern; a closed space the cases leave values of warns `'match' is not exhaustive; missing case(s) for …`, naming at most eight values with an Oxford comma and counting the rest (`, and 7 more`) — the pin's texts. A match whose cases cover the subject concludes it: every case keeps its tests and the fall-through is sealed `hlcf.unreachable`, so nothing falls through, a `|` whose alternatives bind included (`case (True, var x) | (False, var x): return x` returns on every path). A case earlier ones cover is checked but sealed, as upstream's `hlcf.unreachable`: its region runs nothing, and return, named-result, `self`-field, explicit-destroy, and uninitialized-variable analyses skip it, so a `.Some(x)` / `.None` match whose cases return or initialize a variable satisfies them (`assets/ok/match_coverage_warnings.mojo`, `match_coverage_enums_and_products.mojo`, `match_coverage_enum_like_conformers.mojo`, `match_exits_and_return_analysis.mojo`; `assets/type_error/match_enum_exhaustive_not_static.mojo`, `match_none_literal_pattern.mojo`). Literal patterns key on the source spelling the lexer keeps on each literal token. The pin also warns `unreachable code after match case that always completes` over dead regions, which Mojito does not (R1242). Warnings are `CheckedProgram::warnings()` / `CompiledProgram::warnings()`, in the pin's emission order — statements in source order, each match's case warnings in case order before its own missing-case warning, so an enclosing match's precede a nested one's — and the CLI prints each as `warning: FILE:LINE: message` on stderr after a successful compile. `if case pattern = value:` / `elif case …` / `while case …` run through the same commands (`check_case_condition`): the right-hand side is anchored and evaluated only when its branch is reached, the pattern's bindings exist in that branch's body alone (a sibling branch, the `else`, or the statement after sees none), a failed match takes the next branch or the `else`, and `while case` anchors its subject at every header visit, a failed match running the loop's `else` while `break` skips it (`assets/ok/if_case_elif_chains_and_while_case.mojo`, `if_case_mixed_with_boolean_elif.mojo`; `assets/type_error/if_case_binding_not_*`). A fixed-length sequence pattern `[p0, p1]` (`[]`, `[x]`) needs a subject whose type conforms to `Sized` by declaration (a type parameter by its bounds; `String`, and a struct with `__len__` that does not declare `Sized`, are rejected with the pin's texts; an `Int`, `UInt`, `Float64`, or `SIMD` subject, which crashes the pin, is rejected as unsupported): it tests the length once per case through upstream's `std.builtin._stubs.list_length_matches(subject, arity, True)`, a wrong length advancing before any element is read, then reads each element through the subject's own subscript (`__getitem__`, raising as it raises) once per case and per `|` alternative, at the first command that needs it and in pattern order, `_` reading nothing; a reference result binds by reference, an owned result is an rvalue a lone `var` binding takes by move and that `ref`/`as` cannot name when register-passable; an element is untyped, as at the pin, so a tuple, struct, field, or nested sequence pattern inside one is rejected, while a sequence pattern inside a tuple pattern runs; sequence cases cover nothing, as at the pin, so only a catch-all concludes them (`assets/ok/match_sequence_fixed_length.mojo`, `if_case_and_while_case.mojo`; `assets/type_error/match_sequence_*`). A starred pattern `[u, *m, v, w]` tests a minimum length (`list_length_matches(subject, 3, False)`), reads an element before the star by index and one after it at `subject[subject.__len__() - k]`, and matches the star's closed pattern (`*_`, `*rest`, `*var rest`) against the subject's own slice `subject[1:subject.__len__() - 2]` (`subject[1:]` when the star is last), each `__len__` read afresh as upstream's path values are and each path read once at the first command that needs it, so `*_` slices nothing; over a `List` the slice is a `Span` borrowing it, a temporary subject lives until the remainder's last use, and the slice is untyped, as an element is, so a tuple or sequence pattern under the star is rejected (`assets/ok/match_sequence_custom_sized.mojo`, `sequence_starred_evaluation_order.mojo`, `sequence_starred_forms.mojo`, `sequence_starred_temporary_lifetime.mojo`; `assets/type_error/match_sequence_star_*`, `assets/ownership_error/match_sequence_star_source_mutated.mojo`). HIR lowers a chain holding a pattern branch as one-armed conditionals, each branch's test in the previous one's else region, a pattern branch a one-case `hlcf.match`. The pin moves, rather than copies, a `var` binding that is the subject's last use; Mojito still copies it (roadmap). HIR emits the cases as the check's recorded emission plans them, upstream's `emitCases`: a list that is one cluster as its `hlcf.if` (an elif per further key, its else arm the trailing complement), one case whose commands all bind inline, and any other list as the `match` region op (`hlcf.match`), whose case regions exit by `match.next`/`match.complete`; a nested residual match inside an enclosing case completes that case where the pin falls through (`docs/non-goals.md`). An enum-case pattern `.Case(p)` / `Owner.Case(p)` over a subject whose type declares the bundled `EnumLike` (found by the trait's identity, never its spelling) finds the case's index by name in the subject's `_enum_case_names` (a repeated name selects its first index), tests it against `_get_enum_discriminant()` (an `EnumTag` command), then matches its one positional payload pattern over `_unsafe_get_enum_payload[case]()`, read once per case and per `|` alternative at the first command that needs it, so `.Some(_)` and a bare tag test `.Some` / `Optional.Some` never call the getter; the payload type comes from `_enum_case_types`, read only as far as a payload pattern needs, so a nullary case past a short list runs. The payload is typed, so tuple, nested-enum, literal, struct, and `as` patterns run inside it; a bare payload binding is immutable, `ref` writes through a place subject, and a temporary subject's payload is read immutably, as a `ref self` getter over an rvalue is at the pin. A qualified owner is checked at the root alone and nominally, parameters ignored (`Optional[String].Some(q)` over an `Optional[Int]` binds the `Int`, nested owners go unchecked), as upstream's. Over a subject that is no `EnumLike` a bare qualified head is an equality test (`case DType.float32`) and a call-shaped head a struct pattern. Rejections carry the pin's texts: an unknown case, a payload on a `NoneType` case, empty parentheses, keyword or extra payloads, a wrong root owner, a call head that is no member, a too-short `_enum_case_types`, a parametric `T: EnumLike` subject, and a leading-dot head over a non-enum subject (`assets/ok/match_enum_cases_optional.mojo`, `match_enum_cases_custom.mojo`; `assets/type_error/match_enum_case_*`). An `Optional` subject whose payload is not trivial is copied by its discriminant's truthiness test (R1249); over a generic `Optional[T]` tag tests run but a payload loses `T`'s bounds (R1236); and a getter on a field of a place subject no case binds by reference conflicts with the anchor (R1240). `comptime match` runs as upstream's `hlcf.comptime.if` over its cases (`Checker::check_comptime_match`): the subject is a compile-time value — a type or value parameter, a `comptime` binding, a `comptime for` variable, and `Self.n` are read where they are, and any other subject, a CTFE application included, is bound once as `comptime $match<id> = subject`, so it is demanded once — and each case compiles the same commands a runtime `match` does in compile-time mode: every test (`== Int` type equality, a literal, an enum tag, a sequence length) is a conjunct of the case's compile-time condition, recorded as a `comptime if` condition is, a path is its expression over the subject (struct fields, tuple and sequence elements, enum payloads), a bare or `as` binding is `comptime name = path`, a compile-time value the guard reads as the value it denotes, and `|` is the disjunction of its alternatives, a name every alternative binds being the first matching alternative's value (`v0 if c0 else v1`). HIR emits the statement as `HirInstr::ComptimeIf`, one arm per tested case a value reaches, its bindings then its body, and the concluding case the else arm, so MIR, ownership, the textual MIR, and `native::mono` see only the existing `comptime_branch`; every case body is checked, a case's body under what its condition proves, and coverage, return, initialization, and explicit-destroy analyses read the same recorded plan as a runtime `match` (`assets/ok/comptime_match_forms.mojo`, `comptime_match_patterns.mojo`, `comptime_match_coverage_warnings.mojo`, `comptime_conditions_short_circuit.mojo`). Rejections carry the pin's texts: a dynamic subject or `== value` operand ("cannot use a dynamic value in 'match' subject"), a dynamic guard, `var` and `ref` bindings, a literal over a type subject, a raising `__eq__` ("cannot call raising function in boolean condition"), an untaken case's type error, a non-concluding match ending a function with results, and module scope ("'comptime match' must be contained in a function") (`assets/type_error/comptime_match_*`, `assets/parse_error/comptime_match_module_level.mojo`). A starred sequence pattern in `comptime match` is rejected as unsupported where the pin binds the star's slice (R1243), and a case arm, like a `comptime if` arm, assumes only its own condition where upstream also assumes every earlier condition failed (R1244). `comptime if case` is a parse error (R1248). A mapping pattern (`case {"k": v}`) is rejected with the pin's "expression is not a valid match pattern; use '==' to compare against its value", as upstream has not implemented mapping patterns (`assets/parse_error/match_mapping_pattern.mojo`; `docs/non-goals.md`). |
| Bindings | Recursive destructuring targets | Run | `a, b = t`, `var a, b = t`, `(var a, (ref b, _)) = pair`, `var (x, (y, z)) = e`, `var (p, q), r = (7, 8), 9`, `(a, (b, c)) = v`, and struct-field places (`(p.x, p.y) = pair()`) run through one extraction plan (`checker/destructure.rs`, `CheckedTupleUnpackElement` trees): the destinations' index expressions are evaluated before the right-hand side, the value once, each element read through the nominal `Tuple` accessor and copied (implicit-copy rule) or bound by reference (`ref` leaves write through), and a nested tuple's elements through a hidden reference slot (`assets/ok/destructure_nested_targets.mojo`, `destructure_swap_and_places.mojo`; arity, undeclared-name, and literal-target rejections in the error folders). A `for` statement's or comprehension clause's target reuses this plan through the loop's hidden binder (see the Control flow row). One gap is filed: a nominal subscript leaf (`xs[i]`) is rejected ("a nominal subscript (`xs[i]`) as a destructuring target") until the `__setitem__` path serves it. |
| Bindings | Sequence destructuring targets, starred included | Run | `var [a, b] = values`, `[p, q] = mk`, `[xs[idx(0)], xs[idx(1)]] = make()`, `[var m, ref r, s] = ys`, and nested `var [(c, d), [e, _]] = rows` run as upstream's list-pattern store, a checked desugar into ordinary statements (`checker/destructure.rs`, spliced by `checker/desugar.rs`): the value is evaluated once (a place borrowed to read, a temporary held until its last read), it must declare `Sized` (the pattern gate's two texts), the raising `std.builtin._stubs.check_list_length` runs before any store (so the statement needs a raising context, and a wrong length is caught by `try` with the targets untouched), then each element is read through `value.__getitem__(i)` and stored in order, a target's own subexpressions evaluating after its read. `_` still reads its element, an owned getter result moves into a `var` leaf, a `ref` leaf binds its element to read, a bare name must already be declared, and a subscript leaf must yield a mutable reference (`expression must be mutable in collection literal`, never `__setitem__`); a `Tuple` value has no `__getitem__` and rejects (`assets/ok/destructure_sequence_targets.mojo`, `destructure_list_wrong_length_raises.mojo`; `destructure_sequence_*` in the error folders). A starred target `var [a, *mid, b, c] = values` checks a minimum length (`check_list_length(value, 3, False)`), reads `__len__` once more for the suffix start `len - 2`, then stores in order the prefix elements, the star's slice — the ordinary subscript `value[1:start]` (`value[1:]` when the star is last), so a `List` gives a `Span` borrowing it — and the suffix elements `__getitem__(start + k)`; the star takes any target (`*_`, which still slices, `*var m`, a place, a nested `*[g, *h]`), a temporary value lives until the remainder's last use, mutating or moving the source before that use is rejected, a value without a slice `__getitem__` rejects, and a list display value (natively an `Array`) waits for R1126 (`assets/ok/destructure_starred_slice.mojo`, `sequence_starred_evaluation_order.mojo`, `sequence_starred_forms.mojo`, `sequence_starred_temporary_lifetime.mojo`; `destructure_starred_*` in the error folders). A sequence target nested inside a tuple target for R1224, `Int`/`SIMD` values (which lack `__len__`/`__getitem__`) for R1223, and the aliasing swap `[xs[1], xs[0]] = xs` for R1082. |
| Control flow | `if`, `while`, `for`, `break`, `continue`, ternary | Run | Concrete ordinary iteration, and consuming iteration for a type that supplies `__iter__(var self)`, use checker-selected nominal `__iter__`/`__next__` methods. A `for` target is a recursive destructuring target, as at the pin: `for a, b in pairs`, `for (var a, _) in pairs`, `for ref (a, b) in pairs`, `for i, (var x, ref y) in nested`, `for [first, *rest] in rows`, and the place targets `for p.x, p.y in pairs` and `for q[0] in xs` run. A bare name always declares an immutable binding to its piece (upstream's `Bind` pattern), shadowing an outer name, and never copies it, so a piece that is not `ImplicitlyCopyable` binds while a `var` leaf over it is rejected; the loop binds each element to a hidden immutable binder (no copy of a yielded reference) and the checker desugars the target into the body's first statements, so a `ref` leaf of `for ref (a, b)` is read-only, a sequence target runs R1136/R1137's raising store (it needs a raising context), an owned tuple yield is destroyed after its leaves' last use (before `continue` and `break` too), and each iteration's leaves are its own for a capturing closure (`assets/ok/for_destructuring_targets.mojo`, `for_destructuring_lifetimes.mojo`, `for_destructuring_closure_capture.mojo`; `for_target_*`, `for_sequence_target_not_raising`, and `for_discard_target_read` in the error folders). `for _ in` binds nothing to read. A `comptime for` target stays one name (R1228), and `enumerate`/`zip` are missing (R1229); a by-value capture of a bare leaf, as of any reference binding, keeps the reference's type (R1230). A loop or comprehension target's binding convention — unadorned (immutable), `var`, or `ref` — is modeled independently of whether the source is borrowed or consumed: a `var` target moves an owned result into the loop variable or lifecycle-copies a yielded reference into owned storage (referent `ImplicitlyCopyable`), while an unadorned or `ref` target binds an owned result directly into per-iteration storage (a droppable `Deinitable` or `Copyable` element, dropped each iteration) or retains a yielded reference handle to read/write through the borrowed referent. The raw `__next__` result is retained in a compiler-owned slot and adapted to the target only on the yielded edge, so moves and copies never run on the `StopIteration` path; mutating an immutable target and a copying `var` over a non-`ImplicitlyCopyable` reference result are rejected. Borrowed sources lower uniformly — in statements and comprehensions — through a reference-bound retained-source slot and a distinct iterator-object slot, with whole-source versus interior borrowing expressed only as loan granularity. Bundled borrowed iteration covers List, Set, Dict, the range family, Span, and StringSpan through the origin-parameterized `Iterable` protocol (`__iter__(ref self) -> Self.IteratorType[origin_of(self)]`, origin erased in the bundled member templates), and a borrowed conformer may spell its receiver `self` or `ref self` — generic dispatch reaches either spelling; bundled owned iteration is currently List-specific.
