Iteration follows current Mojo's protocol only: `__next__` raises the typed `StopIteration` at exhaustion, and a nonraising `__next__` is rejected with upstream's "does not implement the '__has_next__' method" (the bounded `__len__` protocol was removed upstream); a loop over a generic `Iterable`/`IterableOwned` bound advances through the same raising contract. `Iterator`, `Iterable`, `IterableOwned`, and `StopIteration` live in `std.iter` and are prelude names that a program's own top-level declaration shadows. A loop source dies by the ASAP rule: one its iterator cannot refer to (no origin parameter, reference or pointer field, or reference-yielding `__next__`) is destroyed as soon as `__iter__` returns — named, temporary, or comprehension source alike — while a source the iterator borrows lives, and stays loaned, until the loop exits. An abstract value-yielding iterator may dispatch to a concrete reference-yielding `__next__` for a `Copyable` element; the checked adapter reads and lifecycle-copies the referent. Concrete List/Set/mapping `for ref` (named or temporary sources) retains a live interior origin through the ordinary protocol — a parametric-mut yielded reference resolves to the source's mutability at each loop site, its declared `_get_owned_interior` projection sets the loan granularity, and an infer-only `Bool` binding a sibling origin's `mut=` erases from a struct's runtime parameters like the origin itself; abstract-bound `for ref` is rejected, with generic-bound breadth tracked under generic-bound reference iteration. Mapping key yields are declaration-level immutable (`_DictKeyIter.__next__` wraps its yielded origin in `Origin[mut=False].cast_from[...]`), so a `for ref` key binding rejects writes even over a mutable mapping while reads stay live borrowed references — matching real Mojo's read-only key iteration. `for var item in collection^` consumes the source, moves non-Copyable elements, drops permitted residual elements on early exit, and rejects early abandonment of linear residuals. Protocol-driven owned iteration carries current Mojo's element bounds: the yielded element must be `Movable & Deinitable`, enforced by the bundled `__iter__(var self)` where clauses (List rejects a non-`Deinitable` element specialization with the bound named) and by a checker gate over user-declared owned iterators, so the implicitly dropped iterator always has its residual-destroying `__deinit__` available. Variadic packs are not library iterators — linear whole-pack forwarding stays supported under guaranteed exhaustion: every element must be consumed, the body may neither `break`, `return`, `raise`, nor contain a raising call whose handler sits outside the loop (a `try` inside the body contains its error; the rejection names the element's `@explicit_destroy` obligation), and comprehensions additionally reject filter clauses over a linear binder, since a skipped element would be abandoned. The pack-level escape rule remains syntactic and conservative: a `raise` statement inside a body-level `try` still counts as an escape. A condition — `if`, `while`, the conditional expression, and a comprehension filter — may be any `Boolable` struct (`if collection:`), converted through its `__bool__`, or a numeric scalar (`if n:` over an `Int`, `UInt`, `Float64`, or width-one lane), converted through `Bool(x)` as is the operand of `not` (`assets/ok/numeric_truthiness_condition.mojo`); `and`/`or` still take only `Bool` operands (roadmap R97). A conditional expression's result is an owned temporary that each arm initializes once — a `^` arm by its move initializer, a place arm by its copy initializer, which only an `ImplicitlyCopyable` value allows (`r if c else M(3)` over a merely `Copyable` `M` is rejected, as on the pin: `assets/type_error/implicit_copy_conditional_arm.mojo`), a temporary arm as it stands where the pin moves it in once more (R1107) — and its consumer takes it without another copy, as the pin does: an owned parameter or binding takes its storage, and a read parameter or receiver borrows it until the call returns (`assets/ok/conditional_expression_transfer.mojo`). A conditional expression whose condition is a parameter value — a `Bool` literal, a `Bool` value parameter (`Self.flag` included), a `comptime for` variable, or a local or module `comptime` binding, `comptime(e)`, `conforms_to(T, Trait)` or a trivial-type predicate, `not` of one, or a comparison of `Int`, `UInt`, or `Bool` parameter values — selects its arm per instance as `hlcf.comptime.if`, so a dead arm is never elaborated (a recursive call there terminates); `and`/`or`, a `Float64` comparison, and a call of an ordinary `def` stay run-time conditions, elaborating both arms, as the pin's `getIfPValue` has them (`assets/ok/conditional_expression_parameter_condition.mojo`). |
| Exceptions | `raise`, `try`/`except`/`else`/`finally` | Run | Direct, selected overload/method, indirect callable, trait-requirement, bounded-dispatch, and struct infix-operator (plain or reflected dunder) effects are checked with exact typed error contracts; inferred `except` bindings and `Never` run. A name inside a `try`, `except`, `else`, or `finally` body, read or assigned, is the binding its lexical scope declares, and each `except` name and `with … as` target is its own variable (`assets/ok/try_region_shadowed_binding.mojo`). **Limits:** a raising prefix dunder (`-x`) is not yet a raising call (`docs/roadmap.md` R719). |
| Contexts | `with` statements | Run | A checker desugar selected by the manager's declared methods (the pinned Mojo's semantics): a consuming `__enter__` (`var self`, the `FileHandle` shape, no `__exit__` allowed) hands the manager to its result, which lives to the end of the block bound or not; a plain `__exit__(self)` runs by `try`/`finally` on a normal exit, `return`, `break`, `continue`, and an escaping error; with both overloads in a raising context an error raised in the body goes to `__exit__(self, err: Error) -> Bool` — `True` swallows it, `False` re-raises it — and the plain overload runs otherwise; a manager without any `__exit__` lives to the block end, while the unbound result of a non-consuming `__enter__` is a discarded temporary destroyed before the body runs. Comma-separated items nest (a later expression sees an earlier `as` name), exits run innermost-first, and an `as` name is block-scoped and destroyed at its last use like any local. Upstream's texts reject a manager without `__enter__`, the error overload without the plain one, and a consuming `__enter__` beside an `__exit__`; a generic `__exit__[E]` is not modeled. |
| Modules | Source modules/packages, wildcard/selective, dotted/relative linking | Run | Every dotted prefix binds a namespace; ordinary directories can form namespace paths; package members require explicit import or `__init__.mojo` re-export; and a source package wins over a same-named source module. Explicit imports may repeat one target but cannot bind one local name to different declarations; an exact source-file or package-initializer self-import rejects, while distinct mutual cycles retain provisional exports. The canonical `std.traits`/`std.origin` module homes export the builtin lifecycle traits, `IsTrivially*` predicates, and origin spellings as identity bindings, so explicit `from std.traits import Deinitable` / `from std.origin import Origin` spellings (named, aliased, or wildcard) resolve while the builtins stay implicitly available; the same table gives `size_of` a home at `std.sys` and `DivModable` one at `std.math`, additively, so `std.math`'s own declarations are unaffected. A module under a search root is named by its path there (`std/io/file.mojo` links as `std.io.file`), whichever import first reaches it. An import of `std.*` resolves at the bundled library alone, as the pin binds `std` to its library package: a user `std/` beside the entry or under a search root is never read. The entry's own declaration of a name the library links bare, a compiler builtin identity, or a prelude name (`struct List`, `def range`, `struct Slice`, `struct String`) shadows the library's in the entry and takes the entry module's linked name, so the library's own uses still reach the library's; declaring a name the entry imports explicitly is the link's redefinition error, as at the pin. Compiled `.mojoc`/`.mojopkg` lookup for a user's directory remains Packaging, Artifacts, And Developer Tooling work; the bundled library is already imported from its compiled package (the Tooling row below). |
| Modules | Qualified `import module`, module/member aliases | Run | Aliased and full dotted calls, values, and types resolve without merging same-named declarations. |
| Ownership | `^` moves, partial moves, use-after-move analysis | Run | MIR owns these rules, walking each body's structured regions as upstream's `CheckLifetimes` does: an `if` joins its arms, a loop iterates its body until the states its `continue`s carry back settle, and the state at a `break` or `continue` reaches the loop it names, through any `finally` it crosses, so a move before a `break`/`continue` out of a `try` is seen after the loop or in the next iteration, as on the pin (`assets/ownership_error/try_break_move_then_use_after_loop.mojo`, `try_continue_move_reentered.mojo`), and a value or `mut` parameter refilled after such a loop is accepted (`assets/ownership_ok/try_break_partial_move_refilled_after_loop.mojo`, `try_break_mut_parameter_refilled_after_loop.mojo`). A `mut` parameter moved before a loop that never breaks, or before `abort()`, is still rejected as uninitialized at return, where the pin accepts it (roadmap R1242, R50). `try` regions take the precise raise-point rule: an `except` arm starts from the join of the states at the body's potentially-raising instructions (a value consumed before a raising call is uninitialized there, one consumed after the last raise is not), `else` and the code after the `try` start from the body's completion, a `finally` is checked from every entering channel, and a raising named destructor consumes its receiver at the call. A field moved out of a value (`p.a^`) must be written back before any other part of the value is read or written (`value 'p.a' cannot be consumed, because 'p' is used later`), before the variable is redefined, and before the function exits (`field 'p.a' destroyed out of the middle of a value, preventing the overall value from being destroyed`), as upstream; a `deinit` parameter's direct fields are independent values and may be moved out singly, while a move below one of them is an ordinary hole. Reading the moved field itself, or the whole value, stays `use of uninitialized value`. Definite and path-dependent transfers alike report upstream's `use of uninitialized value 'x'`. A `^` bound to a read parameter or a read receiver lends its place instead of moving it, as upstream: the source stays live and is destroyed at its own last use, while a `^` into a `var` parameter moves and a `^` into a `mut`/`ref` parameter is rejected. A `^` whose source is rooted at an immutable binding — a parameter or `self` with no owning convention, a field of one, or a `for`/comprehension binder — is rejected by the checker (`cannot transfer out of immutable reference`), as upstream: the body does not own that storage. A trivial register value (`Int`, `Bool`, a float, a SIMD value) transfers as a copy there, where upstream only warns. A `mut` parameter or `mut self` receiver is the caller's storage: `x^` takes the value out through the reference, and the body must write a value back before it leaves, whole or field by field, on every return (`'x' is uninitialized at return from this function`) and at every raise (`use of uninitialized value 'x'`), as upstream; a read in between is a use of an uninitialized value, a write-back destroys what an earlier write-back stored, and a value that owns no storage transfers as a copy (`assets/ok/mut_parameter_transfer_refilled.mojo`). A `TrivialRegisterPassable` struct is still moved there, where upstream copies it (roadmap R121), and a `mut` capture is treated like a `mut` parameter, where upstream refuses the transfer (roadmap R120). A `comptime if` arm and a template-served `comptime for` body are analysed as the `if` and the loop of the same shape, with the condition opaque and the trip count unknown, so a move in an untaken arm or in a loop body the instantiation runs zero or one times rejects as the pin rejects it (`assets/ownership_error/comptime_if_untaken_*.mojo`, `comptime_for_zero_iterations_move.mojo`, `comptime_for_body_move_without_refill.mojo`), and the join of the arms and the loop body decide as the pin does (`assets/ownership_ok/comptime_if_conditional_use_then_after.mojo`, `comptime_for_loop_carried_refill.mojo`; `assets/ownership_error/comptime_if_other_arm_moves_after_return.mojo`, `comptime_for_body_move_then_use.mojo`). A value live after a region is not destroyed when the region raises out of the function (roadmap R20). |
| Borrowing | Local loans, interior origins, and origin-bearing cross-call references | Run | A temporary lives as long as its borrower: a `ref[self]`-returning call on a temporary receiver chains for the statement (the temporary is materialized in a hidden frame slot), a subscript view (`s[byte=a:b]`, a `Span` slice) passed straight to a method, free call, or construction at its source's last use keeps the source alive until the call returns (an assignment back over the source in the same statement is judged by the call-result aliasing rule, not by that loan), and a plain `self` method writes through a mutable-origin `Pointer` field (`self.p[] = v`; the pointer is read, its origin carries the capability). A pointer a call returns, dereferenced in place (`idp(p)[]`, `h.get()[]`, `first(h)[]` over a generic holder), loans the owner its origin names until the read, as binding the result first would; a subtree origin (the extension) reads through the hidden slot's handle. A pointer a method returns is a shared alias like `Pointer(to=x)`, so copies of one held in a generic struct's parameter-typed field coexist with the holder. Frame/slot handles execute reborrows, free and receiver-projected returns, unions, captured indexes, and reference-valued aggregates. Direct, lifted indirect, method, callable-receiver, and handwritten-constructor ABIs retain positional and keyword caller places; synchronous `try` execution mirrors the caller's real frame identity, so projected or aggregate reference returns and mutations on both normal and raising paths have the same semantics as continuation-driven calls. Pointer-backed and self-hosted List projections remain executable through those handles. Bare `ref` parameters/receivers propagate caller mutability but cannot assume write permission; an explicit mutable origin is required to write. Named interior generations (`"element"`/`"value"`), including every member of a union return, let owner reads, overlapping element aliases, and direct List element writes coexist; structural mutation, nested-interior or whole-owner replacement through direct/reference/pointer access, mutable calls, Dict lookups, and Variant replacement invalidate matching old generations across branches, loops, and exact structured-exception channels; the interior check is part of the move scan's walk of the structured regions (upstream's `TrackedAndInteriorLiveness`), so a mutation before a `break`/`continue` out of a `try` stales a view used after the loop or in the next iteration, as on the pin (`assets/ownership_error/list_element_view_stale_after_break_from_try.mojo`, `list_element_view_stale_after_continue_from_try.mojo`). A view a method returns from a field receiver (`self.name.strip()` in a `mut self` method, `h.name.strip()` through a local or a `mut` parameter) lends that field's named interior (`self.name["bytes"]`): a store or mutable access over the field rejects the view's next use as an invalidated interior reference, as the pin does, while reads of the field and stores to its siblings coexist; a bare-variable receiver (`s.strip()`) still takes a whole-place loan, rejected at the store (`docs/roadmap.md` R266). Consuming an aggregate that carries an interior-origin loan requires the origin to still be live (current Mojo's rule): moving a stale view or passing it to a named explicit destructor rejects as a use of the invalidated interior reference, while a scope-end implicit drop of a stale view stays legal. The dual holds for owners: a whole-variable move (`dealloc(a^)`, a `var` argument, a rebind) invalidates every interior generation rooted at the moved variable, so a tracked pointer or view into it rejects on its next use as a use of the moved owner (`use of uninitialized value 'ys'`), as the pin reports it (`assets/ownership_error/list_element_view_used_after_list_moved.mojo`). Direct `ref` struct fields are a Mojito extension slated for removal (upstream rejects them — `'ref' patterns are only valid on the left side of an assignment` — and spells reference storage through `Pointer`; see `docs/non-goals.md`). A `ref[origin] T` field or binding whose origin is a struct/callable origin *parameter* may be returned from a method — directly (`self.value`) or by indexing/projecting through it (`self.src[i]` on a `ref[origin] List` field): the handle already names its borrowed region, so the return stays within the declared origin (an immutable origin yields a read-only borrow, a mutable origin a write-through handle) instead of escaping as a receiver-rooted place. The VM re-roots the returned handle at the borrowed storage so it survives the accessor frame. Reading a `ref[origin] <aggregate>` field's referent — subscripting it, taking its `len`, and similar — works under a `mut`/`ref self` receiver exactly as under `def self` (a borrowed receiver is a runtime alias, and the field load now dereferences to the referent rather than yielding its stored handle). A reference returned from a struct method and bound to a `ref` local keeps its source alive: the returned reference's struct origin parameter resolves to the origin the receiver's `ref[o]` field borrows, so the loan roots at the ultimate owner and it is not dropped while the reference is live (a `mut self` reference-yielding accessor reads and writes through end-to-end). Dereferencing an origin-bearing *pointer* field and returning it (`self.p[0]`, with `p: UnsafePointer[T, o]`) executes: the returned reference stays within the pointer's origin parameter, and the VM re-roots the handle at the single pointee — `UnsafePointer(to=v)` is a handle straight at `v` — forwarding the offset-0 index to it (an immutable origin reads, a mutable origin writes through the source). A `for` loop over a user-defined iterator whose `__next__` returns a reference into the borrowed source executes: the loop calls `__iter__`/`__next__` with the loop frame reachable, and a borrowed `__iter__(ref self)` receives a `ref self` handle, so a user iterator holding a `ref` into the loop frame resolves it and the yielded reference flows through the loop as a handle (owned-temporary source, dropped once after the loop; a named source binds the source slot to a genuine reference and is recorded as a whole-source shared loan on the iterator, so it is not copied, survives the loop without the `KeepAlive` hack, and mutating it during iteration is rejected as a loan conflict). The concrete `__next__` target, raising effect, and executable result convention cross the checked/HIR/MIR boundary together, so a reference yield is typed and verified as `TyKind::Ref` rather than carried dynamically through a register typed as its referent. An abstract value-result `__next__` may retarget to that concrete reference ABI only for an identical `Copyable` referent; its explicit adapter reads from the identified returned frame and runs the copy lifecycle before producing the promised value. Static, untracked, and unsafe-any contracts lower explicitly, and `UnsafePointer(to=place)` attaches executable owner loans to pointers and pointer-storing aggregates; those loans are shared aliases, so any number of pointers to one owner (or through one `ref` binding) coexist. This is currently stricter than Mojo: Mojito rejects mutation of the designated owner while such a pointer remains live, whereas the pinned nightly permits it; the loan check walks the structured regions but treats a `try` as one use of everything it reads, so an access or loan inside a `try` is not loan-checked. A bare owned place (a local, or an owned field) auto-borrows into a `ref` constructor parameter — `View(source, i)` needs no prior `ref` binding — installing the same source loan, escape rejection, and origin-mutability check the explicit reborrow gets. A temporary auto-borrows too (upstream's temporary-lifetime rule, pin-attested): `View(make_list(), 0)` and `ref x = make_list()` materialize the rvalue into a hidden owned slot whose loans give it the borrower's lifetime, and a view of a temporary may not escape the frame. Parameter origin clauses accept upstream's qualified `Self.o` binder spelling alongside the bare binder. Upstream's iterator-storage shape executes: a `Pointer[T, Self.o]` field stores `Pointer(to=xs)` of a `ref[Self.o] xs` constructor parameter (the pointer carries the declared binder, so the store type-checks by identity; a pointer to a plain read parameter still rejects — the initializer's binder is fixed), a handwritten initializer records the pointer field's construction-time source like a ref field's, a call whose result carries an origin-bearing pointer field lends its `ref`-bound and aggregate place arguments to the result (so the returned view keeps its source alive and re-roots at it), and dereferencing a pointer-to-collection field then indexing (`self.src[][r]`) reads the element — the dereference is its own identity reference projection, distinct from element 0. A write through a pointer field whose origin binder has symbolic mutability (`Origin[mut=m]`, or a bare `Origin`) is judged per binding, as upstream judges it per instantiation: the binder resolves through the holder's construction-time origins (`var p = P(Pointer(to=xs))`, a `ref[Self.o]` constructor, a field of such a value, an `ImmOrigin(o)` application — kept through a field chain, so `Wrap(Cell[ImmOrigin(…)](…))` rejects at `w.cell.src[][0]` too) or, for a view a call returns (`make(xs).src[][0] = 9`, `h.view().src[][0]`, a local bound from either, and a field chain below it), through the callee's return contract: `-> P[origin_of(xs)]` binds the slot to the argument place with the callee's own capability, so a `mut` parameter or `mut self` writes through while a read parameter or receiver rejects whatever the caller's `var` allows — a mutable source writes through, an immutable one rejects with `expression must be mutable in assignment` (`… for in-place operator destination` for `+=`), and a binder no binding resolves — the generic body itself, a `Pointer[T, o]` parameter — rejects too, as upstream rejects the generic body. A call result used as the holder of a dereferenced pointer field materializes as a hidden owned slot for the access, so reads and writes through it, and a whole store `make(b).src[] = Box(11)`, run on both backends, and so do a field store below the dereferenced pointer field (`p.src[].v = 9`) and a field store or builtin augmented assignment below a nominal reference-returning subscript (`xs[i].field = v`, `xs[i].n += 1`, `h.items[i].inner.n = v`): the selected `__getitem__` is evaluated once into a hidden `ref` handle, the store goes through it, and a replaced droppable field is destroyed at the store. The dereference of a pointer field takes the pointer's own capability, never the holder binding's, so a plain `self` method writes through a `MutOrigin` field with `+=` as well. A method (or subscript) chained directly onto a temporary borrowing view retains the view in a hidden slot whose loans keep the source alive and conflict-checked across the chained call (`b.pane().first()`, nested view-to-view chains); `mut self` methods on temporaries stay rejected. A method result whose loans arrive only through a type argument — `spans.pop()` on a `List[Span[Int, o]]`, as its template's `-> Self.T` declares it, in ordinary code and in the per-instantiation clone alike — is an element, not a view of its receiver: it does not borrow the list, which may be mutated while the popped value lives (the element's own loans are not carried by the read either: `docs/roadmap.md` R93). Subscript writes through a parametric-mut ref field (`Origin[mut=m]`) are accepted inside the generic body and judged per instantiation: each call site of a method that writes through such a field resolves the receiver's construction-time origin binding — a mutable source writes through, an immutable source rejects, and a receiver that leaves the parameter symbolic (a wrapper generic over the same origin) rejects because transitive write-requirement propagation is not supported. Upstream's expression-origin ref return is accepted for delegated call projections: `def __next__(mut self) raises StopIteration -> ref [self.iter.step(1).key] K` resolves the origin structurally from the callee's declared contract — argument-taking callees are legal (the clause's origin depends only on the receiver walk; arguments are checked per call), and multi-origin-binder correspondences resolve through the field application's recorded binder bindings (`var second: EntryCursor[Self.o2]` links the callee's binder to `o2`, and the returned reference loans exactly the resolved source). The field application may be spelled through the declaring struct's own comptime alias (`var iter: Self.dict_entry_iter`, or a parameterized `Self.view_t[o2]` whose body may also name the struct's binders directly): the binder correspondence is read from the alias body in source form, and multi-hop receiver paths compose the per-field records. A clause rooted at a bare carrier *parameter* (`def first_key(c: EntryCursor) -> ref[c.current().key] Int`) resolves the callee's binder to the carrier: the call site loans the sources the carrier holds (its construction-time origins — upstream's exact origin; the executable handle re-roots at that storage), so the source must outlive the reference and, as with every Mojito loan, may not be mutated while it lives. Subset: one delegated call per origin clause, receiver rooted at `self`/a parameter through fields, the callee's contract already declared, and exactly one ref-returning callee overload; construction-time field origins are recorded per top-level field, so a reference delegated through a nested multi-binder carrier loans every source that carrier holds. The stdlib dict key/value iterators delegate stepping to the wrapped entry iterator in upstream shape. A signature origin clause REQUIRES upstream's qualified `Self.o` spelling for struct origin parameters (pin-attested: the bare binder rejects with "unqualified access to struct parameter 'o'; use 'Self.o' instead"), in direct, projected, and cast-wrapped clause forms; function-, method-, and alias-own origin binders stay bare (compiler-reserved `__`-prefixed synthesized binders too), and ref-field annotations accept both spellings (`ref[o]` and `ref[Self.o]`). A ref-field view returned from a method with a plain (read) `self` receiver, or from a free function borrowing a place argument, keeps its borrow contract end-to-end: the receiver passes as a caller-place handle whenever the result type structurally carries a reference, and free-function calls install the same caller-side view loans as methods, and a view returned through a plain read-convention *parameter* (of a free function or a method) works the same way: the call retains that argument place as a shared read — the ownership analysis classifies a retained place by the callee's convention at that slot, so it does not conflict with live shared loans — and the VM binds the parameter to the caller's storage. A loan-carrying temporary constructor result used as a function-call argument (direct or through a callable value) is anchored for the full statement — including a `return` value or branch condition, which lower as terminator operands — so its borrowed sources survive the consuming call; and a heap-backed `Copyable` field projected off a reference-returning call result in value position runs its copy lifecycle instead of aliasing the source allocation. |
| Borrowing | Cross-call loan transfer | Run | A callable body's accepted store of a loan-carrying value into `self` or a `mut`/`ref` parameter is recorded as a transfer effect (`dest`/`src` in signature-origin terms), inferred at the store-outward acceptance point for user code and seeded for the bundled `List.append`/`insert`/`__setitem__`, whose pointer-mediated stores bypass that path. A generic body storing a value of a symbolic type outward records the effect as latent: the body does not publish it, and an instance derived from the body publishes it where the substituted type carries a loan, as the instance's own check would (`List.append$…` over `Span[Int, origin_of(xs)]`, a user `Cell.put`). The same store also publishes an effect whose source is the stored type (`SigOrigin::Carried`): a call through a struct receiver closes the type with the receiver's arguments, and the destination borrows the places it then names, so a template-served `push_copy` on `Bag[Pointer[List[Int], ImmOrigin(origin_of(xs))]]` keeps `xs` alive and guarded (assets/ownership_error/template_served_instance_keeps_loan.mojo). An effect whose source is a union of parameters (a stored value borrowed through a `ref[origin_of(a, b)]` result) is kept member by member: an instance drops each member whose parameter is plain data there. Each call replays the callee's effects against the actual argument expressions: the checker enforces the store-outward escape rule across the boundary (a frame-locally rooted source flowing into an outliving destination rejects with the same `escapes storage` family), merges the transferred origins into its own bookkeeping so a later return of the destination sees them, derives a transitive effect when the destination is the enclosing callable's own parameter or receiver (a wrapper that stashes into a field it then appends carries the effect outward), and hands MIR a per-call-site record. MIR installs the merged loans on the destination's root after the call — a borrowed (`mut`/`ref`) parameter source loans the actual's own storage while an owned parameter only forwards the loans its moved value carries, and keeps them shared, so the viewed storage stays readable while the destination holds the view (assets/ok/transferred_view_loan_stays_shared.mojo) — so ownership analysis rejects mutating or dropping the loan root while the stored alias lives (the conflict names both variables) and keeps a borrowed source alive while a carrier collection holds a reference to it, with no source-level liveness required. Effect visibility is declaration-order independent: the check reruns, seeded with the prior round's committed effects, whenever a call site observed a stale callee entry, so later same-struct methods and recursion cycles (including mutually recursive pairs whose store is reachable only through the cycle) carry their effects to every caller; a program whose effects were all committed before any call site consulted them converges in one round. The store-outward rule also covers stores inside nested `def`s through captured owners — `self`, parameters, AND enclosing locals (a closure-locally rooted loan stored into any of them rejects; the store records a concrete `Bound`-destination effect that invocation sites ground, intermediate frames propagate, and the owning frame re-abstracts to its own callers) — plus unpack-into-place targets and, via ordinary method selection, user in-place dunders (`sink += carrier` replays `__iadd__`'s effects). Every call shape replays: checked function types carry identity-transparent inferred effects baked at value position and replayed at indirect call sites; callable-struct calls replay `Struct.__call__`; overloaded methods replay only the selected signature-qualified effect entry while free-function overloads retain their shared bare-name entry; abstract trait dispatch replays the union over every conforming implementation and overload; and a body calling through its own callable parameter (runtime `def(...)` param or compile-time callable value param) records a higher-order call-through residue that each call site resolves against the concrete callable it supplies, composing transitively through forwarding chains. Destinations are interior-precise: the transferred generation carries its destination domain, so sibling fields keep independent generations and rebinding the exact interior place releases its loans. A closure stored in an explicitly `capturing[...]`-annotated local loans its reference captures' owners (`imm` immutably, `mut`/`ref` mutably) while the storage lives — direct nested calls keep the loan-free capture model. `def(...)`-typed fields and elements are rejected; the internal field-invocation channel remains only as retained machinery for supported callable channels. Frozen residues: effects erase into explicitly annotated `def(...)` storage (plain function values carry no loans of their own); a bare `callable_bound` with no value provenance carries nothing; a call-through destination frame-local to the higher-order body is untracked inside that body; transfer via a returned `self` is owned by the return-origin path; source loans stay root-abstracted (only destinations carry interior paths); the bare `objs[0](args)` spelling over a value base dispatches the subscripted element call — identifier, member, and multi-index bases — through the selected `__getitem__` contract and the element's indirect `__call__`, replaying `Struct.__call__` transfer effects exactly like the parenthesized spelling; only non-value bracket arguments (types, names) stay rejected. |
| Borrowing | Argument exclusivity over carried origins | Run | A call may not reach one place mutably through one argument and by any path through another, the receiver included. An argument reaches its own place and every origin its type carries, whether the parameter type spells the origin or receives it through a type argument: `l.insert(0, Span(xs))` on a `List[Span[Int, origin_of(xs)]]`, `pick(l, Span(xs))` over `def pick[T](items: List[T], default: T)`, and `p.unsafe_write(Span(xs))` through a `Pointer[Span[Int, origin_of(xs)], MutUntrackedOrigin]` are rejected when `xs` is mutable, with upstream's "aliasing values passed mutably to … and passed mutably to …". Two immutable paths never conflict, so the same calls are accepted over a read parameter's origin. A callee decorated `@__unsafe_nested_origins_read_only` reaches the origins its argument types carry, and a `ref` argument's own place, immutably; the bundled library declares the members upstream does (`List.append`, the `List`/`Dict`/`String` subscript reads, `List.unsafe_get`, `Variant.__getitem_param__`) and `List.__setitem__`, which upstream spells as a store through `__getitem__`'s reference. Each element a free function's `*args` or pack collects is an argument of its own under the collector's name, and a tuple literal is its `Tuple` initializer's call over `var *args`, so `show(Span(xs), Span(xs))` over `def show[*Ts: Copyable](*args: *Ts)` and `(Span(xs), Span(xs))` are rejected over a mutable `xs`, as upstream (`assets/type_error/variadic_elements_share_mutable_origin.mojo`, `assets/type_error/tuple_literal_elements_share_mutable_origin.mojo`). A method's collector is judged alike, under its declared name. A gathered element read by borrow is held by reference whatever its type, so it conflicts with a mutable path to its own place where a regular trivial read takes a copy: `r(x, x)` over `r[*Ts](ref b: Int, *rest: *Ts)`, `both(k, k)` over `both(mut a: Int, *b: Int)`, and `s.m(s.v)` over `m(mut self, *rest: Int)` are rejected as upstream (`assets/type_error/ref_argument_aliases_pack_element.mojo`, `assets/type_error/mut_argument_aliases_variadic_element.mojo`, `assets/type_error/mut_receiver_aliases_method_variadic_element.mojo`). A constructor call (`Tuple(Span(xs), Span(xs))`) is not judged yet (roadmap R101). |
| Unsafe pointers | Provenance, arithmetic, and lifetime | Run | Stable allocation identities, element offsets, allocation bounds, aligned allocation, dangling placeholders, real deallocation, and invalid/double-free diagnostics execute in the VM. `Pointer` is the canonical type name (`UnsafePointer` is accepted as upstream's deprecated alias; `MutPointer`/`ImmPointer` fix the origin argument's statically known mutability and reject a mismatch; a bare `Pointer[T, _]` parameter reads as `ImmPointer[T, _]` — any provenance binds, writes and mutable-demanding forwards reject, and the bare placeholder is not concrete outside parameter position, all as upstream's per-call origin inference decides); the one-argument `Pointer[T]` spelling resolves to `MutUntrackedOrigin`, the origin of heap allocations, and `Pointer(to=place)`/`UnsafePointer(to=place)` both spell the place-pointer constructor. Writes through a statically immutable origin — place, untracked, unsafe-any, or static — reject at the checker. So do writes through a symbolic-mutability origin that no binding resolves (the Borrowing row has the per-binding rule). A `ref[ImmStaticOrigin]`/`ref[ImmUntrackedOrigin]`/`ref[MutUntrackedOrigin]` parameter binds only an actual carrying that exact origin (an untracked pointer's dereference binds; a tracked local rejects with upstream's `cannot be converted from 'Int' to ref 'Int'` conversion text and origin note), while `ref[MutUnsafeAnyOrigin]` binds anything. The empty subscript `ptr[]` is the direct dereference — offset-0 load/store (including augmented stores) on heap pointers and pointee access on place pointers — with a dedicated parse marker so `p[None]` is never a dereference and a non-pointer receiver rejects contextually. Place-origin pointers execute as frame/slot handles; their checked origins reject offsets, arithmetic, comparison, `free()`, and escapes, and only the VM value erases provenance. The experimental conservative origin form `origin._subtree` (current Mojo's `Origin._subtree`) is accepted in Pointer origin arguments and `unsafe_origin_cast` targets — over origin parameters, `origin_of(...)` places, and interior projections, but strictly terminal (nothing projects below it) and rejected in `ref [...]` clauses; a subtree pointer designates its base or any descendant, so it is never multi-element (offsets reject) and dereferences go through the runtime handle rather than a re-derived place. Its loan is a lazy generation, stricter than a named interior generation: mutating the base, any subfield, or any interior of the base stales it (across branches, loop backedges, and structured-exception regions), while ordinary reads coexist and an unrelated owner's mutation leaves it live; a write through a mutable subtree pointer succeeds once and invalidates the pointer's own generation for every later use (current Mojo's first-write rule), with the invalidation site named in the rejection. `Pointer(to=r)` over a `ref` binding — a local `ref` or a `ref` parameter — mints exactly this subtree provenance over the reference's origin (the referent is that base or some descendant): reads and a first write execute through the runtime handle, owner mutation stales it, a bare `ref` parameter's parametric mutability stays non-writable, and a union-origin reference (`ref[self.a, self.b]` under one owner) mints the subtree of its members' deepest common base. Allocation is the current layout-based model, self-hosted in `std.memory`: `alloc(Layout[T](count=n))` → `Allocation[T]` (owning a `ThinAllocation[T]` plus its `Layout`, both linear via `Deinitable where False` — implicit drop is a checker error with a dealloc hint), `unsafe_ptr()`/`layout()`/`into_thin()`/`unsafe_leak()`, `dealloc(allocation^)`, and raw `unsafe_alloc[T](n, alignment=…)`; `alloc` is prelude-exported, `Layout`/`Allocation`/`ThinAllocation`/`dealloc`, `MaybeUninit`, and `OwnedPointer` import from the `std.memory` package (`alloc`, `maybe_uninit`, `owned_pointer` submodules mirroring upstream's layout), and the raw migration spelling `unsafe_alloc` imports only from `std.memory.alloc` — `from std.memory import unsafe_alloc` rejects, as upstream (deferred: `Layout.alignment()` accessor — no `align_of` builtin — plus `ManagedAllocation` and Writable formatting). `Allocation.unsafe_ptr()` is tracked: it carries the Allocation's `element` interior-generation origin, so the owner stays alive through the pointer's uses, and moving the Allocation into `dealloc`/`into_thin`/`unsafe_leak` stales the pointer — use-after-free rejects statically as an invalidated interior reference (a whole-variable move invalidates every interior generation rooted at the moved variable; `ThinAllocation.unsafe_ptr()` stays the raw untracked escape hatch). The legacy static `UnsafePointer[T].alloc[_aligned]` spelling rejects in user code with a migration hint and survives only as the compiler-private heap primitive inside `std/memory/alloc.mojo` (the sourceless stage-composed test seam retains it); the bundled collections and String allocate through `std.memory` like everyone else, and the former compiler-private `take(i)`/`destroy(i)` methods are gone — `unsafe_offset(i).unsafe_take_pointee()`/`unsafe_deinit_pointee()` are their public replacements over the same `PointerStorageTake`/`PointerStorageDestroy` MIR operations. `Pointer[T].unsafe_dangling()` is the dangling-placeholder spelling (`dangling()` rejects with a rename hint). A generic struct's `Pointer[Self.T, Self.o]` field infers `T` from the pointee and binds `o` from a tracked argument (`Cell(Pointer(to=w))`); an explicit `Cell[Int, origin_of(w)]` checks the pointer's provenance against the supplied origin. There is no implicit widening of a tracked pointer to a `MutUntrackedOrigin` slot (upstream rejects the conversion, and an untracked field would drop the source's keep-alive loan); `unsafe_origin_cast[MutUntrackedOrigin]()` is the explicit spelling. A loan-carrying value stored through a dereferenced pointer (`q[] = Span(xs)` into an `unsafe_alloc[Span[Int, origin_of(xs)]](n)`) keeps its borrowed owner alive while the pointer is in use, on both backends (`assets/ok/pointer_deref_store_loan_carrying_pointee.mojo`). A pointer subscript (`p[i]`, `p[]`, `p[unsafe_offset=i]`) reads its pointee in place, as upstream's `ref`-returning `__getitem__` does: a field read or a `.copy()` receiver copies nothing, and a consuming use copies once, on both backends (`assets/ok/pointer_subscript_reads_in_place.mojo`). The dereference of a pointer the expression forms in place — `Pointer(to=s.x)[]`, an origin-cast or offset temporary — is a place too: it assigns, passes as a `mut` argument, and binds a `ref` that aliases the pointee, its pointer held in a hidden slot for the access (`assets/ok/pointer_temporary_dereference_place.mojo`); a pointer an ordinary call returns (`idp(p)[]`) is still only read in place (R1212). A pointer dereferences its address, never its origin: one whose origin was cast (`Pointer(to=b).unsafe_origin_cast[origin_of(a)]()`) or one a method returns under a wider origin than the place it addresses (`-> Pointer[Int, origin_of(self)]` over `self.y`) reads, stores, and calls through its pointee, its origin only keeping an owner alive, on both backends (`assets/ok/pointer_origin_cast_reads_pointee.mojo`). |
| Unsafe pointers | `MaybeUninit[T]` inline-uninit storage | Run | Inline possibly-uninitialized storage for one `T: AnyType`, self-hosted in `std.memory` over the compiler-private `__UninitStorage[T]` field primitive (a new inline-uninit VM capability — `Value::UninitStorage` plus the `UninitStorage`/`UninitStorageTake`/`UninitStorageDestroy` MIR operations and the `UninitPayload` place projection — not a spelling over heap slots). | Destruction | ASAP `__deinit__`, region/try cleanup, aggregate teardown | Run | Liveness rewrites every owned MIR root with explicit drop glue, placed in the structured body as upstream's destructor insertion places it: after a value's last use, or at the entry of an `if` arm that leaves out a value a sibling arm keeps. `try` parts get the same elaboration, with a raise-observer liveness seed at every checked raise point (a `raise`, a call declared `raises`, a struct operator whose dunder raises): an outer variable rebound in a region runs the overwritten value's destructor on the normal path (skipped when the constructing call raises), and dead rebinds in `except`/`else`/`finally` regions drop in-region. A `try`'s cleanup list runs on its body's raise edge only and names the slots some raise point there still holds that the raise's handler (or `finally`) does not read, so a value a handler abandons with a `break` or `return` dies before the handler runs (`assets/ok/try_handler_exit_raise_edge_drop.mojo`); a value a `break` or `continue` out of a `try` leaves behind dies before the exit when the `finally` does not read it, and survives into the `finally` when it does, so a `break` whose `finally` reads the loop's reference into a named `List` runs (`assets/ok/try_finally_break_reads_loop_reference.mojo`); a return leaving through a `try` drops in-region what nothing after it reads and lists only what it holds past the `finally` blocks it crosses; no pass drops or lists a slot every path already ended, which the MIR verifier rejects. A return through a `try` destroys a value the code after the `try` still needs before it leaves, and one the `finally` reads after the `finally`, as the pin does (`assets/ok/try_return_destroys_held_values.mojo`). **Limits:** such a value is destroyed after the outermost `finally` a return crosses, not after the innermost (R829); a loop's iterator outlives its last `__next__` on a return (R828); a value the `finally` reads and nothing after a `break` reaches is never destroyed on the VM, and at the function's exit natively (R884); one raise-edge list serves every raise point of a body (R831). A value only a handler, `else`, or `finally` defines dies at its last use in that region, not round an enclosing loop; the caught error dies in its handler, an unread `except e: pass` binding on entry; and nothing the `try` ended is dropped again before or after it, matching the pin on both backends (`assets/ok/try_region_local_drop_order.mojo`). A call consumes its owned operands itself, after every operand is evaluated, so a raise in a later operand destroys each owned operand evaluated before it — a `^` transfer, a temporary, a copy construction, a keyword or `*xs` collector argument, a conditional's result, an owned receiver — in reverse declaration order before the handler runs, and an outer value whose last use follows the raise dies on the raise edge too (`assets/ok/owned_operand_destroyed_on_later_raise.mojo`). **Limits:** the same raise leaving the function leaks those operands (R20). An operand that must be explicitly destroyed is not destroyed there: the raise abandons it, which the checker rejects, as the pin does (`@explicit_destroy` row). A call's argument temporaries in a `try` body die when the call returns, before its result's consumer, inside a loop as outside one, whether the call raises or not (`assets/ok/try_in_loop_temporary_drop_order.mojo`). A read temporary loaded before a later conditional-expression argument's branch dies when the call returns, at top level, in a `try`, round a loop, and as a method receiver (`assets/ok/read_temporary_conditional_argument_drop.mojo`). Reference-bearing aggregate generations retire their exact old owner on straight-line or CFG-edge rebinding, and transient reference-register provenance retains the selected owner through the consuming expression without extending it to scope exit. Nominal collection fields follow ordinary struct teardown; elements in the compiler-private heterogeneous pack carrier drop left-to-right, including moved variadic-pack and public-Tuple backing storage, exactly once. Timing and order match the pinned Mojo: a discarded call result is destroyed before the next statement, and so is each `_ = e` value, on both backends, whatever the function's other discards hold (`assets/ok/discard_slots_typed_per_statement.mojo`); a discarded transfer — `_ = x^`, or `x^` as a statement — destroys `x` where it lies with no move initializer, over a local, a `var` parameter, or a `mut` parameter, while `var c = x^` still moves (`assets/ok/discarded_transfer_destroyed_in_place.mojo`); **Limits:** `_ = w.m^` over a field is rejected where the pin destroys the field in place (R750); a non-raising call writes its result in place, so a discarded result — of a free, method, infix, reflected, function-value, or generic call — and an unused `var x = call(...)` die at the call, before its argument temporaries and the owners whose last use it is, while a raising call's result, `_ = e`, and an assignment die after them (`assets/ok/discarded_call_result_drop_order.mojo`); a construction is its `__init__`'s call, so `P(S(1), S(2))` destroys each read temporary when the initializer returns, `P(s, s)` reads `s` in place without a copy, and a raising `__init__` makes the construction a raising call, rejected outside `try` or a `raises` function (`assets/ok/constructor_read_temporary_drop_order.mojo`, `assets/type_error/raising_constructor_unhandled.mojo`); an owner whose last use is a field read feeding a call is destroyed after that call returns, a consuming parameter (`var`/`deinit`, receivers included) is destroyed at its last use inside the callee — an unused one at entry — and a struct's fields destroy in declaration order while simultaneously dying locals and parameters destroy in reverse declaration order. A drop of a slot that holds a whole value on every path to it is the call of its named destructor (R1043), as upstream's destructor insertion leaves it: a forwarding use of the slot and a `__deinit__` method call on it, the shape an explicit `deinit self` call lowers to, on both backends and in the elaborator's instances; the state is a definite-initialization fold that follows every region op as upstream's lifetime checker walks it and tracks moved-out fields, so a field moved out and stored again (`assets/ok/partial_move_reinit_sibling_use.mojo`), a field destroyed and replaced (`assets/ok/field_store_overwrite_drop.mojo`), and a slot a `try` body defines or reads (`assets/ok/late_initialized_local.mojo`, `assets/ok/template_method_with_statement.mojo`) end in the call (R1087), and a drop of a slot empty on every path to it is erased; a `for` loop binds what `__next__` yields only past its exhaustion test, in the body, and an owned `for x in a^` consumes `a` into an iterator of its own type in a slot of its own, whose drop names the iterator's `__deinit__`, as upstream makes the iterator a value of its own, so neither leaves a runtime-tested drop (`assets/ok/optional_owning_api.mojo`, `assets/ok/array_fixed_size_core.mojo`, R1088); a drop whose slot some path leaves empty or partial stays a `drop.var` the backends test at run time. A struct with droppable fields and no `__deinit__` has a synthesized `nodebug` destructor (R1041), as upstream synthesizes a `__del__` for every struct that declares none: `S.__deinit__(deinit self)` with an empty body, which drop elaboration refines into one named `drop.place` per field at entry, which every drop of the struct names, and whose call at a whole slot's drop the pre-elaboration inliner inlines; a struct whose every field's destruction runs no code gets none (`assets/ok/deinit_param_destruction_timing.mojo`, `Tx`). Inside a `__deinit__`/`deinit self` body each direct field of the receiver is destroyed at its own last use — an unused one at entry, one read in an `if` condition right after it, one read in a single arm inside that arm and at the other arm's entry, one a `try` handler reads right after the body's last raising call (`assets/ok/deinit_field_dies_before_non_raising_call_in_try.mojo`), a nested read (`self.p.b`) keeping the whole direct field alive, a transferred field (`self.b^`) being its new owner's — on both backends through the `DropPlace` instruction the field-granular refinement of drop elaboration emits on the structured body (`assets/ok/deinit_body_field_last_use.mojo`); a field read only inside a loop body dies after the loop, where the pinned Mojo destroys it at entry and reads the destroyed value (recorded `output-diff` `deinit-body-field-loop-read`). A store into an initialized droppable field or constant-index element destroys the replaced value at the store, through a local, a `mut self` receiver, a `mut` parameter, or a nested field chain; a constructor's first store into an `out self` field only initializes it and a second destroys the first, and a field moved out on some paths is destroyed only where it still holds a value — on both backends through a `DropPlace` spliced before the `Store` (`assets/ok/field_store_overwrite_drop_shapes.mojo`). A whole place written through a reference destroys its replaced value the same way, since it has no redefining `DefVar` either: reassigning a `mut` parameter or `self` in a `mut self` method destroys the caller's old value at the assignment, and so does a store through a place pointer (`q[] = v`) or a whole-variable `ref` binding (`assets/ok/mut_parameter_reassignment_drop_shapes.mojo`, `assets/ok/pointer_deref_store_overwrite_drop.mojo`). Values whose last use is the same call die in the pin's order: a scalar read (`h.t`) passed to a concrete register-passable `read`/`var` parameter, a built-in operator, or a slice bound is loaded at call setup, so its owner dies before the call, in parameter order (`a.t + b.t` destroys `a` first, `two(h.t, e.t)` destroys `h` and `e` before `two` runs), and a `deinit self` field read so dies the same way; one passed by address (`print`, a generic `T`, a `*xs` collector, a by-reference argument) keeps its owner through the call, and owners dying there drop in reverse declaration order (`assets/ok/by_value_argument_drop_order.mojo`). A struct operator is its dunder's call, so its operands' owners die when the dunder returns, before the result's consumer: `print(o + 1)` destroys `o` before `print` runs, as `o.__add__(1)` does, for a lone or overloaded dunder, a reflected one, a generic struct's, and `!=` served by `__eq__` (`assets/ok/struct_operator_operand_owner_drop.mojo`). A comparison through an `Equatable` or `Comparable` bound in a generic body is likewise the bound requirement's call, so `print(x < a)` over a copied `x` destroys it before `print` runs at a struct instance (`assets/ok/trait_bound_comparison_drop_order.mojo`). Each link of a chained comparison is its own dunder's call likewise: `print(a < b < c)` destroys `a` after the first link and the middle's temporary and `c` after the second (`assets/ok/chained_comparison_link_drop_order.mojo`). The read itself happens at call setup, after every argument is evaluated, for a plain, keyword, method, receiver, operator, comparison-chain, subscript, setter, or indirect call: `two(c.t, bump(c))` sees `bump`'s write, a method's `var` copies run in parameter order, and `two(a.t, take(a^))` is rejected as a use of the moved `a` (`assets/ok/by_value_argument_read_at_call_setup.mojo`, `assets/ownership_error/by_value_argument_read_after_move.mojo`). A `^` operand transfers at the call, after the later operands, so `two(x^, look(x))`, `two(x^, x.v)`, and `owe(k.v, k^)` read the value before it moves, as upstream (`assets/ok/owned_operand_destroyed_on_later_raise.mojo`). A by-value operand with a reference result — a tuple element, a user `ref` accessor, a `List`/`Dict` element, a `ref`-returning free or method call — is evaluated to its reference where written and read at call setup too: `two(tup[0], bt(tup))` and `print(t[0] + bt(t))` see `bt`'s write, a `String` element reaches a read or `var` parameter with the later argument's append, `two(lst[0], bl(lst))` is rejected as a use of an invalidated interior reference, and `two(tup[0], take(tup^))` as a use of the moved `tup` (`assets/ok/reference_argument_read_at_call_setup.mojo`, `assets/ownership_error/reference_argument_read_after_invalidation.mojo`, `assets/ownership_error/reference_argument_read_after_move.mojo`). A tuple, list, set, or dict display is its constructor's call, so it reads its elements at its own construction's setup, a dict display evaluating every key before any value (`assets/ok/display_element_read_at_construction.mojo`, `assets/ownership_error/display_element_read_after_invalidation.mojo`). A non-empty dict display is `Dict`'s `__dict_literal__` constructor over a key list and a value list, as upstream lowers it, so it copies each entry as that constructor does and its `where` clause requires `Copyable & Deinitable` keys and values at the display (`assets/ok/dict_display_literal_constructor.mojo`, `assets/type_error/dict_display_requires_copyable_value.mojo`); an empty `{}` stays `Dict()`. **Limits:** inside a generic `def`, a display over a parameter that lacks `Copyable` or `Deinitable` is reported as a violated constraint where the pin reports lacking evidence (`docs/roadmap.md` R698), and the dict destroys its entries in insertion order where the pin's table destroys them in slot order (R699). A list, set, dict, or array display passed as a call argument (or nested as an element of one) is built as that argument's conversion at the call's setup, so `ha([a.t, 1], bump(a))` sees `bump`'s write and `ha([lst[0], 1], set0(lst))` is rejected as a use of an invalidated interior reference, while a tuple display, a comprehension, and a display used as a receiver or nested in another call are built where written (`assets/ok/display_argument_built_at_call_setup.mojo`, `assets/ownership_error/display_argument_element_read_after_invalidation.mojo`); a display as a struct operator's right operand (`g(a) + [a.t]`) is still rejected by the checker. An accessor chain over a temporary (`ident(Span(xs))[0]`) is still read where written. A method or subscript call through a trait bound in a generic body passes its arguments by the conventions the requirement declares, as a concrete call does by its callee's: `s.sh(b.t)` over `def sh(self, x: Int)` destroys `b` before `sh` runs, through a refined bound, a trait default's `self.sh(...)`, and `s[c.t]` alike, while a `mut` requirement parameter keeps its owner through the call (`assets/ok/trait_bound_argument_drop_order.mojo`). A storage destroy (`unsafe_deinit_pointee`, `MaybeUninit.unsafe_deinit`), a variant store's replaced payload (`Variant.set`, one named `__deinit__` per alternative under its tag), and an owned closure capture released at the closure's death name the destructor they run, as a drop names its own (R1085); a consumed residue's fields and a pack aggregate's elements still find theirs from the type (R1090). |
| Standard library | Self-hosted collections, algorithms, math, `std.hash`, `std.bit` | Run | Proof subset under `stdlib/`, not Mojo's full standard library. The prelude builtins `next(iterator)` (exhaustion raises) and `reversed(...)` — the three range shapes and upstream's `List[T]` overload over `List.__reversed__()`, the back-to-front borrowed iterator (`for x in xs.__reversed__()` spells the same loop) — live in `std.iter` and `std/builtin/reversed.mojo`. |
| Standard library | Filesystem, files, streams, paths, temporary directories, environment, and libc FFI (`std.os`, `std.os.path`, `std.io`, `std.sys`, `std.pathlib`, `std.tempfile`, `std.ffi`, `std.sys._libc_errno`, `std.stat`) | Run | Upstream's signatures over the `external_call` builtin: the prelude names `open`, `FileHandle`, and `RawFileDescriptor` — `std.io.file` `FileHandle` over a raw descriptor (`open(path, mode)` for `"r"`/`"w"`/`"rw"`/`"a"` with upstream's mode and `errno` texts, a written file's parent directories created; `read`/`read_bytes` to EOF or a byte count, `seek` with `SEEK_*`, `write_string`/`write_bytes`/`write_once`/`write_all` and the `Writer` family's variadic `write`, an idempotent `close`, and destruction closing the handle), `std.io.file_descriptor` `RawFileDescriptor` (a `Writer` over a descriptor, from an `Int` or an open `FileHandle`; `read_bytes` into a mutable span), `std.sys` `stdin`/`stdout`/`stderr` as compile-time `RawFileDescriptor` values, `print(sep=, end=, flush=, file=)` (`sep`/`end` a literal, `String`, or `StringSpan`; `file` a `RawFileDescriptor`, writing through libc `write`; `flush` a no-op), `std.pathlib` `Path` (`/` and `joinpath`, `suffix`/`name`/`parts`, `==`/`<`/`hash`/`Bool`, `exists`/`is_dir`/`is_file`/`listdir`, `read_text`/`read_bytes`/`write_text`/`write_bytes`, `expanduser`/`home`, `repr` as `Path('...')`), `cwd`, `DIR_SEPARATOR`; `std.tempfile` `gettempdir`, `mkdtemp(suffix=, prefix=, dir=)`, and `TemporaryDirectory` (cleanup on a normal and an erroring exit); `std.os` `listdir`, `mkdir`, `makedirs`, `remove`/`unlink`, `rmdir`, `removedirs`, `getenv`/`setenv`/`unsetenv`, `sep`, `SEEK_*`, `PathLike` (conformed to by `String` and `StringSpan`); `std.os.path` `exists`, `lexists`, `isdir`, `isfile`, `islink`, `getsize`, `is_absolute`, `join`, `split`, `basename`, `dirname`, `split_extension`, `splitroot`, `expandvars`, `expanduser` (`~` through `HOME` only); `std.ffi` `external_call[callee, return_type, num_fixed_args=](*args)` over a closed libc allowlist (`open`, `read`, `write`, `close`, `lseek`, `unlink`, `mkdir`, `rmdir`, `opendir`/`readdir`/`closedir`, `getcwd`, `getenv`/`setenv`/`unsetenv`, `strerror`, `__errno_location`, `memcpy`, `strlen`, `__xstat`/`__lxstat`; any other callee is a checker error), the `c_*` scalar aliases, `CStringSlice` (byte elements; minted by `String.as_c_string_slice()`), `String(unsafe_from_utf8_ptr=)`, `get_errno()`/`ErrNo` (`String(err)` is glibc's `strerror` text). The VM runs each callee on Rust's standard library with libc's return/`errno`/buffer contract (fd 1 appends to the captured stdout; `setenv` writes a per-VM overlay), the native backend calls libc directly; `readdir` results are read as bytes (`d_name` at offset 19) and `stat` records fill a scalar-only `_c_stat`, Linux x86-64 glibc layouts. Public `stat`/`lstat`/`stat_result`, `realpath`, `symlink`/`link`/`chdir`, `isatty` (`RawFileDescriptor.isatty`/`fchdir`), `OptionalPointer`, `ErrNo`'s named constants, `NamedTemporaryFile`, `FileHandle.read` into a typed `Span[Scalar[dtype], origin]`, `Path.stat`/`lstat` and `_dir_of_current_file`, `KeyElement`, and a string literal bound to a `PathLike` parameter (`isdir("/tmp")`; spell `String(...)`) are not ported; temporary names come from `/dev/urandom` until `std.random` lands. |
| Backend | Register VM | Run | Sole backend and runtime; direct calls use an explicit continuation-driven frame stack with monotonic frame identities. Concrete MIR's structured control flow runs as it stands, unlowered, as upstream's interpreter runs `hlcf` (R882): each frame's region cursor (`backend/vm/regions.rs`) steps `if`, `loop`, and `match` and their region terminators, so a call inside any region is a pushed frame and recursion through one runs at any depth (100 000 deep in a debug build), and a `break`/`continue` from a `try` nested in another `try`'s part to a loop inside that part runs, each `finally` on the way (R905's VM half). Reaching `hlcf.unreachable` is a runtime error. A `try` still runs its parts on the host stack, so recursion through a call inside a `try` part overflows at a depth of about 40 in a debug build (R1272). |
| Tooling | MIR 1.0 schema and disassembler | Check | `mir::text::disassemble` rejects unverified MIR and emits deterministic UTF-8/LF artifacts with canonical ordering, exact literals, full checked metadata, and stable snapshots. `CompiledProgram::emit_mir` and `mojito emit-mir [FILE]` expose the compiler-produced, post-drop executable form — the lazily cached, re-verified `CompiledProgram::drop_elaborated_mir` artifact that backend execution also consumes. |
| Tooling | Textual MIR assembler parser and structural diagnostics | Check | `mir::text::parse_artifact` consumes UTF-8 bytes independently of Mojo syntax, decodes the complete 1.0 schema (every instruction, terminator, type, origin, and declaration-metadata form the printer emits, including nested try regions and the structs/decls sections), retains assembly spans, and reports recoverable syntax/shape errors. Semantic verification composes on top via `verify_artifact`/`load_artifact`; print → parse → print byte equality is enforced per executable corpus fixture by the `roundtrip::*` corpus group. Execution remains separate. |
| Tooling | Artifact verifier integration | Check | `mir::text::verify_artifact` runs the canonical MIR semantic verifier on assembled programs and reports each finding at its artifact source span — block, then function, then artifact-root precision — naming the resolved artifact path in the diagnostic context. `load_artifact` is the composed parse-then-verify loading gate artifact execution will sit behind. |
| Tooling | Textual MIR artifact execution | Run | `mojito exec [FILE]` (file or stdin) loads a textual artifact through `mir::text::load_artifact` — parse plus the canonical MIR semantic verifier, the artifact's semantic gate — and executes it on the register VM via `artifact::run_artifact`/`Backend::run_elaborated`, printing the program's output (no bindings echo; artifacts are compiled programs). `emit-mir | exec -` is the supported source-to-artifact-to-VM composition, and every shared runnable conformance case pins direct/artifact output and binding equivalence. The serialized MIR runs exactly as written: no re-elaboration, re-verification, or post-drop ownership re-analysis. |
| Tooling | MIR pass tests (`mojito opt`) | Check | `analysis::pass_named` is the pass registry (every pre-elaboration `PIPELINE` pass plus `elaborate-drops`, whose output is structured, `lower-arg-conventions`, and `simplify-cf`), and `analysis::opt` loads a textual MIR artifact, runs named passes in order (a program pass over the program, a body pass over every body but `__toplevel__`), and verifies the program after each pass in every build; `mojito opt [--pass NAME]… [FILE]` prints the result, and with no pass only canonicalizes. `tests/mir_pass_test.rs` pins each case under `tests/mir_passes/<pass>/` by its printed `functions:` section (`mir::text::disassemble_functions`), so a format bump breaks no expected file. `elaborate-drops` is idempotent (`already_elaborated`): an input `DropVar` is the slot's teardown. The verifier rejects a `drop.var` or `var.consume` of a slot every path reaching it already dropped, consumed, or moved — walking the structured body as upstream's `CheckLifetimes` does, an `if` or `match` joining its arms, a loop iterated to a fixpoint with its `break`s as its live-out, and each `break`/`continue` resolved to its loop through every `finally` it crosses (R1273) — so `mojito opt` refuses a doubled drop at load (`double_drop`, an `.error` case, whose report `mojito opt` prints) and a pass that emits one after the pass. The two cleanup lists end what they name where they run, so a `try`'s raise-edge list or a return's naming a slot every path reaching it already ended is the same finding (`return_through_finally` pins a return's list). MIR text 1.54 reads and writes upstream's structured `hlcf` region ops — `if`, `loop`, `comptime.if`, `comptime.for` — and their region terminators, and the verifier checks each terminator against the op it ends; MIR lowering builds `and`/`or`, conditional expressions, comparison chains, and comprehensions as `if`/`loop` ops, HIR builds run-time `if`/`elif`/`else` as an `if` op with elif condition regions, `while`/`for` as a `loop` labelled uniquely in its function whose body opens with an `if` on the condition or has-next flag, its else arm holding the loop's `else` and a `break`, `comptime for` as a `comptime.for` op, and `try` as a `try` op whose parts are regions of the function's own walk, each completed by `try.yield` (MIR text 1.59, R874), with every `break`/`continue` naming its loop (one in a `try` part included) and the statements after a `return`, `break`, `continue`, or `raise` erased before they lower, so they reach no slot, block, or nested function (R889, `assets/ok/dead_code_after_terminators.mojo`), as `LowerSemanticCF` erases dead code, so every region is one block, which `mir::verify` requires of every body in every mode, as upstream's `hlcf` regions are `SizedRegion<1>` (R1274: MIR text 1.88 withdraws the CFG terminators `jump`, `branch`, `comptime_branch`, `comptime_for.iterator`, and `escape`, an older artifact holding one fails at load as an unknown terminator, and every analysis, pass, and the VM read one block per region, with no block-list fixpoint, dominator tree, or escape channel left above Pliron's own lowering); the elaborator instantiates the structured body, unrolling each `comptime for` and deciding each `comptime if` on its region op, and each instance keeps its region ops, which the VM runs and Pliron alone lowers to LLVM blocks, as upstream's `LowerControlFlow` lowers `hlcf` (no MIR pass lowers control flow since R1273). A `loop` has results, which the `break` that leaves it carries (an inlined call's result is its wrapper loop's). A `loop` also has arguments (MIR text 1.83), which its operands define on entry and each `continue` that resumes it for the next iteration, as `hlcf.loop`'s body block arguments are; the verifier checks each edge's values against them, `canonicalize` removes an argument nothing reads (`RemoveUnusedLoopArgs`), and `sccp` meets each argument with its operand and its continues' values; `mem-2-reg` produces them for a slot stored in the body (R927; `tests/mir_passes/mem-2-reg/region_*.mir`). An elif's condition region and arm, an `if`'s and a `match`'s else region, and each `match` case after the first take arguments too (MIR text 1.84), which the `if.elifcond.yield` or `match.next`s entering them define, as `hlcf.if.elifcond.yield` passes its carried values to both targets and `hlcf.match.next` its operands to the next region; the verifier checks each edge, `canonicalize` decides or promotes an elif only when its condition carries no values, `sccp` follows a constant elif condition to one target and enters a case only from an executable `match.next`, meeting each argument with the values carried in; `mem-2-reg` produces them for a slot stored in an elif condition, an arm, or a case (R927). A `match` has results (MIR text 1.85), which each `match.complete` and its else region's `yield` carry, and a `comptime for` operands, arguments, and results, which its `comptime.for.continue`s and `comptime.for.break`s carry, as `hlcf.match` and `hlcf.comptime.for` have; the verifier checks each edge and a `comptime for`'s operands against its results, and `sccp` leaves them overdefined as upstream's generic path does (`tests/mir_passes/sccp/match_result_kept.mir`, `comptime_for_values_kept.mir`); `mem-2-reg` produces them for a slot stored inside the op (R927), and the elaborator carries a `comptime for`'s values as upstream's `processComptimeForOp` does, each iteration loop's results the next clone's arguments and the last the outer loop's break. **Limits:** a `break`/`continue` from a `try` nested in another `try`'s part to a loop inside that outer part is unsupported (R905). |
| Tooling | The bundled library is a compiled package | Run | The bundled `std` library is checked, lowered to MIR, and ownership-verified once per compiler build, and kept as checked in one package, as upstream ships a precompiled `std.mojoc`. The package names each library file relative to the library's root, as upstream's names `Mojo/stdlib/std/…`, and the build refuses one that names the root it was built under, so it reads wherever it is installed and is keyed by the build id alone, which covers the library's source. An installed compiler reads `<prefix>/lib/mojito/std.mojoc`, which `mojito precompile <stdlib>/std -o <path>/std.mojoc` writes (only the bundled `std` precompiles; the output must be a `std.mojoc` or `std.mojopkg`) and `scripts/package-pliron` ships, and refuses to compile without it or with another build's, as upstream's does; a development build writes its package to `target/mojito-cache` the first time it needs it (`MOJITO_CACHE_DIR` moves it). A compilation reads no library source: its link resolves a library import against the package's module table, probing, canonicalizing, and reading no library file, and reads each library module it reaches from the package's record of that module (its loads and export tables, as bytecode; no declaration), found in the package's one module table, which every declaration names its module in. It prepares and elaborates only the program's own statements, reading the library's structs and functions as the build's check resolved them, and its module constants, as the build's elaboration folded them, as context, each constant decoded from its declaration's record the first time a lookup names it. Its check resolves a library declaration from the package's record of that declaration (the build's check resolved it; bytecode) the first time a lookup names it, for every checker pass and the elaborator's conformance oracle alike, which share each decoded record so it decodes once per compilation, and checks only the program's own statements; an overload query or a module-scope compile-time fact its own tables miss is answered from the record of the library declaration owning the name, and nothing is copied in when a compilation starts, and it lowers none of the library's bodies ahead of use: a call reads the callee's effect summaries from its record, and the elaborator lowers a library body from the package's checked record of its declaration (a lowering unit: a module `def`, one struct method, an inherited default's wrapper, a trait default, or a module constant's lifted applications; its checked nodes as the build's check left them, the subtree of the whole library's checked nodes under the unit's statement node, copied out rather than rebuilt from the unit's syntax, as upstream's unit is an op of its module, installed as they decode with no arena or syntax rebuilt, each checked expression holding its own operation with its operands as child nodes, its type, and its attributes, as an upstream op does, and each statement a checked node holding its operation, structured bodies as statement nodes, its binding, and its declaration (made by a walk over the checked statement nodes, not the syntax, and a `def`'s op holding its explicit captures, as upstream's closure op holds its captured values), a trait's naming each default's member `def` node, which holds that body's nodes once, as upstream's trait names its member function; no statement or expression is held as syntax (a declaration's op holds its symbol and its checked signature, its compile-time parameters, parameter and result types, and effect, and a struct's op its fields' checked types, as upstream's function op holds its resolved function type, which MIR reads in place of the declaration-site rows), and an op's type operand — a bracket argument written as a type, or written as a value and bound to a type or an origin (`List[String]`, `Pointer[Int, origin_of(x)]`), or a type in value position — as the attribute the check resolved it to, a type, a value (`Self.n`, a pack spread, a display element), or an origin, as upstream's call op carries each bracket argument as a parameter attribute, which MIR reads in place of the spelling), through HIR's region tree and MIR, then inserts its drops and verifies it, the first time it reaches it (Hello World lowers 3 of about 875 bodies, about 0.8 ms more than decoding MIR bodies took), as upstream's package holds each body's IR as its parser wrote it and its checks validated it (its structured `hlcf` loops and `lit` `return`, no `br`), and its importer lowers that control flow and runs its lifetime pass on each body it materializes (`docs/notes/upstream-package-contents.md`);
