| Tooling | The VM runs concrete MIR and nothing else | Run | `Compiler::execute` and `mojito exec` elaborate drop-elaborated MIR with `native::mono` from `main` and module initialization and run the result, the graph the native backend compiles; there is no other execution path, and a parametric instruction, terminator, or constant reaching the VM is a compiler invariant error (`verify_concrete` rejects each before a program runs). The `artifact::*` corpus group (`tests/corpus_test.rs`, no LLVM) compares the production run with the serialized artifact loaded and elaborated for every `assets/ok`, `assets/extensions/ok`, and `assets/runtime_error` program that reads no `input()`: output or error text. A printed instance displays through the `write_to`/`write_repr_to` the elaborator instantiated for it, and the elaborator keeps the `write_to` of each argument a nominal writer's `write` formats. A `**kwargs` callee collects there too: the VM builds the collector under its instance name (`StringDict$mono$TInt`), and the elaborator keeps that instance's empty constructor and `__setitem__` for every callee that declares a keyword pack. A `!=` on a struct that declares only `__eq__` is the negated `__eq__`, and a sized-scalar comparison converts to the `Bool` the template declared, as the elaborator types it. A runtime struct value carries no compile-time arguments: its instance name does, as `native::mono` minted it. |
| Tooling | Concrete MIR verification | Check | `mir::verify::verify_concrete` is the verifier mode for elaborated MIR: every structural rule of `mir::verify::verify`, plus the rejection of a symbolic type (a type or value parameter, an associated or dependent projection, `Self`, an unsolved inference variable, a generic callable) in a signature, slot, register, place, instruction, or declaration, of a compile-time parameter a declaration, a method or indirect call, or a subscript contract still declares, and of a `ConstructTypeParam`. A copy-reference result adapter stays only on a `__next__` step that returns a reference. Parametric MIR never passes it. `native::mono` verifies its output in this mode and asks the same rules per instance while materializing, so it holds no concreteness scan of its own. It rejects any compile-time argument slot too, on a call, a method or indirect call, or a subscript contract: a type bracket argument (`Array[UInt8, 16]`, `external_call["strlen", UInt]`) lowers to no register, as Mojo's call carries a type parameter as an attribute, and `native::mono` drops every slot of a concrete call, a builtin's included, so a concrete call has no parameter list. |
| Tooling | MIR type and parameter visitor | Run | `mir::visit` is the one walk over every site of a MIR body that holds a type or names a compile-time parameter (R915), as MLIR's `AttrTypeReplacer`: the function's type tables, each instruction's types, places, variant indices, lane masks, bracket arguments, contracts, constants, and parameter references, the regions after their instruction, and the `comptime` terminators. The elaborator substitutes an instance's bindings through it, with every program's output and elaboration counters unchanged; a multi-dimensional subscript's keyword places are now substituted too. `inline-parametric` binds a generic callee's compile-time parameters through it too, and the verifier's scope and concrete modes still read types through their own narrower walk (R944). |
| Tooling | Elaboration counters under `--timings` | Run | `mojito run --timings` reports, under `elaborate`, the elaborator's pass statistics: `concrete_instructions` (the whole concrete program), `generators` (templates instantiated under a name of their own, as `twice` → `twice$mono$TInt`), `generator_instances`, `generator_instructions` (each reached generator's template body, once), and `generator_instance_instructions`, and the same three sizes in blocks, `concrete_blocks`, `generator_blocks`, and `generator_instance_blocks` (R836); `MOJITO_TIMING_NOTES=1` adds one `generator` note per template with its instance and instruction counts. `native::mono::ElaborationCounts` (`SpecializedProgram::counts`) carries them; `docs/performance.md` §3 defines each, and `tests/compiler_test.rs::elaboration_counts_name_generators_and_their_instances` pins them. Three compile benchmarks are mostly instances (`benchmarks/compile/collections_many.mojo`, `nested_generics.mojo`, `trait_defaults.mojo`); §3 records their counters. Every `benchmarks/compile` program must run at the pin with Mojito's output, which the overnight gate's `benchmarks-mojo-sweep` step (`scripts/sweep-assets-mojo --benchmarks`) checks with no burn-down list; `tuple.mojo` is spelled as the pin accepts it, through `__getitem_param__`, a `Deinitable` bound, and a declared `Sized` for its `len`; the sweep passes on all eleven benchmarks. `scripts/instantiation-report` records the counters for every `assets/ok` and `benchmarks/compile` fixture in the committed `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`, one row per fixture plus a `total` row, so a change's effect on expansion is that file's diff. The overnight gate runs `scripts/instantiation-report --check`, which fails when the concrete-instruction total over the fixtures elaborated in both runs differs from the baseline's or a fixture stops elaborating. |
| Tooling | Pre-elaboration pipeline seam | Run | An ordered list of passes (`analysis::PIPELINE`) runs over drop-elaborated parametric MIR before the elaborator, as upstream's pre-elaboration pipeline does: over the program (the `pre_elaboration` `--timings` span), a program pass once and a body pass over each of its own bodies, and the body passes over each imported package body as it is finished, once per body, never per instance, with `mir::verify` after each body pass in debug builds. It runs the pin's four rounds in order (R869): `remove-unused-params`, `eliminate-dead-symbols`, `sroa`, `mem-2-reg`, `canonicalize`; `inline-parametric`, `remove-unused-params`, `eliminate-dead-symbols`, `sroa`, `mem-2-reg`, `sccp`, `canonicalize`; `remove-unused-params`, `eliminate-dead-symbols`, `inline-parametric`, `sroa`, `mem-2-reg`, `canonicalize`; then `remove-unused-params`, `eliminate-dead-symbols`, `cse`. `cse` (R926) is the fourth round's generator `CSEPass`: the post-elaboration pass, walking the structured body as MLIR's `CSE` walks regions, so a region sees what is available at its op and its own arguments and sibling regions share nothing (`tests/mir_passes/cse/structured_regions.mir`); a `SIMD[dtype, width]` result merges whatever its parameters (R930), and a register of a parameter type `T` is never merged, since an instance may own memory. No program's output changes; generator instructions fall 0.4 % over a 42-fixture sample, whose concrete instruction count does not move, since the post-elaboration `cse` already merged the same instructions in each instance. `sroa` (upstream's `SROA`, R928) splits a local slot of a declared struct whose drop runs no code and whose fields, at the slot type's arguments, are non-pointer and trivially register-passable or such structs, into one slot per field typed at those arguments, when every mention is a whole store (a `field.get` and a store per field), a whole read running no constructor (a read per field and a `struct.create`, MIR text 1.87), a lifetime marker, or a place projecting a field first (which then addresses the field's slot), repeating so a nested struct splits a level a round; `mem-2-reg` then promotes each field, so `var p = Pt(a, 2); p.x += 1; return p.x + p.y` and an inlined `@always_inline` constructor's `out self` keep no slot, and `canonicalize` folds a field read of a `struct.create` to its operand and a `struct.create` of every field of one value back to that value (`assets/ok/struct_slot_fields_split.mojo`; corpus concrete instructions −0.57 %, generator instances −0.35 %, generator-instance instructions −0.36 %, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`). A generic body splits a slot of its own application, as upstream's element allocation has the `!kgen.struct` element type (R1266): `Arr[n]`'s field slot is a `SIMD[DType.int64, n]` over the body's `n`, and `P[Int]` over a field `T` splits at `Int`, its synthesized destructor running no code there, so a dead `Arr[n](...)` construction passed to a nested function's removed argument leaves no read of `n` and `plain_nested[1/2/4]` is one instance, as on the pin (`assets/ok/generic_struct_slot_instances.mojo`; corpus generator instructions −0.6 %, concrete instructions −0.23 %); a field its arguments leave open (`P[T]`'s `T`) keeps the slot. The instances stay structured through it, every pass reading regions, and the VM runs them as they stand (R882), and Pliron lowers them to LLVM blocks itself, in `compile` (R883). `simplify-cf` is upstream's `SimplifyCF` whole (R881): a loop whose body ends in its one `break` (every `inlined_cf_scope` loop an inlined body leaves) or that nothing jumps to is replaced by its body, a `try` with a handler whose body cannot raise by its body and `else` (one with a `finally` is kept, R884), and after an `if` whose `then` arm (or, with no elifs, `else` arm) cannot fall through, its condition reads as the constant it implies in the rest of the block; with `cse` seeing inlined values in one scope again, corpus concrete instructions fall 4.7 % and concrete blocks 9.3 % against the previous pipeline, both counted over regions (`benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`), and no program's output changes. `cse` (R845) is MLIR's `CSE` over each region's dominator tree, a region's one block: an instruction free of effects (a constant, literal materialization, size or type-name query, parameter list address, field or variant-tag read of a value register, SIMD construction, cast, or shuffle, or built-in operator with no operator method over built-in scalar or SIMD operands, as MLIR's arithmetic and comparisons are, a trapping duplicate division being dominated by the identical division that traps first: R1270; `(a * b) + (a * b)` multiplies once, `tests/mir_passes/cse/built_in_operators.mir`) that a dominating one matches is erased, when its result is of a register-passable trivial type (a scalar, pointer, SIMD vector of any width, `StringLiteral`, or `TrivialRegisterPassable` struct, as upstream holds only such a value in an SSA value: R930; two identical `SIMD[DType.float32, 4]` constructions merge, `tests/mir_passes/cse/storage_values.mir`) (corpus concrete instructions −3.3 %, generator-instance instructions −2.4 %, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`), and a read of memory (a `place.load`, `ref.make`, `ref.read`, `var.use` borrow or register-passable trivial copy, or field read through a reference) is erased when an identical read earlier in its block matches it with no instruction between that may write, as MLIR's `CSE` merges an op whose only effect is a read (R929; `p.x + p.x` reads `p.x` once, a store between keeps both); a register is a value no reader writes, so a merged one's consuming readers copy it: the Pliron callee of an owned parameter of such a type copies the value its caller's pointer addresses into a slot of its own, as upstream passes it by value, and the VM's `UninitStorage` and `StoreToMem` copy their operand; a value of any other type is not merged, since it may own what a consuming reader takes, and the Pliron backend lowers blocks in reverse postorder so a reused definition precedes its reads. `lower-arg-conventions` stores each `read` or owned argument of a trivially register-passable type into a fresh local at the entry, as upstream's callee does, and `mem-2-reg` promotes it and the instance's own locals (with the pre-elaboration runs: corpus concrete instructions −9.1 %, generator-instance instructions −6.5 %, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`); a `mut` argument stays in its slot, as upstream's does. Operators over a parameter read the elaborator answered fold, an `if` they decide keeps its taken arm, and a copy whose type substitution made trivial is forwarded, including a dispatched `x.copy()` at a built-in receiver, which the elaborator writes as a value copy (R839), also through a `ref` binding such as a variadic collector's loop variable (R864), since an instance keeps its substituted generic register types and the concrete verifier rejects a reference slot read as its handle (R865) (corpus concrete instructions −0.96 %, generator-instance instructions −2.05 %, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`). The program is re-admitted as concrete MIR and its elaboration counts recounted after it; each pass reports whether it changed a body, and a release build re-verifies only the changed bodies (R844; `collections_many`'s `post_elaboration` span 31.6 → 16.6 ms). `eliminate-dead-symbols` runs over the concrete program straight out of the elaborator and again after the pipeline, as upstream runs `EliminateDeadSymbols` around its post-elaboration function pipeline, rooted at the entry roots, the requested entries' instances, and the compilation's exports, so the instances only a compile-time application reached (`paramfor_*` stubs, `range` iterator methods, `$comptime$` thunks, the collections a comptime display builds) leave the concrete program (R989; corpus concrete functions −11.5 %, concrete instructions −6.3 %, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`); a generator's counts are its instances that remain. Before that first run, `eliminate-duplicate-functions` keeps one of each set of identical functions (equal bodies, declarations, and source locations, each spelling its own name alike) and points the others' references at it, repeating while merged callees make callers identical, as upstream's `EliminateDuplicateFunctions` does (R990; `foo[1]` and `foo[2]` merge when a `comptime if` picks one arm for both; corpus concrete functions −26, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`); the elaborator erases the constant a call's compile-time argument was read into with the argument it drops, as upstream's call carries the parameter as an attribute, so `bar[1]` and `bar[2]` of `bar[n]` returning `foo[n]() + 10` merge once `foo[1]` and `foo[2]` do (R991). A `drop.var` or `drop.place` names the destructor it runs, as upstream's destructor insertion leaves a call naming the `__del__` at each death: drop elaboration names the template member its struct's member table records, the elaborator respells it to the instance it demands, `eliminate-dead-symbols` and `remove-unused-params` follow it as a call, and the VM and the native backend call it without looking it up from the type (R1016; no program's output or instance count changes). A cleanup list — a `try`'s raise edge or a `return.cleanup` — is a list of such drops too, each entry naming its slot's destructor (`CleanupDrop`, MIR text 1.72), named, respelled, followed, and verified as a `drop.var`'s is, as upstream's destructor insertion names the `__del__` in the unwind blocks, so the VM's `run_cleanup` and Pliron's raise and return edges call the named destructor and look none up from the slot's type (R1042; no program's output changes). A copying or moving `var.use` and a `value.copy` name the `__copyinit__` or `__moveinit__` they run the same way, from MIR lowering on: lowering, copy elision, and `inline-parametric` name the template member, the elaborator respells it to the instance, and the VM and the native backend call it; a copy or move of a type with no such member of its own stays a value copy (R1017; no program's output or instance count changes). An owned closure capture (`closure.make`), a variant payload store (`variant.make`, `variant.set`, `variant.replace`), and the copy a method or iterator result adapter makes of the referent a reference-returning `__next__` yields (`call.method`, `iter.try_next`) name their `__copyinit__` or `__moveinit__` the same way, and the native backend runs a named capture constructor instead of rejecting a user-observable one (R1045; MIR text 1.71). A field move still relocates bytes (R1047). A slice descriptor's bound read (`s.start`) names the `Optional` constructors it builds its result through, its nullary and positional one-argument instances, which the elaborator demands and the VM calls, so they live only while a body reading a bound does, not as long as `Optional` (R1030; no program's output changes); a keyword collector's `StringDict` constructor is still recorded in its struct's member table and kept with it (R1070). A struct's destructor is instantiated and kept only where a drop names it or a body tears down a value of the struct without naming the destructor: a `drop.var` or `drop.place` of a symbolic type, a cleanup list holding it, a pointer or uninitialized-storage destroy of it, a variant store over it, an owned closure capture of it, or a field of a struct one of those reaches; a consumed value's fields count, its own destructor does not (`mojito_mir::mir::destroyed_structs`, R985; corpus concrete functions −1.8 %, concrete instructions −1.7 %, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`). A program that still imports package bodies, or a body that drops a value of a parameter type, keeps every live struct's destructor. A struct's `__copyinit__` and `__moveinit__` are likewise instantiated and kept only where a copy or move names it or a body copies or moves a value of it without naming one: a `var.use copy` or `value.copy`, a `var.use move` of a symbolic type, an owned closure capture, a variant payload, a `Type(copy=value)` construction, a method or iterator result read through its reference, or a field of a struct one of those reaches (`mojito_mir::mir::relocated_structs`, R994; `String`'s leave `assets/ok/mut_owned_params.mojo`; corpus concrete functions 29,001 → 28,325, concrete instructions 392,176 → 387,437, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`). A read out of storage (a load, a field, an element, a variant payload) and a store copy nothing on either backend, as on the pin: the native backend copies only where the MIR names a copy, so reading a `String` field into a borrow keeps no `String.__copyinit__` (R1010; corpus concrete functions 28,205 → 27,318, concrete instructions 387,199 → 367,578, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`; no program's output changes). A body that copies or moves a value of a parameter type, or an imported program, keeps every live struct's copy and move constructors. A constructor is instantiated only where a call reaches it — a call naming it, a call naming the struct (`T()` at `T = String`), which the elaborator resolves by arity, or a slice descriptor's bound read, which builds its `Optional` — as upstream instantiates only what a call reaches, so an uncalled overload that would fail at the instance does not fail the program (R993; `assets/ok/mut_owned_params.mojo` keeps only `String`'s `StringLiteral` constructor; corpus concrete functions −27.6 %, concrete instructions −35.7 %, `benchmarks/instantiation.tsv`). The concrete runs keep a constructor or an overload only where something names it — a call, or a struct's member table, which records the constructors a slice descriptor's bound read and a keyword collector run with no call naming them — as upstream removes any symbol nothing references, while the pre-elaboration runs keep every constructor of a live struct and every member of a live function's overload family, through which the elaborator resolves a generic body's calls (`analysis::eliminate_dead_concrete_symbols`, R1008; corpus concrete functions 27,962 → 27,853, concrete instructions 386,064 → 384,462). It runs for `run`, `exec`, and the native backend; the pre-elaboration switch turns it off too. No program's output changes. |
| Backend | Pliron native compile and run: scalars, strings, aggregates, allocation, references, destruction, exceptions | Run | Behind the `backend-pliron` feature (LLVM 23.1), `mojito compile [FILE] --backend pliron --emit plir\|ll\|bc\|obj\|exe [--native-opt 0\|1]` compiles the call-graph closure of `main` from the cached post-drop `drop_elaborated_mir` artifact to Pliron's LLVM dialect and on to LLVM IR, bitcode, objects, and host executables. The lowered surface covers the complete checked scalar subset (Int/UInt/Float64/Bool, every scalar operator incl. `**`, true `/`, unsigned div/mod/shift, float floor-div/mod without a zero trap, and the saturating `Int`/`UInt`/`Float64`/`Bool` conversions; keyword arguments and constant defaults bind through the shared call-slot matcher, and an omitted argument whose default is a converting constructor (`arg: Optional[T] = None`, `x: Optional[Int] = 5`, `m: Meters = 3` over a hand-written `@implicit` constructor) runs that constructor over fresh storage at a call and at a subscript, as the VM's `bind_for_call` runs it; overflow is defined two's-complement wrapping on both backends), the Stage 5 narrow-scalar and literal-value surface — width-1 SIMD scalar aliases (`Int8`…`Int64`, `UInt8`…`UInt64`, `Float32`) stored at their lane width with VM-exact wrapping `+`/`-`/`*`, signedness-correct comparisons, width-1 construction and `cast[DType.x]()` (int rewrap; float conversion through the f64 view with `Float32` rounding; float→int truncation saturating at the 128-bit intermediate before wrapping), `Int`/`UInt`/`Float64`/`Bool` conversions over sized operands, and `Float32` computed and printed through its f64 view (widen, operate at f64, round — reusing `mjrt_fmt_f64`; no ABI change), plus `IntLiteral`/`FloatLiteral`/`StringLiteral` as register/variable/field types — literal materialization into scalars and sized lanes wraps VM-exactly (`Float32` correctly rounded from the exact rational), literal-typed storage is exact i64/f64 (an `IntLiteral` constant exceeding i64 rejects with `LiteralOutOfRange` rather than silently diverging from the VM's arbitrary precision; displaying a runtime `FloatLiteral` value rejects — the VM prints its exact rational), and `StringLiteral` storage is the borrowed 16-byte `MjStrDesc` descriptor (typed variables/parameters/returns/fields, copies, printing, and `String(x)` conversion) — plus the Stage 3 runtime surface: string-literal constant pools (private `mjstr_*` globals, compile-time literal `+`/`==`/`!=` folds), `print` composed byte-exactly through the bundled `_int_digits`/`_uint_digits` Mojo bodies, `mjrt_fmt_f64`, and `mjrt_write_stdout`, target-layout aggregates (struct and internal tuple storage by the shared layout engine, fieldwise and compiled-`__init__` construction — a variadic initializer collects even a single argument into its pack, so a one-element tuple `(7,)` and a variadic struct over one element construct natively — resolved method calls with `mut self`/`deinit self` receiver write-back, `clone_value` copies through compiled `__copyinit__`, ASAP drops through compiled `__deinit__` with declaration-order field recursion), heap allocation (`std.memory.unsafe_alloc` and unchecked pointer subscripts over the headered ABI-v2 allocator; `free`/`unsafe_free` lower to the size-less `mjrt_free`), the nominal `String` end-to-end (native literal/copy constructor bridges mirroring the VM's own literal bridge; `__deinit__` compiles from real MIR; `String(x)` stringify; owned register temporaries release invisibly after their last use, matching the VM's undropped register temps), and the complete Stage 4 exceptional/destruction surface: raising functions return ABI-v3 tagged `{tag, ok, err}` outcomes through a prepended outcome pointer with explicit CFG edges (no unwinding), `try`/`except`/`else`/`finally` flatten structurally (per-edge flag-guarded `Try.cleanup` drops exactly like the VM, single-instance finalbodies with pending-outcome dispatch where a finalbody's own raise/return/region exit wins and resolves the overridden outcome, `break`/`continue`/`return` crossing regions and finalbodies), region ops lower to LLVM blocks as upstream's `LowerControlFlow` lowers `hlcf` and `lit.try` (R883: `if`/`loop`/`match` results and region arguments are block arguments, aggregates through staging storage; a `break`/`continue` from a `try` nested in another `try`'s body to a loop inside that body runs natively, R905; owned register temporaries release on the region tree), handled errors bind/print/copy/drop as owned `MjError` values (deep copies; drops free the message invisibly — the built-in error has no user destructor), per-variable initialization flags make drops no-op-safe on already-emptied or never-initialized slots (the VM's load-bearing idempotency), references lower as verified place addresses (`mut`/`ref` parameters pass the caller's storage address and alias write-through, `MakeRef`/`ReadRef`/`WriteRef`/`StoreRef`, places through local reference bindings, reference-returning functions return one pointer; ownership is never re-run), the error-propagation path frees releasable locals' buffers without running user destructors (the VM abandons raising frames), and an error reaching the executable wrapper reports through `mjrt_unhandled_error` (stderr `unhandled error: <message>`, exit 69). The Stage 5 iterator protocol runs `for` loops natively over user iterators and the scalar ranges: monomorphization types each split iterator slot from its (retargeted) `__iter__` prepare chain and unrolls dynamic trait-dispatch normalization statically under the VM's budget, the bounded `__len__() > 0`/`__next__(mut self)` protocol advances the iterator variable's own storage in place (borrowed step-0 receivers alias the source slot, so borrowing iterators root at the loop frame), raising iterators run `__next__` over the tagged-outcome ABI with a statically typed StopIteration exhausted edge (the caught error frees; the element slot zeroes inert; elements with user destructors and raising reference-yielding `__next__` reject until the Collections slice), the `CopyIteratorReference` adapter lifecycle-copies through concrete reference returns, and `raise StopIteration()` lowers nullary error structs to owned `MjError`s spelling the VM's display byte-exactly. The Stage 5 pointer/uninit-storage and builtin surface also runs natively: the compiler-private `UnsafePointer` storage intrinsics (raw single-take moves, in-place element destructors at computed element offsets, `unsafe_offset` pointer `+` arithmetic, `UnsafePointer.alloc`/`alloc_aligned`/`unsafe_dangling` over the headered allocator), payload-only `__UninitStorage[T]` inline storage (raw writes/takes, in-place destroys, and the VM's leak-by-design no-op drop), the `len`/`abs`/`min`/`max`/`round`/`divmod`/`input` builtins and scalar `__floor__`/`__ceil__`/`__trunc__`/`__ceildiv__` intrinsics with VM-exact semantics (`round` is nearest-ties-to-even, `llvm.roundeven.f64` against the VM's `round_ties_even`, matching upstream) (nominal receivers monomorphize to their dunder methods, so `String.__add__` and friends run the compiled stdlib byte loops; mixed concrete Int/UInt `min`/`max` rejects — the VM compares those exactly), `input()` through the ABI-v4 `mjrt_read_line` (prompts flush before the read; natively EOF yields `""` and never blocks, where the checker and the VM raise `EOF` as upstream does — native `EOF` waits on the next runtime ABI bump; `run --backend pliron` inherits the CLI's stdin), direct specialized-`__init__` calls binding their destination as `out self`, and `None` constants as erased zero-sized registers, and a compile-time callable parameter bound to a closure that captures (`observe[scale](5)` where `scale` reads an enclosing `var`) becoming the instance's last runtime parameter, so the environment reaches the body and its call stays indirect (a `MethodCall` in that shape is unbuilt behind a checker gap, `docs/roadmap.md` R172); backend-side monomorphization rejects instance-identity collisions (two instantiations of one generic template share a declaration name) contextually until the Collections slice canonicalizes instance names. Checked traps call `mjrt_trap` (div/mod-by-zero, `**`-exponent, allocation, stdout, stdin, unhandled-error categories; exit `64 + category`); `run --backend pliron` executes the advertised subset natively and maps trap exits back to the VM diagnostic (re-rendering unhandled raises from the executable's stderr). Anything outside the advertised subset rejects with a contextual, source-located diagnostic. VM parity is pinned by the typed JIT scalar lane (`conformance/pliron-scalar.tsv`) and the native-parity exe gate (`conformance/pliron-parity.tsv`, now covering `assets/ownership_ok` too): stdout-byte equality at `O0`/`O1`, AddressSanitizer/LeakSanitizer-clean runs (handled-raise and `finally` paths included), raise category/stderr differentials, the ordered lifecycle-trace differential (`mjrt_trace` events under `CompileOptions::trace_lifecycle` against the VM's test-only event log), and `llvm-nm` symbol-surface inspection, all shrink-guarded. Design and divergence records: `docs/notes/pliron-stage5.md` (earlier stages: `pliron-stage1.md` … `pliron-stage4.md`). |
| Backend | Shared native target, layout, and runtime ABI | Run | The normative contract is [`docs/native-abi.md`](native-abi.md) (ABI version 7), owned in code by `src/native/` — checked build configuration (`Triple`/`CpuFeatures`/`OptLevel`/`EmitKind`, CLI `--target`), the layout engine (`LayoutCx`: declaration-order C-style aggregates, payload-only `__UninitStorage` storage, `u32`-tag Variant overlay, `MjStrDesc`/`MjString`/`MjError` string and error representations, pointer-sized origin-erased references), injective `mj_` mangling, and the runtime contract table (`rt_abi`) — plus the independently versioned `crates/mojito-runtime` C-ABI library (no dependency on the `mojito` crate or a native toolchain) (`mjrt_*`: version, tracked headered allocation with sized `dealloc` validation and size-less `free`, pointer-lifetime classification, stdout, the `input()` stdin line reader, VM-display formatting, aborts, traps, unhandled-error reporting, and test-lane lifecycle-event tracing). Rust-side (`tests/native_abi_test.rs`, default lane) and LLVM-side (`native_abi_cross_checks`, pliron lane: target-data agreement from the pinned data-layout string, declaration snapshots, clang pin, `llvm-nm` symbol inspection) checks are mechanical and target-only; no native ABI touches VM `Value`. |
| Backend | Elaboration to concrete MIR (`native::mono`) | Run | Before execution on the VM and before native capability checking, the feature-independent elaborator clones the entry-reachable concrete function/method/struct graph from verified post-drop MIR, shares VM dispatch selection, substitutes retained type/value facts, and rejects residual symbolic runtime state. The specialized program must pass `mir::verify`, or specialization refuses it with the verifier's findings. Canonical MIR is unchanged; the VM and the native backend both run the result, which the driver caches. The specialized program runs on the VM as the erased program does: it keeps the nominal `String`'s `__copyinit__` and `write_to`, which native lowering bridges and never reaches, and the VM displays through a `write_to` whose writer specialization bound to the builtin string writer (pinned for the seven compile benchmarks by `a1_focused_inputs_convert`). The VM builds a slice bound as the `Optional` its projection declares, `Optional$mono$TInt` in a specialized program, and hashes a string literal through the nominal `String.__hash__` instance minted for its hasher. A type argument spelled by name (`Set[Tuple[Int, String]]()`) leaves a binder the receiver already solved alone, so a `Tuple` keeps its element arguments across one instance and its fields and projections name one type. A static's pack element type joins its instance identity beside its parameter types, so `Pair[Int].first(3, 4)` and `Pair[String].first("x", "y")` over `*values: Self.T` are two instances (`assets/ok/template_method_generic_static_parameter_pack.mojo`); a static whose arguments carry none of the struct's parameter (an empty pack, or a body-only `List[Self.T]()`) is reached through the spelled instance's clone, which the call names (`assets/ok/template_method_generic_static_owner_unbound.mojo`). A callable binding is called directly only while one definition is its sole write: a binding reassigned, or handed to a `mut` parameter that assigns it, is called through its stored value, so the call follows the write (`assets/ok/mut_callable_parameter_write_back.mojo`). Elaboration is bounded only by its instance count, as upstream sets no depth limit: the 4096th distinct instance demanded stops it with "instantiation past the 4096-instance budget", so expanding polymorphic recursion (`depth(W[T](x.copy()), n - 1)`, where the pin runs out of memory) is rejected within 350 MB after under 2 s of debug-build elaboration, whose time grows about linearly with the nesting depth (`conformance/probes/mono_polymorphic_recursion.mojo`). A nested instance type holds the level below it once: a struct type's argument list is shared between clones and caches its hash and whether it is closed, so a walk over the type stops at a closed level, and an instance symbol whose argument spelling passes 96 characters names its arguments by digest (`W$mono$H…`). A concrete type's conformance to a trait is decided once. A program that genuinely needs more instances is rejected too. |
| Backend | Pliron Variant, vector SIMD, and move/drop residues | Run | The `__VariantStorage` primitive behind `std.utils.Variant` uses the normative tag/payload layout for construction, projection, owning replacement, extraction, placement factories, consuming handlers, and active-payload destruction; Variant equality, hashing, and formatting are the self-hosted struct's compiled Mojo bodies. Multi-lane SIMD computes as LLVM fixed vectors in SSA — construction and splats, elementwise arithmetic with masked shift counts and trap-free floor division, comparisons, mask `select`, casts (float-to-int converting the whole vector inside the exactly-convertible range, saturating at i128 lane by lane outside it), `to_bits`, `shuffle`, bounds-checked lane extraction, `llvm.vector.reduce.*` integer/bool/ordered-float reductions and `minnum`/`maxnum` float min/max — over the unchanged lane-aligned aggregate storage ABI (byte-per-lane Bool masks); a lane write is the lane read's mirror (a guarded `insertelement` into the loaded vector, stored back at the base), formatting stays lane-wise, and the release object code selects packed instructions (`simd_lowering_emits_vector_code`). A generic struct instance over a multi-lane vector (`Box[SIMD[DType.float32, 2]]`) and a `List` of vectors read and stored by subscript compile natively (`assets/ok/template_method_vector_instance.mojo`, `assets/ok/list_of_vectors_subscript.mojo`). User `__moveinit__` transfers run their compiled constructor, and compiled destructors own residual droppable-field teardown through their `deinit self` epilogue. A named destructor called on a field of a consumed `self` (`self.lease^.release()`) destroys the droppable fields that field's destructor leaves in place, exactly once (`assets/ok/explicit_destroy_consumed_field_droppable.mojo`). |
| Backend | Pliron Stage 6 optimization and distributable artifacts | Run | Profiles `--native-opt 0\|release` (alias `1`) map through the snapshot-pinned pipeline table (shared pliron mem2reg/DCE stage; `release` adds LLVM `default<O1>` via the pinned `opt`); the toolchain resolves clang/`opt` 23 to absolute paths and validates the runtime archive (provenance, sha256, embedded ABI version) before any frontend work, reported by `--print-toolchain`. Artifacts are failure-atomic and byte-reproducible across clean builds at both profiles; `--native-debug lines` (default) attaches DWARF 5 subprograms and call-granular line locations (gdb backtraces name exact file:line at `O0`; zero correlation degradations corpus-wide); `--emit obj` writes a `<obj>.link.tsv` manifest that the `mojito link` verb validates before issuing the same deterministic link line; runtime discovery follows `--runtime-lib` → `MOJITO_RUNTIME_LIB` → installation bundle → dev tree; `scripts/package-pliron` produces a relocatable, self-contained bundle (stdlib included) with a deterministic tar; `benchmarks/native/` + `tools/bench` + `scripts/bench-pliron` own measurement under `benchmarks/native/noise-policy.md`. Policy record: `docs/notes/pliron-stage6.md`; the pinned-runner baseline, upgrade rehearsal, and promotion evidence are recorded. |
| Backend | Cranelift | No | Cranelift remains a feasible alternate below verified MIR but is not currently implemented. `--backend=llvm\|mlir\|cranelift\|ebpf` names are recognized seams that refuse construction. |
| Later backend | C/C++ source, LLVM, MLIR, eBPF | No | A C or C++ source backend is a possible additional target; eBPF is not prioritized. Whether MLIR (or any backend IR) becomes an internal compiler layer is a staged question (`docs/pliron-backend-pivot-plan.md`), not a standing exclusion. |
| Stretch goal | GPU | No | Intended eventually, not on the immediate horizon. |
| Expected later | primitive concurrency | No | The smallest concurrency primitives the language needs, on no schedule yet; parallelism and tasks are not included. |
| Out of scope | parallelism, distributed execution, Python interop | No | Outside the current Mojito parity target, and not intended. |

For planned semantic work, see [`roadmap.md`](roadmap.md). For exact VM
operations, see [`vm-instruction-set.md`](vm-instruction-set.md).
